**Why do companies create their own custom AMI ?** 

+ `Standardization`: Custom AMIs allow companies to create a standardized environment for their applications, ensuring consistency across all instances launched from the AMI.

+ `Faster Deployment`: Launching an instance from a custom AMI is faster than setting up a new instance from scratch, as the AMI already contains the necessary software and configurations.

+ `Cost Efficiency`: By creating a custom AMI, companies can optimize the software and configurations to reduce costs and improve performance.

+ `Security`: Custom AMIs can be configured with specific security settings and software, helping to enhance the security posture of the instances launched from the AMI.

+ `Compliance`: Custom AMIs can be designed to meet specific compliance requirements, ensuring that instances launched from the AMI adhere to necessary regulations and standards.


`What are some of the best practices for AMI` 

+ Regularly update and patch your custom AMIs to ensure they include the latest security updates and software versions.
+ Use descriptive names and tags for your AMIs to make it easier to identify their purpose and contents.
+ Limit access to your custom AMIs to only those who need it, using AWS Identity and
Access Management (IAM) policies.
+ Test your custom AMIs before using them in production to ensure they work as expected and do
not contain any vulnerabilities or misconfigurations.
+ Use versioning for your custom AMIs to keep track of changes and allow for easy rollback (e.g parameterstore)
+ Automate → Use EC2 Image Builder or CI/CD pipelines to refresh AMIs monthly/quarterly.
+ Secure them → Harden OS, remove unused packages, include security agents.


**How to copy an AMI from ONE region in aws to another** 

+ Go to the EC2 dashboard and click on "AMIs" in the left-hand menu.
+ Locate the AMI you want to copy and select it.
+ Click on the "Actions" button and select "Copy AMI" from the dropdown menu
+ In the "Copy AMI" dialog box, select the destination region where you want to copy the AMI.
+ Provide a name and description for the copied AMI, and click on the "Copy AMI" button to start the copying process.
+ Once the copying process is complete, you can go to the destination region and find the copied AMI in the "AMIs" section of the EC2 dashboard.


**What are some of the use cases of An AMI ?**  

+ `Backup and Disaster Recovery`: AMIs can be used to quickly recover from failures by launching new instances from a pre-configured AMI.
+ `Scaling`: AMIs can be used to quickly scale up your infrastructure by launching multiple instances from the same AMI using an auto scaling group in aws 
+ `Testing and Development`: AMIs can be used to create consistent environments for testing and development, allowing developers to quickly spin up instances with the necessary software and configurations.
+ `Custom Software`: AMIs can be used to create instances with custom software and configurations that
are not available in standard AWS-provided AMIs.
+ `Multi-Region Deployments`: AMIs can be copied across regions, allowing you to
deploy applications in multiple regions with the same configuration.
+ `Data migration` moving an ami from one region to another with all the data inside 


**KEY PAIRS** 

`What is a Keypair` : 

A key pair is a set of security credentials that are used to securely connect to an EC2 instance. It consists of a `public key and a private key`. The public key is stored on the EC2 instance, while the private key is kept by the user. When you launch an EC2 instance, you can specify a key pair to associate with the instance, allowing you to securely connect to it using SSH (for Linux instances) or RDP (for Windows instances). Key pairs are essential for ensuring secure access to your EC2 instances and should be managed carefully to prevent `unauthorized access.` 

`Note`; You can create an ec2 instance without a keypair . But you wont be able to login via ssh 

`How Key Pairs Work`
+ When you create a key pair, AWS generates a public key and a private key.
+ The public key is stored on the EC2 instance, while you download and securely store the
private key on your local machine.
+ To connect to the EC2 instance, you use the private key with an SSH client (
Linux) or RDP client (Windows) to authenticate your access.
+ The EC2 instance verifies the private key against the stored public key to grant access.
+ You can select an existing keypair if you have one already (or create) a Key new Pair.
+ You can use a single key pair on multiple ec2 instances 
+ You download the private key file (.pem) to your local machine. ( downloads folder )

`Note :`

+ Keep your private key secure and do not share it with anyone. If someone gains access to your private key, they can potentially access your EC2 instances. 
+ If you lose your private key, you will not be able to access your EC2 instances through ssh , and you will need to create a new key pair and update your instances to use the new public key.
+ An ec2 instance without a keypair you wont be able to SSH into the server but you can still login to the server using different methods
+ When naming a keypair use a single name , do not put spaces in the naming convention
+ When you create a keypair for the first time it goes to your downloads but you like you can move it from there and place in any folder of your choice 
+ If this is the first time you are using the keypair you will need to update the permissions and you need to be in the location where the key is. `chmod 400 <keypair-name>`
+ To login into an EC2 instance through ssh, security group inbound rule port 22 must be open . The common troubleshooting error you will see when its connectivity problems or issues like ports `Operation timed out`: Security group or NACL
+ If you are using windows and want to connect to a linux instance you can use `putty` as an ssh client but putty does not support .pem file so you will need to convert the .pem file to .ppk file using puttygen

**Use Cases of Key Pairs**

+ `Secure Access`: Key pairs provide a secure method for accessing EC2 instances, ensuring that only authorized users can connect to the instances.
+ `Automation`: Key pairs can be used in automation scripts and tools to securely connect to EC2
instances for tasks such as configuration management, deployment, and monitoring.
+ `Multi-User Access`: Key pairs allow multiple users to securely access the same EC2
instance by sharing the public key while keeping their private keys secure.
+ `Compliance`: Key pairs help meet security and compliance requirements by providing a secure authentication method for accessing EC2 instances.
+ `Remote Management`: Key pairs enable remote management of EC2 instances, allowing administrators to perform
maintenance, updates, and troubleshooting tasks without needing physical access to the server.


**What are the methods of logging into an EC2 instance**

+ `SSH (Secure Shell)`: For Linux instances, you can use an SSH client (like OpenSSH or PuTTY) to connect to the instance using the private key from your key pair. 

+ `RDP (Remote Desktop Protocol)`: For Windows instances, you can use an RDP client to connect to the instance using the private key from your key pair to retrieve the administrator password.

+ `EC2 Instance Connect`: This is a browser-based SSH connection method provided by AWS for Linux instances, allowing you to connect to your instance without needing a separate SSH client or key pair.

+ `Session Manager`: This is a feature of AWS Systems Manager that allows you to securely connect to your EC2 instances without needing SSH or RDP. It uses the AWS Management Console or AWS CLI to establish a secure session with the instance.

+ `EC2 Serial Console` : 
This is a feature that provides access to the serial port of your EC2 instances, allowing you to troubleshoot and recover instances that are not responding to SSH or RDP connections. It can be accessed through the AWS Management Console or AWS CLI.


**How To Login into an EC2 using Session Manager** 

+ Ensure that the EC2 instance has the SSM agent installed and running.
+ Attach an IAM role to the EC2 instance with the necessary permissions for Systems Manager (e.g., `AmazonSSMManagedInstanceCore`).
+ Go to the AWS Systems Manager console and navigate to "Session Manager."
+ Click on "Start session" and select the EC2 instance you want to connect to.
+ Click on "Start session" to establish a secure connection to the instance through the browser-based
terminal provided by Session Manager. You can now run commands and manage your EC2 instance without needing SSH or RDP access.


**Most commonly used approach to login into EC2 instances by companies ?**

+ For Linux instances, SSH is the most commonly used method for logging in, as it provides secure and direct access to the instance's command line.

+ For Windows instances, RDP is the most commonly used method for logging in, as it provides a graphical interface for managing the instance.

+ However, with the increasing emphasis on security and automation, many companies are also adopting `Session Manager` for secure and convenient access to their EC2 instances without the need for managing SSH keys or RDP credentials.



***HOW DO YOU GRANT ACCESS TO A USER WHO DONT HAVE A PRIVATE KEY***

Note : We stated that do not share private keys . What happens when you created an ec2 instance e.g Linux operating system and your colleage at work wants to login but you are the one with the private key . what will you do to grant them access ? 

+ Login into the EC2 instance as the administrator 
+ Create a new user account for your colleague using the `adduser` command (e.g., `sudo adduser colleague_username`).

+  Add the new user to the `sudo` group if they need administrative privileges (e.g., `sudo usermod -aG sudo colleague_username`).

`Next step` 

+ Ask your colleague to generate an ssh key on their local computer using the following commands : `ssh-keygen -t rsa -b 2048 -f calson@team4techsolutions.com` or `ssh-keygen` 

+ This will generate a public key (colleague_key.pem.pub) and a private key (colleague_key.pem).

+ When your colleage generates the key it comes with two keys . public and private and they can find it in their .ssh directory by running the command 
`cd .ssh` 

+ Your colleague should share the public key (colleague_key.pem.pub) with you securely (e.g., via email or a secure file-sharing service) or slack or teams . 


+ Once you receive the public key, log in to the EC2 instance and switch to the new user account using `su - colleague_username`.

+ Create a `.ssh` directory in the new user's home directory if it doesn't already exist ( e.g., `mkdir -p /home/colleague_username/.ssh`).
+ Set the appropriate permissions for the `.ssh` directory (e.g., `chmod 700 .ssh `    
/home/colleague_username/.ssh`).
+ Create an `authorized_keys` file in the `.ssh` directory and add the colleague's
public key to it (e.g., `echo "colleague_public_key_content" >> /home/colleague_username/.ssh/authorized_keys`).
+ Set the appropriate permissions for the `authorized_keys` file (e.g., `chmod
600`  /home/colleague_username/.ssh/authorized_keys`).
+ Now your colleague can use their private key (colleague_key.pem) to securely connect to

Last step 

+ Give your collegue the username you created on the instance .e.g joel 
make sure the spelling matches the user you created on the ec2 instance 
+ Give your colleague the host ip address so they can use to login ( public or private ip ) if instance is in public subnet 



**HOW TO LOGIN TO WINDOWS OPERATING SYSTEM USING RDP** :
+ Launch a Windows EC2 instance and ensure it has a key pair associated with it.
+ Once the instance is running, select it in the EC2 console and click on "Connect
+ In the "Connect to instance" dialog, select the "RDP client" tab.
+ Click on the "Get Password" button and provide the private key file (.pem) associated with the instance to decrypt the administrator password.
+ Download the RDP file provided in the dialog and
open it with an RDP client (like Remote Desktop Connection on Windows).
+ When prompted, enter the decrypted administrator password to log in to the Windows EC2 instance.


**SECURITY GROUPS** 

`What is a Security Group `:

A security group is a virtual firewall that controls inbound (incoming) and outbound (out-going) traffic to and from EC2 instances. It acts as a set of rules that determine which traffic (network) is allowed to reach the instance and which traffic is allowed to leave the instance. Security groups are associated with EC2 instances, and you can have multiple security groups for different instances or even the same instance. They are essential for securing your EC2 instances by controlling access based on IP addresses, ports, and protocols.

+ It helps protect what gets in and what gets out . Its security at the instance level
+ By default when you create an ec2-instance it comes with a default security group
+ You need port 22 inbound rule open on your security group before user can ssh into your ec2 instance
+ You can have multiple security groups attached to a single EC2 instance, and each security group can have its own set of rules.
+ A security must be attached to a VPC (virtual private cloud)
+ When you configure a source like 0.0.0.0/0 : it means you are allowing access from anyone and anywhere .`This is not recommended and not a good security practise`
+ apart from using the source as ip address cidr like (0.0.0.0.0/0 or 123.495.697/32) you can also configure the source as a security group id sgr-08adaec2ddb600c5f
+ Security groups are stateful in nature If it allows inbound request or traffic into your ec2 instance , it automatically allow outbound request without setting any outbond rules .
+ When you create a security group by default it denies all inbound traffic and allow all outbound traffic .

**Key Takeways** 
+ Virtual firewall - controls network traffic in and out of your ec2 instances
+ Security groups are stateful this means if a security allows inbound traffic it will automatically allow outbound traffic
+ It has inbound rules and outbound rules : inbound rules define which traffic can reach the instance , while outbound rule defines what traffic can leave the ec2 instance
+ You can have multiple security groups attached to a single EC2 instance, and each security group can have its own set of rules.


**USE CASE OF SECURITY GROUPS** 

+ `Web Server`: For a web server, you would typically allow inbound traffic on port 80 (HTTP) and port 443 (HTTPS) from any source 
+ `SSH Access`: For a Linux server that requires SSH access, you would allow inbound traffic on port 22 from specific IP addresses (e.g., your office IP or home IP) to enhance security.
+ `Database Server`: For a database server, you would allow inbound traffic on the database port
(e.g., port 3306 for MySQL) only from the IP addresses of your application servers or other trusted sources.
+ `Internal Communication`: For instances that need to communicate with each other within a VPC,
you would allow inbound traffic on the necessary ports (e.g., port 8080 for application communication) from the security group associated with the other instances.
+ `Remote Desktop Access`: For a Windows server that requires RDP access, you would allow
inbound traffic on port 3389 from specific IP addresses to ensure secure access.
+ `Custom Applications`: For custom applications, you would allow inbound traffic on the specific ports used by your application (e.g., port 5000 for a Flask application) from trusted sources or security groups.

**Best Practices for Security Groups**

+ Follow the principle of least privilege by only allowing necessary traffic and blocking all other traffic.
+ Regularly review and update your security group rules to ensure they align with your current security requirements.
+ Use specific IP addresses or CIDR blocks instead of allowing traffic from all sources (0.0.0.0/0)
+ Use security group references to allow traffic between instances within the same VPC instead of using IP addresses.
+ Monitor security group usage and changes using AWS CloudTrail and AWS Config to detect any unauthorized modifications
+ Use descriptive names and tags for your security groups to easily identify their purpose and associated instances.


**Lets have a closer look at Inbound security group rules** 

+ `Security Group rule ID e.g sgr-0480d4464a009b91f :` 

This is a unique identifier for the specific rule within the security group. It helps you manage and reference individual rules when you need to modify or delete them.

+ Security group `Type` :

This specifies the type of traffic that the rule applies to. Common types include SSH, HTTP, HTTPS, RDP, and Custom TCP/UDP. The type determines the default port and protocol settings for the rule.

+ `Protocol` : 

This specifies the network protocol that the rule applies to, such as TCP, UDP, or ICMP. The protocol determines how the traffic is handled and what ports are relevant for the rule.

+ `Port range` :
This specifies the range of ports that the rule applies to. For example, port 22 for SSH, port 80 for HTTPS 443 , or a custom range like 5000-6000 for specific applications. 

+ `Source`: This is where the traffic is coming coming from . It can be an ip address cidr , or security group id 

+ `Description - optional`:

This is an optional field where you can provide a description for the rule to help you remember its purpose or any specific details about it. It can be useful for documentation and management purposes, especially when you have multiple rules in a security group and you manage it with a team 


Questions 

`Why should we set outbound rules if by default security groups are stateful` meaning what is allow inbound automatically is allow for outbount  ?:

+ While security groups are stateful and allow return traffic for allowed inbound connections, setting outbound rules can still be important for controlling and restricting outgoing traffic from your EC2 instances. By default, all outbound traffic is allowed, which may not be desirable in certain scenarios where you want to limit the communication from your instances to specific destinations or services. Setting outbound rules allows you to enforce stricter security policies and prevent unauthorized data exfiltration or communication with untrusted sources.


**EBS VOLUMES : Elastic Block Store**

`What is an EBS Volume ?`:

An Elastic Block Store (EBS) volume is a `persistent block storage device` that can be attached to an EC2 instance. It provides durable and high-performance storage for your EC2 instances, allowing you to `store applications data, and operating systems data . EBS volumes are designed to be highly available and can be easily attached, detached, and resized as needed. They are commonly used for storing data that requires low-latency access and high throughput, such as databases, file systems, and application data.

An example of an ebs volume when compare to your local computer is ? 

+ EBS Volume = Hard Drive

What is a persistent Block storage or volume ?: 

+ Persistent storage means that the data stored on the EBS volume will persist(preserved) even after the EC2 instance is stopped or terminated. This allows you to retain your data and applications without losing them when the instance is not running.

`Features of an EBS volume`

+ `Persistent Storage` : EBS volumes provide persistent storage, meaning that the data stored on the volume will persist (stay) even if the EC2 instance is stopped or terminated. This allows you to retain(get) your data and applications without losing them when the instance is not running. 

+ `High Performance` : EBS volumes are designed to provide high performance with low latency and high throughput. They are suitable for applications that require fast access to data, such as databases and file systems.

+ `Scalability` : EBS volumes can be easily resized to accommodate changing storage needs. You can increase the size of an EBS volume without downtime, allowing you to scale your storage as your application grows.

+ `Durability` : EBS volumes are designed for durability, with data replicated across multiple availability zones (AZs) to protect against hardware failures. This ensures that your data is safe and available even in the event of a failure.

+ `Flexibility` : EBS volumes can be attached to and detached from EC2 instances as needed. This allows you to easily manage your storage and move volumes between instances if necessary.

+ `Backup and disaster recovery through Snapshots` : EBS volumes can be backed up using snapshots, which are point-in-time copies of the volume. Snapshots can be used for backup, recovery, and creating new volumes from existing data.


**Different Types of EBS Volumes**

`Note` : Voume refers to storage 

These are focused on performance , purpose and pricing 

+ `General Purpose SSD (gp2 and gp3)`: These volumes are suitable for a wide range of workloads, including boot volumes, small to medium-sized databases, and development environments. They offer a balance of price and performance. 
Cost effective and has low latency

+ `Provisioned IOPS SSD (io1 and io2)`: These volumes are designed for I/O-intensive applications that require high performance, such as large databases and critical workloads. They provide consistent performance with provisioned IOPS (Input/Output Operations Per Second).

+ `Throughput Optimized HDD (st1)`: These volumes are optimized for streaming workloads that require high throughput, such as big data and log processing. They are cost-effective for large, sequential workloads.

what is Throughput : in the context of computer networking and storage , This is when data is succesfully transfered from one location to another and its measured in bits per second (bps ), Mbps (mega-bits per second) , gigabits per second.

High throughput - data is transfered from one location to another faster 
low throughput ----data is transfered from one location to another in a slow manner

+ `Cold HDD (sc1)`: These volumes are designed for infrequently accessed data that requires low cost storage, such as archival data and backup. They are the lowest cost option for EBS volumes.This is good for scenarios where price is more of a concern than perfomance . e.g its good to save backup files or files saves in ec2 .

+ `Magnetic (standard)`: These volumes are the previous generation of EBS volumes and are not recommended for new applications. They are suitable for workloads that require low cost storage and do not require high performance, such as infrequently accessed data and archival storage.

`Note` : Any ec2 instance type that you select comes with a default EBS volume ( root volume)


Take note when it comes storage 

+ Frequently accessed data are costly 
+ Infrequently accessed data are cost effective 


**HOW CAN WE SCALE AN EBS VOLUME ?**

There are two ways we can scale in aws 

+ `Vertical Scaling` : This involves increasing the size of an existing EBS volume. You can do this by modifying the volume and specifying a larger size. The process is seamless and does not require downtime, allowing you to scale your storage without interrupting your applications.

+ `Horizontal Scaling` : This involves adding additional EBS volumes and attaching them to your EC2 instance. This allows you to distribute your data across multiple volumes, increasing your overall storage capacity and performance. You can use techniques like RAID (Redundant Array of Independent Disks) to combine multiple volumes into a single logical volume for improved performance and redundancy.

**How to Scale an EBS Volume (Vertical Scaling)**
+ Go to the EC2 dashboard and click on "Volumes" in the left-hand menu.
+ Locate the EBS volume you want to scale and select it.
+ Click on the "Actions" button and select "Modify Volume" from the dropdown menu.
+ In the "Modify Volume" dialog box, specify the new size for the volume and click
on the "Modify" button to start the scaling process.
+ Once the modification process is complete, you may need to extend the file system on the volume
to utilize the new space. This can be done using the appropriate commands for your operating system (e.g., `resize2fs` for Linux or Disk Management for Windows). For linux run the following commands :

`lsblk` : to list the block devices and identify the volume you modified ( e.g /dev/xvdf)

`sudo resize2fs /dev/nvme0n1p1` : to grow the partition on the volume ( e.g xvdf1)

`df -hT` : verify using this command 






**How to Scale an EBS Volume (Horizontal Scaling)**

+ Go to the EC2 dashboard and click on "Volumes" in the left-hand menu.
+ Click on the "Create Volume" button to create a new EBS volume with the desired
size and configuration.
+ Once the new volume is created, select it and click on the "Actions" button,
then select "Attach Volume" from the dropdown menu.
+ In the "Attach Volume" dialog box, select the EC2 instance you want to attach
the volume to and specify the device name (e.g., /dev/sdf) before clicking on the "Attach" button.
+ After the volume is attached, you may need to configure it on the EC2 instance (
e.g., partitioning, formatting, and mounting) to start using the additional storage capacity


**EBS SNAPHOTS**

All ec2 instances comes with a root volume ( default ebs volume)

`What is a root voume ? `:

`Root Volume` : A root volume is the primary EBS volume that contains the `operating system and boot files` for an EC2 instance. It is the volume from which the instance boots and runs its operating system. The root volume is typically created from an Amazon Machine Image (AMI) when you launch an EC2 instance, and it serves as the foundation for the instance's file system and applications.

`Data Volume`; A data volume is an additional EBS volume that can be attached to an EC2 instance to provide extra storage capacity for applications, databases, or other data. Unlike the root volume, a data volume does not contain the operating system and is used solely for storing `application data or files`. Data volumes can be created and attached to an EC2 instance as needed, allowing you to scale your storage independently of the root volume.

`What is data` ? : 

Data refers to any information that is stored, processed, or transmitted by a computer system. It can take various forms, including text, numbers, images, audio, video, and more. In the context of computing and technology, data is often organized into structured formats (like databases) or unstructured formats (like files) and is used for various purposes such as analysis, decision-making, communication, and storage. Data can be generated by users, applications, sensors, and other sources and is a fundamental component of modern computing systems.


**WHAT AN EBS SNAPHOT ?** 

What are EBS snapshots = This is a copy or replica of your EBS Volume .

An EBS snapshot is a point-in-time backup of an EBS volume. It captures the state of the volume at the moment the snapshot is taken, allowing you to create a backup of your data and applications. EBS snapshots are stored in Amazon S3 and can be used for various purposes, such as creating `new EBS volumes`,  `restoring data to an existing volume`, or `sharing snapshots with other AWS accounts`. 

Snapshots are incremental, meaning that only the changes made to the volume since the last snapshot are saved, which helps reduce storage costs and speeds up the snapshot creation process. 

By default when you create an aws account , ec2 ebs encryption is turned off . you will need to manually turn it on . What happen if you have already created EBS volumes with encryption not turn on ?

`What is encryption ?` : 

Encryption is the process of converting data into a coded format that can only be accessed or decrypted by someone with the appropriate decryption key. It is a security measure used to protect sensitive information from unauthorized access. In the context of EBS volumes, encryption helps ensure that the data stored on the volume is secure and cannot be read by anyone who does not have the necessary permissions or keys to decrypt it.


**What can be the use or use case of creating an EBS SNAPSHOTS ?**

+ `Backup and Recovery`: EBS snapshots can be used to create backups of your EBS volumes, allowing you to restore your data and applications in case of accidental deletion, corruption, or other issues.

+ `Creating New Volumes`: You can create new EBS volumes from existing snapshots, which is useful for scaling your storage or creating additional volumes with the same data and configuration as the original volume.

+ `Data Migration`: EBS snapshots can be used to migrate data between different regions or accounts by copying the snapshot to the desired location and creating a new volume from it.

+ `Testing and Development`: EBS snapshots can be used to create test environments or development instances with the same data and configuration as production volumes, allowing you to test changes or new features without affecting the live environment.

**Life CYCLE Manager**

`What is a life cycle manager ?`

A lifecycle manager is a tool or service that helps automate the management of resources throughout their lifecycle. In the context of AWS, it can refer to services like AWS Systems Manager Lifecycle Manager, which allows you to automate the creation, retention, and deletion of EBS snapshots based on defined policies. This helps ensure that your backups are regularly created and maintained without manual intervention, improving data protection and operational efficiency. OR 

This is feature that helps you to automate the creation ,Retention and deletion of EBS snapshots and AMI's Think of it as A schedule manager that makes sure backups (snapshots) happen regularly and old ones are deleted automatically.It Simplifies backup management and ensures compliance (no manual snapshot cleanup).

+ You can create a life cyle policy to automatically take snapshots on your behalf at specific time
+ You can create a custom policy and or use a aws default policy .
+ For custom policies you can create an `EBS SNAPSHOOT POLICY ,EBS AMI BACKED CUSTOM POLICY , CROSS ACCOUNT COPY EVENT POLICY`

1. `EBS SNAPSHOOT POLICY` :

This policy allows you to automate the creation and management of EBS snapshots. You can specify the schedule for taking snapshots, the retention period for keeping them, and any tags or filters to apply. This helps ensure that your EBS volumes are regularly backed up without manual intervention.

2. `EBS AMI BACKED CUSTOM POLICY`:

This policy allows you to automate the creation and management of Amazon Machine Images (AMIs) that are backed by EBS volumes. You can specify the schedule for creating AMIs, the retention period for keeping them, and any tags or filters to apply. This helps ensure that your EC2 instances are regularly backed up as AMIs without manual intervention or creation.

3. `CROSS ACCOUNT COPY EVENT POLICY`:

This policy allows you to automate the copying of EBS snapshots or AMIs across AWS accounts. You can specify the schedule for copying, the source and destination accounts, and any tags or filters to apply. This helps ensure that your backups are securely copied to another account for disaster recovery or compliance purposes without manual intervention.

`Note:` : The life cycle manager feature is free but you will incur charges for the snapshots that are being created by the life cycle policies since its data being stored


`SNAPSHOTS ARE INCEMENTAL WHAT DOES THAT MEAN ?`

+ Incremental snapshots mean that after the initial snapshot is taken, subsequent snapshots only capture the changes made to the EBS volume since the last snapshot. This helps reduce storage costs and speeds up the snapshot creation process, as only the modified data blocks are saved rather than creating a full copy of the entire volume each time.

**EBS SNAPSHOT RECYCLE BIN**

`What is EBS Snapshot Recycle Bin ?`: 

The EBS Snapshot Recycle Bin is a feature that allows you to recover deleted EBS snapshots within a specified retention period. When you delete an EBS snapshot, it is moved to the recycle bin instead of being permanently deleted immediately. This provides a safety net in case you accidentally delete a snapshot or need to recover it within the retention period. You can restore snapshots from the recycle bin back to their original state or permanently delete them if they are no longer needed. This feature helps prevent data loss and provides an additional layer of protection for your EBS snapshots.

`HOW TO CREATE A RECYLEBIN retention rule`
+ Go to the EC2 dashboard and click on "Snapshots" in the left-hand menu.
+ Click on the "Recycle Bin" tab at the top of the page.
+ Click on the "Create Rule" button to create a new recycle bin retention rule.
+ In the "Create Recycle Bin Rule" dialog box, specify the following details:
  Rule details (Retention rule name and Retention rule description - optional )
  Retention settings  : Resource type ( ebs snapshots, volumes or ami )
  Select the resources to retain ( retain all resources or resources with tag ) , 
  Retention : how long u want the snapshot to be kept in the recycle bin 





  **EFS : Elastic File System** 


`What is EFS ?`:

Elastic File System (EFS) is a `fully managed`, scalable, and elastic file storage service provided by AWS. It allows you to create and manage shared file systems that can be accessed by multiple EC2 instances simultaneously. EFS is designed to provide high performance and low latency for applications that require `shared access to files`, such as content management systems, web servers, and big data analytics. It `automatically scales storage capacity as needed`, so you only pay for what you use, and it provides durability and availability by replicating data across multiple availability zones (AZs). EFS is a great choice for applications that require shared file storage with high throughput and low latency.


`Its Fully Managed Service` . AWS handles ( Infrastructure management hardware , Operating system , networking ,it handles scaling (vertical or horizontal scaling), Patch Management , Backup & Recovery , Monitoring ) on our behalf 


**Features of ELASTIC FILE SYSTEM ( EFS )**

+ `Scale performance` : 

EFS can automatically scale its performance to meet the demands of your applications. It provides two performance modes: General Purpose and Max I/O. The General Purpose mode is suitable for most workloads and offers low latency, while the Max I/O mode is designed for applications that require high throughput and can tolerate higher latency.

+ `Highly Available:`

EFS is designed for high availability, with data replicated across multiple availability zones (AZs) within a region. This means that if one AZ experiences an outage, your file system will still be accessible from other AZs, ensuring that your applications remain available and resilient.

+ Its compactible ( can work) with Linux based Operating systems . It does not support windows based operating systems
+ `Cost Effective`: 
EFS is a cost-effective solution for shared file storage, as you only pay for the storage you use. There are no upfront costs or minimum fees, and you can scale your storage up or down as needed without worrying about provisioning or managing infrastructure.
+ `Secured`:
EFS provides multiple layers of security to protect your data. You can use AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) to control access to your file systems, and you can also enable encryption at rest and in transit to ensure that your data is secure both when it is stored and when it is being accessed.

NOTE : 
+ You can connect your EFS from aws cloud to on premise using VPN or Direct Connect (transit)
+ You can mount multiple EFS file system on a single ec2 and vice versa
1 EFS = 5 ec2 instances attached to it 
5 EC2 = 1 efs attached to them 
5 EFS = 1 ec2 instance 

+ You can create multiple mount point from a single EFS file system 

what is a mount point : 
A mount point is a directory in the file system where an EFS file system is attached or mounted. It serves as the access point for applications to read and write data to the EFS file system. When you create a mount point, you specify the EFS file system you want to mount and the directory path where it will be accessible on your EC2 instances. This allows multiple EC2 instances to access the same EFS file system through their respective mount points, enabling shared access to files and data across those instances.


**USE CASE OF EFS * What can prompt a you or a company to create an EFS ( Elastic File System)** 

+ `Content Management Systems (CMS)`: EFS is ideal for CMS applications that require shared access to files, such as images, videos, and documents. Multiple web servers can access the same EFS file system to serve content to users without the need for complex synchronization.

+ `Web Servers`: EFS can be used to store and serve web application data, allowing multiple web servers to access the same file system for consistent content delivery and easy management.

+ `Big Data Analytics`: EFS can provide scalable and high-performance storage for big data analytics workloads, allowing multiple instances to access and process large datasets concurrently.

+ `Shared Development Environments`: EFS can be used to create shared development environments where multiple developers can access the same file system for code sharing, collaboration, and testing.

+ `Media Processing`: EFS can be used for media processing applications that require shared access to media files, such as video encoding or image processing, allowing multiple instances to work on the same files without conflicts.

+ `Data Migration` : 

EFS can be used as a temporary storage solution during data migration projects, allowing you to easily transfer data between on-premises systems and AWS without the need for complex data transfer processes.

+ `Backups` :

EFS can be used to store backups of critical data and applications, providing a scalable and durable storage solution for backup and recovery purposes. Multiple instances can access the same EFS file system to create and manage backups without the need for separate storage solutions.


**EFS (ELASTIC FILE SYSTEM) STORAGE CLASSES** 

Most storages have what we call a storage class . A storage is focused how often you access the data (information). If you frequently access data then you need to store the data in a storage class that requires frequent access . 

EFS offers the following Storage Classes: 

+ `EFS Standard`: This storage class is designed for frequently accessed data and provides low latency and high throughput. It is suitable for applications that require consistent performance and low access times.

+ `EFS Infrequent Access (IA)`: This storage class is designed
for infrequently accessed data and provides lower storage costs compared to the standard class. It is suitable for applications that do not require immediate access to data and can tolerate slightly higher latency.

+ `EFS One Zone`: This storage class is designed for data that can be stored in a single availability zone (AZ) and provides lower storage costs compared to the standard class. It is suitable for applications that do not require high availability and can tolerate data loss in the event of an AZ failure.

+ `EFS One Zone Infrequent Access `: This storage class is designed for data that is infrequently accessed but requires high durability and availability. It provides lower storage costs compared to the standard class while still maintaining the same level of durability and availability.

**Lifecycle Management For EFS**

Lifecycle management simply means how data moves from one storage class to another using life cyle policies . 

+ You can create lifecycle policies to automatically transition files between storage classes based on their access patterns. For example, you can set a policy to transition files that have not been accessed for a certain period of time from the EFS Standard class to the EFS Infrequent Access class, helping to optimize storage costs while still ensuring that your data is available when needed. 

EFS has automatic lifecycle Management to move files from one storage class to another base on acces frequency or pattern.

 1. Transition into Infrequent Access (IA):

Files not accessed for 30 days can be moved to IA and this can save up to 92% of cost and then you can also transition files from Infrequent Access to Archive etc 

2. Transition into Archive. 90 days files or data without access will be transitioned into Archive Amazon Elastic File System (EFS) supports auto-transitioning files to Archive storage using Lifecycle Management policies, which can be set up to automatically move files to lower-cost storage tiers like EFS IA and EFS Archive after a specified period of inactivity

Standard ---->Infrequent Access ----> Archive

**Differences Between Elastic File System (EFS) and EBS volumes***

| Feature              | **Amazon EBS (Elastic Block Store)**                            | **Amazon EFS (Elastic File System)**                       |
|----------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------|
| **Storage Type**     | Block-level storage (like a virtual hard disk).                 | `File-level` storage using NFS protocol.                    |
| **Accessibility**    | Attached to **one EC2 instance** at a time (per AZ). Multi-Attach only for io1/io2 in same AZ. | Can be mounted by **multiple EC2 instances** across AZs.   |
| **Scalability**      | Manual scaling (up to 64 TiB). Must resize and expand FS.       | Automatically scales up/down with usage (petabyte scale).  |
| **Performance**      | Consistent, low-latency performance. Volume types: gp3, io1/io2, st1, sc1. | Throughput scales with size. Supports bursting & provisioned throughput. |
| **Backup & Recovery**| Point-in-time snapshots stored in S3.                          | Automated backups via AWS Backup. Multi-AZ replication.    |
| **Cost Model**       | Pay for **provisioned size** (even if not fully used).          | Pay for **actual storage used**. Higher cost per GB.       |
| **Best Use Cases**   | Databases, boot volumes, transactional apps.                    | Shared storage, CMS, big data, multi-instance workloads.   |



***Difference Between EFS and EBS volume**;

+ Ebs provides a `block level storage` vs EFS offers `file level storage` which allows multiple ec2 instances to access the same file simultaneouly 

+ Scalability : Ebs volumes needs to be scaled manually but with EFS you can scale manually and automatically .

+ Attachment and mounting; for ebs volumes you can attach to only one instance at a time . Multi Attach enabled ebs volumes (oi and o2 types) and with this you will only be able to attach in a single availability zone while EFS you can mount on multiple ec2 instances at the same and different availability zones. 

+ Backup & recovery. Ebs uses snapshots which can be used for point in time backup of volumes ,while EFS you can enable automated backups this is managed by AWS. 

+ Cost and provisioning .EBS requires users to provisioned (that is  which storage capacity you want) when you do this you have anticipated the storage you will need. On other hand EFS will scale in or out automatically if demand increases or decreases

2. Is it possible to have Data inside an EFS and our instances just read from the file system rather than creating the data inside the ec2 instances 


`Yes, it is possible to have data stored inside an EFS file system and allow your EC2 instances to read from it without creating the data directly on the instances. EFS provides shared file storage that can be accessed by multiple EC2 instances simultaneously. You can store your data in the EFS file system, and your EC2 instances can mount the EFS file system and read the data as needed. This allows for centralized storage and easy access to shared data across multiple instances without the need for duplicating the data on each instance.`

3. `Can we mount an EFS on an existing ec2 instance that is running ? :`

`Yes, you can mount an EFS file system on an existing EC2 instance that is running. To do this, you would need to follow these steps:

1. Ensure that the EC2 instance has the necessary permissions to access the EFS file system. This typically involves configuring the appropriate security group rules and IAM roles.

2. Install the NFS client on the EC2 instance if it is not already installed. This allows the instance to communicate with the EFS file system using the NFS protocol.

3. Create a mount point directory on the EC2 instance where you want to mount the EFS file system (e.g., /mnt/efs).

4. Use the `mount` command to mount the EFS file system to the created mount point. You would specify the EFS file system's DNS name and the mount point directory in the command.
For example:
```
sudo mount -t nfs4 -o nfsvers=4.1 fs-0123456789abcdef0.efs.region.amazonaws.com:/ /mnt/efs
```
5. After executing the mount command, the EFS file system will be accessible from the specified mount point on the EC2 instance, allowing you to read and write data to it as needed.

**AMAZON FSX**:

Fully managed third-party file systems optimized for a variety of workloads

`What is Amazon FSx ?`

Amazon FSx is a fully managed `file storage service` provided by AWS that offers high-performance file systems for a `variety of workloads`. It provides two types of file systems: `Amazon FSx for Windows File Server`, which is designed for Windows-based applications and workloads, and `Amazon FSx for Lustre`, which is optimized for high-performance computing (HPC) and machine learning workloads. Amazon FSx allows you to easily create and manage file systems that can be accessed by multiple EC2 instances simultaneously, providing scalable and high-performance storage solutions for your applications.

- FSx for Lustre

It provides a fully managed, highly available, and scalable file storage solution that supports the SMB protocol. It is ideal for use cases such as content management systems, web serving, and home directories.

**EC2 Instance  Advanced DetailsAdvanced Settings** 

+ `Domain Join directory` : 
This allows you to join your EC2 instance to an Active Directory (AD) domain, enabling centralized authentication and management of your instance using AD credentials.This is when you want your instance to have domain based authenticattion with a centralized authentication service like Active Directory  , SSO .

+ `IAM Instance Profile` : 
This allows you to assign an IAM role to your EC2 instance, granting it permissions to access AWS resources securely without the need for hardcoding credentials. This is useful for applications running on the instance that need to interact with other AWS services, such as S3, DynamoDB, or CloudWatch.

+ `Hostname type`:
This allows you to specify the hostname type for your EC2 instance, which can be either `Private DNS` or `Public DNS`. The hostname type determines how the instance's hostname is resolved within the network. Private DNS is used for instances that are only accessible within a VPC, while Public DNS is used for instances that need to be accessible over the internet.


+ `Instance auto-recovery`: 

This allows you to enable automatic recovery for your EC2 instance in the event of a system failure. When enabled, if the underlying hardware of the instance fails, AWS will automatically recover the instance on new hardware, minimizing downtime and ensuring that your applications remain available.

+ `Shutdown behavior` :

This allows you to specify the behavior of your EC2 instance when it is shut down. You can choose between "Stop" and "Terminate". If you select "Stop", the instance will be stopped and can be restarted later, while if you select "Terminate", the instance will be permanently deleted when it is shut down.

+ `Stop - Hibernate behavior`:

This allows you to specify the behavior of your EC2 instance when it is stopped. You can choose between "Stop" and "Hibernate". If you select "Stop", the instance will be stopped and can be restarted later, while if you select "Hibernate", the instance's state will be saved to disk, allowing it to resume from where it left off when restarted.

+ `Termination protection`: 

This allows you to enable termination protection for your EC2 instance, which prevents the instance from being accidentally terminated. When enabled, you will need to disable termination protection before you can terminate the instance, providing an extra layer of protection against accidental deletion.

+ `Stop protection`:

This allows you to enable stop protection for your EC2 instance, which prevents the instance from being accidentally stopped. When enabled, you will need to disable stop protection before you can stop the instance, providing an extra layer of protection against accidental stoppage.

+ `Detailed CloudWatch monitoring`:

This allows you to enable detailed monitoring for your EC2 instance, which provides additional metrics and insights into the performance and health of your instance. With detailed monitoring enabled, you can access more granular metrics in Amazon CloudWatch, such as CPU utilization, disk I/O, and network traffic, helping you to better understand and optimize the performance of your instance.

+ `Credit specification`:

This allows you to specify the credit option for your EC2 instance, which determines how CPU credits are earned and used. You can choose between "Standard" and "Unlimited". With the "Standard" option, your instance will earn CPU credits when it is idle and can use those credits when it needs to burst above its baseline performance. With the "Unlimited" option, your instance can burst above its baseline performance without earning CPU credits, but you will be charged for any additional CPU usage beyond the baseline.

+ `Placement Group`: 

This allows you to specify a placement group for your EC2 instance, which is a logical grouping of instances within a single availability zone. Placement groups can be used to optimize network performance and reduce latency between instances by placing them in close proximity to each other. There are three types of placement groups: Cluster, Spread, and Partition, each with its own use case and benefits for different workloads. 

Types of Placement Groups :

1. **Cluster Placement Group** : A cluster placement group is a logical grouping of instances within a single Availability Zone. Instances in this group are placed close together to provide low latency and high throughput networking.

*Use case* : This is good for applications that require high network performance 

`Advantages of a CLuster placement Group `:

+ Provides low latency and high throughput networking between instances.
+ its good  for applications that require high network performance, such as HPC, big data analytics, and
machine learning workloads.
+ It can support up to 16 instances in a single cluster placement group.

`Disadvantages of Cluster placement Group : `

+ Instances in a cluster placement group must be launched within the same Availability Zone, which can limit flexibility and availability.
+ If the underlying hardware fails, all instances in the cluster placement group may be affected, leading
to potential downtime for your applications.
+ It may not be suitable for applications that require high availability or fault tolerance, as all instances in the cluster placement group are placed on the same hardware.

2. **Spread Placement Group** : A spread placement group distributes instances across multiple underlying hardware racks within a single Availability Zone. This ensures that instances are placed on separate racks to minimize the risk of simultaneous failure.

`Use Case`: Suitable for applications that need to be highly available and resilient to hardware failures. It is often used for critical applications where you want to ensure that no two instances are on the same hardware rack.

`Advantages of Spread Placement Group`:

+ Provides high availability and fault tolerance by distributing instances across multiple hardware racks.
+ It can support up to 7 instances per Availability Zone, allowing for greater flexibility in instance placement.
+ It is ideal for applications that require high availability and resilience to hardware failures.

`Disadvantages of Spread Placement Group`:

+ It may not provide the same level of network performance as a cluster placement group, as instances are distributed across different racks.
+ It may not be suitable for applications that require low latency and high throughput networking between instances.
+ It may require more careful planning and management to ensure that instances are properly distributed across racks.
+ Limitation with the number of instance you can launch in a single AZ  you can have `7 instances per AZ` though the number can change depending the Region.
+ Availability Zone Restrictions: While spreading instances can enhance availability, it also means that your application components might be spread too thinly across AZs, potentially impacting performance due to inter-AZ latency.

3. **Partition Placement Group** : A partition placement group divides instances into logical partitions, where each partition is placed on `separate underlying hardware`(rack). This allows for high availability and fault tolerance while still providing some level of network performance.

+ You can select up to 7 partitions 

USE CASE : Ideal for large distributed and replicated applications that require high fault tolerance, such as Hadoop, Cassandra, or MongoDB clusters.

+ Intances per partitioned: AWS has No hard limit (besides the regional instance quota). You can launch hundreds or thousands of instances as long as they fit within your account limits.

`Advantages of Partition Placement Group`:

+ Provides high availability and fault tolerance by distributing instances across separate hardware racks.
+ It can support a large number of instances across multiple partitions, allowing for greater scalability and flexibility.
+ It is ideal for applications that require high availability and fault tolerance while still providing some level of network performance.

`Disadvantages of Partition Placement Group`:
+ It may not provide the same level of network performance as a cluster placement group, as instances are distributed across different racks.
+ It may require more careful planning and management to ensure that instances are properly distributed across partitions.
+ It may not be suitable for applications that require low latency and high throughput networking between instances.  
+ Limit on partition.There's a limit to the number of partitions you can have within a placement group, which can vary by region. This limit might restrict how you scale your application.
+ inter partition latency : While instances within the same partition benefit from low-latency connections, communication between instances in different partitions might experience higher latency, as they could be located in different physical areas within a data center


+  `EBS-optimized instance`: 

This allows you to specify whether your EC2 instance is optimized for Amazon EBS. EBS-optimized instances provide dedicated bandwidth for Amazon EBS I/O, which can improve the performance of your EBS volumes when attached to the instance. This is particularly beneficial for applications that require high I/O performance, such as databases or data-intensive workloads. When you select this option, your instance will be configured to provide optimal performance for EBS volumes, ensuring that your applications can access data stored on EBS with low latency and high throughput.

+ `Instance bandwidth configuration`:

This allows you to specify the network bandwidth for your EC2 instance. You can choose from different bandwidth options based on the instance type and size, which can range from low to high bandwidth configurations. This is important for applications that require high network performance, such as data transfer, streaming, or communication between instances. By selecting the appropriate bandwidth configuration, you can ensure that your EC2 instance has the necessary network capacity to meet the demands of your applications and workloads.

+ `Purchasing option` :

This allows you to specify the purchasing option for your EC2 instance, which can be either "On-Demand", "Reserved Instances", or "Spot Instances". On-Demand instances are billed at a fixed rate per hour with no long-term commitment, while Reserved Instances require a one-time payment for a specified term and provide a significant discount compared to On-Demand pricing. Spot Instances allow you to bid on unused EC2 capacity at a discounted price, but they can be interrupted by AWS with little notice if the capacity is needed for other purposes. Choosing the right purchasing option can help you optimize costs based on your application's usage patterns and requirements.

+ `Nested virtualization`:

This allows you to enable nested virtualization for your EC2 instance, which means that you can run virtual machines (VMs) inside your EC2 instance. This is useful for testing and development purposes, as it allows you to create and manage multiple VMs on a single EC2 instance without the need for additional physical hardware. With nested virtualization enabled, you can run hypervisors such as VMware, Hyper-V, or KVM within your EC2 instance, providing a flexible environment for testing and development of virtualized workloads.

+ `Metadata accessible`:

This allows you to specify whether the instance metadata service is accessible from your EC2 instance. The instance metadata service provides information about the instance, such as its instance ID, IP address, and security groups. By enabling or disabling access to the metadata service, you can control whether applications running on the instance can retrieve this information, which can be important for security and compliance purposes. If you disable access to the metadata service, applications will not be able to access instance metadata, which can help prevent unauthorized access to sensitive information about your instance.

`Metadata version`:

This allows you to specify the version of the instance metadata service that is accessible from your EC2 instance. The instance metadata service has two versions: Version 1 (IMDSv1) and Version 2 (IMDSv2). IMDSv2 provides enhanced security features, such as requiring session tokens for accessing metadata, which can help protect against certain types of attacks. By selecting the appropriate metadata version, you can ensure that your EC2 instance is using the level of security that best meets your requirements.

`Metadata response hop limit`:

This allows you to specify the maximum number of network hops that instance metadata requests can traverse before being blocked. This is a security feature that helps prevent unauthorized access to instance metadata from outside the instance. By setting a hop limit, you can control how far metadata requests can travel within your network, which can help mitigate the risk of certain types of attacks that attempt to access metadata from outside the instance. A lower hop limit can provide increased security by restricting access to metadata, while a higher hop limit may be necessary for certain applications that require access to metadata from other instances or services within your network.


`Allow tags in metadata`:

This allows you to specify whether tags associated with your EC2 instance are included in the instance metadata. Tags are key-value pairs that you can assign to your EC2 instances for organizational and management purposes. By enabling this option, you can access the tags of your instance through the metadata service, which can be useful for applications that need to retrieve tag information for configuration or automation purposes. If you disable this option, tags will not be included in the instance metadata, which can help enhance security by preventing unauthorized access to tag information.

+ `User data`:

This allows you to specify user data for your EC2 instance, which is a script or set of commands that can be executed when the instance is launched. User data can be used to automate the configuration and setup of your instance, such as installing software, configuring settings, or running initialization scripts. By providing user data, you can ensure that your EC2 instance is properly configured and ready to use immediately after launch, saving time and effort in manual setup processes. 

User data can include shell scripts, cloud-init directives, or other automation data that applies to your instance to configure software packages, install applications, manage files, or perform other bootstrapping tasks.

`Bootstrap` : (user data)

In the context of EC2 instances, bootstrapping refers to the process of automatically configuring and initializing an instance when it is launched. This can include tasks such as installing software, setting up configurations, or running scripts that prepare the instance for use. By providing user data during the launch of an EC2 instance, you can automate the bootstrapping process, ensuring that your instance is ready to perform its intended functions without requiring manual intervention after it starts. This is particularly useful for scaling applications or deploying instances in a consistent and repeatable manner.

**LOAD BALANCER**

`What is a Load Balancer ?`: 

A load balancer is a device or software that distributes incoming network traffic across multiple servers (ec2-instances) or resources to ensure optimal performance, reliability, and scalability of applications. It acts as a reverse proxy, receiving client requests(end users , customers ) and forwarding them to the appropriate backend servers (ec2-instances) based on various algorithms and health checks . Load balancers help prevent `server overload`, improve response times, and enhance the overall availability of applications by distributing the workload evenly across multiple servers. They can be used in various scenarios, such as web applications, databases, and microservices architectures, to ensure that resources are utilized efficiently and that users experience consistent performance. 

Summarise this by saying 

A a load balanceris a device or software that helps to distribute your network or application trafic across multiple devices (e.g ec2 instances , eks pods , load balancers , http and we refer to these devices as targets).The goal is to ensure no single server should be overwhelmed with too much traffic , when your server is not overwhelmed with too much traffic this improves your application perfomance and reliability.


**Benefits of A Load Balancer or Why do companies use a Load Balancer ?**

+ `Improved Performance`: Load balancers distribute incoming traffic across multiple servers, preventing any single server from becoming overwhelmed. This helps to maintain optimal performance and responsiveness of applications, even during high traffic periods. 

+ `High Availability`: Load balancers can detect server failures and automatically redirect traffic to healthy servers, ensuring that applications remain available and accessible to users. This helps to minimize downtime and improve the overall reliability of applications.

+ `Scalability`: Load balancers allow for easy scaling of applications by adding or removing servers from the pool of resources. This enables organizations to handle increased traffic and accommodate growth without disruption to users.

+ `Security`: Load balancers can provide an additional layer of security by acting as a barrier between clients and backend servers. They can help to protect against DDoS attacks, distribute SSL/TLS encryption, and enforce security policies.

+ `Flexibility`: Load balancers can support various types of applications and protocols, making them suitable for a wide range of use cases. They can be used for web applications, databases, microservices, and more.

+ `Cost Efficiency`: By distributing traffic across multiple servers, load balancers can help to optimize resource utilization and reduce the need for over-provisioning. This can lead to cost savings by allowing organizations to use their resources more efficiently.

+ `Centralized Management`: Load balancers provide a centralized point of control for managing traffic distribution and monitoring the health of backend servers. This allows administrators to easily configure and manage their applications from a single interface.

serverless applicatin architecture 


## HOW DOES A LOAD BALANCER DETERMINED WHEN AN EC2 INSTANCE (WEB SERVER) HAS LESS LOAD BEFORE IT ROUTES TRAFFIC TO IT .

A load balancer will monitor seAvers 

1. `A load balancer uses Perfomance Metrics`: 

Load balancers can monitor various performance metrics of backend servers , such as CPU utilization, memory usage, and response times. By analyzing these metrics, the load balancer can determine which servers are under less load and route traffic accordingly.

2. `Round Trip Time`:
Some load balancers measure the round trip time ,which means a longer round trip time indicates the servers might be experiecing more workload .

`What is round trip time ?:`

Round trip time (RTT) is the time it takes for a signal or data packet to travel from the source (client) to the destination (server) and back again. It is a measure of latency in a network connection and is typically expressed in milliseconds (ms). RTT includes the time taken for the data to reach the server, the time taken for the server to process the request, and the time taken for the response to return to the client. A lower RTT indicates a faster and more responsive connection, while a higher RTT may indicate network congestion or other issues that can affect performance.

3. `Health Checks`:
Load balancers perform regular health checks on backend servers to ensure they are functioning properly. If a server is deemed unhealthy or unresponsive, the load balancer will stop routing traffic to it until it becomes healthy again. It uses ping to check if the server is responding as expected . servers will respond with `HTTP 200 OK `

*Ping*: Sending a simple network ping to see if the instance is responsive.
*HTTP/HTTPS Request*: Making a request to a specific URL and checking for a healthy response (e.g., HTTP 200 OK).
*TCP Connection*: Attempting to establish a TCP connection to a specific port.


4. `Load Balancing Algorithms`  

Load balancers uses `Round-Robin algorithm` to distribute request equally regardless of the load. it uses both the `health check and perfomance metrics` to do this .

**There other alorithms** : 

These are features you can set on your application loadbalancer to control it on how it distributes traffic to your backend servers.

+ *Round Robin*: Distributes traffic evenly across all healthy instances in a rotating order. our aws application loadbalancers by default uses round robin algothrithms 

+ *Least Connections*: Directs traffic to the instance with the fewest active connections.

+ *Least Response Time*: Sends traffic to the instance with the quickest response time.

+ *Weighted Round Robin*: Assigns more traffic to instances with greater capacity or better performance, based on predefined weights.

+ *IP Hash*: Routes requests based on a hash of the client’s IP address, ensuring consistent routing for the same client.


`What is an algorithm ?`:

An algorithm is a step-by-step procedure or set of rules designed to perform a specific task or solve a particular problem. It is a well-defined sequence of instructions that takes an input, processes it, and produces an output. Algorithms can be implemented in various programming languages and are fundamental to computer science and software development, as they provide a systematic way to approach and solve problems efficiently.

5 `Timeout and Error Rates`: 

Load balancers can monitor the timeout and error rates of backend servers. If a server is experiencing high timeout rates or error rates, it may indicate that the server is under heavy load or facing issues, prompting the load balancer to route traffic to other healthier servers.


**DIFFERENT TYPES OF LOADBALANCERS ON AWS**

1. `Application Load Balancer (ALB) (Layer 7)`: This type of load balancer operates at the application layer (Layer 7) and is designed to handle `HTTP and HTTPS` traffic. It provides advanced routing capabilities based on content, allowing you to route traffic to different backend services based on URL paths, hostnames, or HTTP headers. 

What is 

`http`: 
HTTP (Hypertext Transfer Protocol) is a protocol used for transmitting data over the internet. It is the foundation of data communication for the World Wide Web and is used to transfer web pages, images, videos, and other resources between clients (such as web browsers) and servers.

`https`: 

HTTPS (Hypertext Transfer Protocol Secure) is an extension of HTTP that adds a layer of security by using SSL/TLS encryption. It ensures that the data transmitted between the client and server is encrypted and secure from eavesdropping or tampering. HTTPS is commonly used for secure communication on the web, such as online banking, e-commerce, and any website that handles sensitive information.

Smart routing based on: Path-based (/api/* → microservice A, /user/* → microservice B).
Host-based (shop.example.com → service A, blog.example.com → service B).
Header or query string values.

2. `Network Load Balancer (NLB) (Layer 4)`: This type of load balancer operates at the transport layer (Layer 4) and is designed to handle `TCP and UDP` traffic. It provides high performance and low latency, making it suitable for applications that require extreme performance, such as gaming or real-time streaming.

What is : - 

`TCP (Transmission Control Protocol)`: TCP is a connection-oriented protocol that provides reliable, ordered, and error-checked delivery of data between applications running on hosts communicating over an IP network. It establishes a connection before transmitting data and ensures that all packets are delivered correctly.

`UDP (User Datagram Protocol)`: UDP is a connectionless protocol that allows applications to send messages (datagrams) without establishing a connection. It is faster than TCP but does not guarantee delivery, order, or error checking, making it suitable for applications that can tolerate some loss of data, such as video streaming or online gaming.

3. `Classic Load Balancer (CLB) (Layer 4 and Layer 7)`: This is the original load balancer provided by AWS and can operate at both the transport layer (Layer 4) and the application layer (Layer 7). It is suitable for applications that require basic load balancing functionality without advanced routing capabilities.AWS 
(does not recommend the use . old version) (supports layer 4 & 7 ) : `Older generation load balancer`.
Supports both Layer 4 (TCP/SSL) and Layer 7 (HTTP/HTTPS), but limited features.

4. `Gateway Load Balancer (Layer 3)`:
This type of load balancer operates at the network layer (Layer 3) and is designed to handle `IP traffic`. It is used for deploying, scaling, and managing third-party virtual appliances, such as firewalls, intrusion detection systems, and deep packet inspection systems. It provides a transparent gateway for routing traffic to these appliances while maintaining high availability and scalability.



**What Does different Layers mean in Load Balancing e.g Layer 2 , Layer 3 , Layer 4 etc**

In the context of load balancing, different layers refer to the OSI (Open Systems Interconnection) model, which is a conceptual framework used to understand and implement network protocols in seven layers. Each layer has specific functions and responsibilities in the communication process.

- `Layer 1` : Physical Layer :

This layer is responsible for the physical connection between devices, including the transmission of raw bits over a communication channel. It deals with the hardware aspects of networking, such as cables, switches, and network interface cards.
Key Elements: Electrical signals, cabling, connectors, network interface cards (NICs).

Example: Ethernet cables, fiber optics, physical ports.

- `Layer 2` : Data Link Layer :

Function: Provides node-to-node data transfer and handles error detection and correction from the physical layer.
Key Elements: MAC addresses, switches, frames.
Example: Ethernet, Wi-Fi (802.11), MAC address.
This layer is responsible for the reliable transfer of data frames between devices on the same local network. It handles error detection and correction, as well as flow control. Load balancers that operate at Layer 2 can distribute traffic based on MAC addresses.

- `Layer 3` : Network Layer :
Function: Responsible for routing packets (data or information) across different networks and handling logical addressing (IP addresses).
Key Elements: IP addresses, routers, packets.
Example: IP (Internet Protocol), ICMP (Internet Control Message Protocol).
This layer is responsible for routing packets across different networks and handling logical addressing (IP addresses). Load balancers that operate at Layer 3 can distribute traffic based on IP addresses.

- `Layer 4` : Transport Layer :
Function: Provides end-to-end communication and manages data flow control, error handling, and connection management.
Key Elements: Ports, TCP/UDP protocols, segments/datagrams.
Example: TCP (Transmission Control Protocol), UDP (User Datagram Protocol).
This layer is responsible for providing end-to-end communication and managing data flow control, error handling, and connection management. Load balancers that operate at Layer 4 can distribute traffic based on TCP/UDP ports and protocols.

- `Layer 5` : Session Layer :
Function: Manages sessions or connections between applications. It establishes, maintains, and terminates connections.  
Key Elements: Sessions, dialogues, connections.
Example: NetBIOS, RPC (Remote Procedure Call).
This layer is responsible for managing sessions or connections between applications. It establishes, maintains, and terminates connections. Load balancers that operate at Layer 5 can manage sessions and maintain state information for client connections.

- `Layer 6` : Presentation Layer :
Function:
This layer is responsible for translating data between the application layer and the network. It handles data encryption, compression, and formatting to ensure that data is presented in a way that applications can understand.
Key Elements: Data translation, encryption, compression.
Example: SSL/TLS (Secure Sockets Layer/Transport Layer Security), JPEG, ASCII.

- `Layer 7` : Application Layer :

Function: Provides end-user services and interfaces directly with applications. It facilitates communication between software applications and lower layers.
Key Elements: Application protocols, interfaces.
Example: HTTP (Hypertext Transfer Protocol), FTP (File Transfer Protocol), SMTP (Simple Mail Transfer Protocol), DNS (Domain Name System).
Understanding Layer 7 (Application Layer)
Layer 7 is the top layer in the OSI model and is closest to the end-user. This layer interacts directly with software applications to provide communication functions. It's responsible for:


Protocol Implementation: Ensuring that network services are available to applications (e.g., web browsers, email clients).
Data Formatting: Converting data into a format that applications can understand.
User Interface: Providing network services directly to end-user applications.
In the context of load balancing, a Layer 7 Load Balancer (like AWS Application Load Balancer) can make routing decisions based on:
Content of the request (e.g., URL path, HTTP headers).

1. **Application Load Balancer:** 

+ This is good  loadbalancer to route traffic to your web applications 
+ Application load balancers are designed for `http and https traffic` or protocols 
+ It operates under layer 7 ( Operates at the OSI (Open system interconnection) Model Layer 7 )
+ Application loadbalancer supports features like `SSL/TSL terminations` ,websockets and HTTP/2 
+ Application loadbalancers are good for modern application archectures (micro service , monolithic applications)
+ Applications loadbalancers can be internet facing or internal facing . 
+ Load Balancers comes with security groups to enhance security 
+ Load balancers will reach instances through what we call a Target group . A target group  helps a load balancer to target specific resources base on your configurations . 


**Application Load Balancer with HTTPS** 

+ Request a certificate under Certificate Manager (ACM )
+ Validate the certificate with Route 53 ( Note this is for those who already have a domain )
+ Use the Certificate to create a load balancer 
+ Create a DNS name 



2. ## Network Load Balancer (Layer 4 )

+ Network load balancers are designed for TCP ,UDP and TLS traffic 
TCP: Transmission Control Protocol (IP network)
UDP: User Datagram Protocol 
TSL: Transport Layer Security 

+ Network load balancer operates at layer 4 which offers high perfomance & low latency 
+ Network load balancers often provide a static IP address or a set of IP addresses that remain constant, which can be beneficial for applications that require predictable IP addresses. 
+ Network load balancers offers high perfomance because it operates at a lower layer and handles fewer data processing tasks compared to application-layer load balancers, NLBs typically offer lower latency and higher throughput.
+ Network Loadbalancers performs health checks on targets to ensure that traffic is only sent to healthy instances. These health checks are typically based on TCP connectivity.
+ Network loadbalancers are good for `Streaming applications , VOIP ,Video Games` 


3. **Gateway Load Balancer (Layer 3)**

+ Its designed to work at layer 3 which is the network layer OSI model
+ It has a single entry point .Provides a single entry point for inbound traffic, simplifying network architecture and reducing the number of routing configurations.
+ It also supports healths check for the virtual instances to ensure traffic is sent to healthy instances 
+ Your target has a deployed Virtual Appliances ( EC2 Or a Third party service )
e.g Firewalls , Intrustion Detection , Prevention systems , Deep Packet inspections 
also u can Deploying 3rd-party firewalls (e.g., Palo Alto, Fortinet)


4. **Classic Load Balancer** - Old version ( Legacy)
+ It Supports layer 4 (TCP) & and Layer7 (http & https) but its considered legacy 
+ It has limited features when compared to ALB /NLB 
+ Its used in old environments but its not recommended for new deployments
