**S3**

`What is an S3`:

S3 stands for `simple storage service`.Its a cloud-based `object storage` service provided or offered by AWS. Multi-purpose object storage .Its not attached to any resource for example an EBS volume is a storage but its attached to an EC2 Instance . You can store data (Information) like files , images , backups , logs , videos etc from anywhere on the web ,



`S3 Service is known for followings` 

+ `S3 is Durable and Reliable` : AWS (SLA-Service Level Agreement) Guarantees 99.9999999999% (11 nines ) durability ,meaning data is replicated across multiple servers within a region to prevent any data loss 

+ `Its Highly Scalable` : It can store unlimited data , weather you want to store KB ,MB , Megabytes , Terrabytes , S3 can handle the storage efficiently 

+ `S3 is Secure` : It provides multiple security features such as encryption (both at rest and in transit) , access control policies and integration with AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) to manage permissions

+ `Accessible from anywhere` ; It allows you to store data and access data from anywhere as long as you have internet and right permissions.

+ `Cost effective` : With S3 you only pay for the storage you use and there are no upfront costs or minimum fees . You can also optimize costs by using different storage classes based on your access patterns and requirements

+ `S3 is integrated with other AWS services` : It can be easily integrated with other AWS services such as EC2, Lambda, CloudFront, and more to create powerful and scalable applications.


S3 - Simple storage service 

`Bucket ( S3 Bucket )`:

`What is an s3 bucket ? :`

An S3 bucket is a logical container within the Amazon S3 service that is used to store objects (files, videos, data etc.). Each bucket has a unique name and serves as a top-level namespace for storing and organizing your data in S3. Buckets are used to group related objects together and provide a way to manage access, permissions, and lifecycle policies for the stored data. When you upload an object to S3, you specify the bucket where it should be stored, and the object is then accessible through that bucket's namespace. Buckets can be configured with various settings such as versioning, logging, and access control to meet specific requirements for data storage and management.


+ **BUCKET** : A bucket is simply a container in amazon s3 for storing objects.It is the highest level structured in S3 (simple storage service) where data is stored and managed . Each S3 bucket has a unique characteristics and serves as a scalable container for files & data. 


**Charateristics of A Bucket** 


+ Each bucket name must be globally unique across AWS ( not just within your account) 
+ Bucket name must meet the following criterias  reference doc : https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/bucketnamingrules.html

`Bucket names must be between 3 (min) and 63 (max) characters long.`
`Bucket names can consist only of lowercase letters, numbers, dots (.), and hyphens (-).`
`Bucket names must begin and end with a letter or number.`
`Bucket names must not contain two adjacent periods.`
`Bucket names must not be formatted as an IP address (for example, 192.168.5.4).`
`Bucket names must not start with the prefix xn--.`
`Bucket names must not start with the prefix sthree-.`
`Bucket names must not start with the prefix sthree-configurator.`
`Bucket names must not start with the prefix amzn-s3-demo-.`
`Bucket names must not end with the suffix -s3alias. This suffix is reserved for access point alias names. For more information` 

+ Buckets are region specific . When creating an s3 bucket you must specify an aws region .The data in the buckets are going to be stored in the data centers in the specific region (AZ). This can be for various reasons for example to reduce the impact of latency , cost effective it avoids data moving from long distance which avoids data transfer cost , and compliance with data residency laws .

+ With an s3 bucket you can organise your files into folders within a bucket for better management , eventhough everything will still be in a flat structure (AWS does use a Hierachical file system)

+ Permissions and Policies control access to the bucket or buckets and their content . You can create a permission to permit someone to create , access and delete objects in a bucket

Note : Permissions can be granted at the bucket level (meaning you want the user to only have access to the bucket) and at the object level (you want the user to also have access to the object)


+ You can configure the bucket to serve for different purposes ( use case ), to host static websites , storage  , software distribution  etc 

+ Though buckets are region specific which means you must specify region when creating the bucket , All your buckets on your aws account can be view in any region regardless of which region the bucket belongs to . 


+ When a bucket is empty you are not charge. You are only charge based on the size of the contents in your bucket


+ When creating a bucket you will choose if you want a `General Purpose or a directory bucket` and you can copy existing configurations or settings from existing buckets to your new bucket 

+ ACL : Access control list : This gives permission to who have ownership of the objects in a bucket . By default its disabled which means the aws account owns all the objects in the bucket , but if you enable it ,it means another aws accounts can have ownership of the objects in the bucket 

+ By default S3  Blocks all Public Access settings for your bucket .This means the objects inside your bucket will not be publicly accessible . Even if you uncheck to turn public access (i.e you want public access to the objects in the bucket) aws s3 wont allow it without you adding a Bucket policy . AWS added this layer of security to avoid instances of misconfiguration or unintended settings where you turn on public access to your objects. 




**OBJECTS**  

`What is an object in S3 ?`

+ An object is anything stored in an s3 bucket e.g (files , images ,videos).Each object consist of a file itself and any associated meta-data(information).Meta data can contain or include information in the objects like size , last modified date , time , creation time and any other properties 
`example` : if you upload a photo called beach.jpg , the actual image is the object , and the meta-data might include details such as file type (JPEG) (PDF), date created , size (2 MB) , Last modified ETC 

+ Each object in your s3 bucket has an object url which enables you to access the object on the browser 
+ Data-Size in an s3 bucket can range from 0 bytes (empty file) to 5 terabytes (huge videos or files or datasets)
+ There is a unique identifier for each object in a bucket. Each object is stored in a bucket with a specific key that aws uses to retreive the object.
+ The objects that are stored in the bucket has a specific structure . The Key includes the full path to the object 

`example` : if you have a folder structure in your bucket like this  /images/photos/2024/beach.jpg , the key will be the full path to the object (`images/photos/2024/beach.jpg`) .This allows s3 to locate the object eventhough it may appear to be a folder structure . `In other words ,a file path is what is refered to as a key`


**KEY FEATURES OF AN S3 BUCKET** 
1. **Under Properties you can see the following features** : 

+ S3 Bucket Overview
+ AWS bucket arn 

+ `Versioning` : This allows you to keep multiple versions of an object in the same bucket. This is useful for data protection and recovery as it allows you to restore previous versions of an object if it gets deleted or overwritten.

+ `Bucket ABAC` : 
This feature allows you to control access to your S3 bucket using Attribute-Based Access Control (ABAC). With ABAC, you can define access policies based on user attributes, such as tags or metadata, rather than just relying on traditional identity-based access control. This provides more granular and flexible access control for your S3 resources.

+ `Default encryption`:
This feature allows you to automatically encrypt all objects stored in your S3 bucket using a specified encryption method. You can choose to use AWS-managed keys (SSE-S3), AWS Key Management Service (KMS) keys (SSE-KMS), or customer-provided keys (SSE-C) for encryption. This helps ensure that your data is protected at rest and meets security and compliance requirements.

+ `Intelligent-Tiering Archive configurations`:
This feature allows you to automatically move objects between different storage tiers based on access patterns. The Intelligent-Tiering storage class has two access tiers: frequent access and infrequent access. When you enable this feature, S3 will automatically move objects that have not been accessed for a certain period of time (e.g., 90 days) to the infrequent access tier, which has lower storage costs. If an object in the infrequent access tier is accessed again, it will be moved back to the frequent access tier. This helps optimize storage costs while ensuring that your data is still accessible when needed.

+ `Server access logging`:
This feature allows you to log requests made to your S3 bucket. When enabled, S3 will create log files that contain details about the requests, such as the requester, bucket name, request time, action performed, and response status. These logs can be useful for auditing, monitoring, and troubleshooting access to your S3 bucket.

+ `AWS CloudTrail data events`
This feature allows you to log API calls made to your S3 bucket using AWS CloudTrail. When enabled, CloudTrail will capture data events for S3, which include object-level API operations such as GetObject, PutObject, and DeleteObject. This provides detailed visibility into the actions performed on your S3 objects, allowing you to track access and changes to your data for security and compliance purposes.

+ `Event notifications `:
This feature allows you to configure notifications for specific events that occur in your S3 bucket. You can set up notifications to trigger when certain actions happen, such as when an object is created, deleted, or modified. These notifications can be sent to various destinations, such as Amazon Simple Notification Service (SNS), Amazon Simple Queue Service (SQS), or AWS Lambda functions. This enables you to automate workflows and respond to changes in your S3 bucket in real-time.

`Amazon EventBridge`:
This is a serverless event bus service that allows you to connect applications using data from your S3 bucket. You can create rules in EventBridge to trigger actions based on specific events that occur in your S3 bucket, such as object creation or deletion. This enables you to build event-driven architectures and automate workflows based on changes in your S3 data.

`Transfer acceleration`:
This feature allows you to speed up the transfer of files to and from your S3 bucket by using Amazon CloudFront's globally distributed edge locations. When you enable transfer acceleration, S3 will route your uploads and downloads through the nearest CloudFront edge location, which can significantly reduce latency and improve transfer speeds, especially for large files or when transferring data over long distances. This is particularly beneficial for applications that require fast and efficient data transfers to and from S3.

`Object Lock`:
This feature allows you to prevent objects in your S3 bucket from being deleted or overwritten for a specified period of time. Object Lock can be used to meet regulatory compliance requirements or to protect critical data from accidental deletion. When you enable Object Lock, you can set a retention period for each object, during which it cannot be deleted or modified. This helps ensure the integrity and durability of your data in S3.

`Requester pays`:

This feature allows you to configure your S3 bucket so that the requester (the person or application making the request) is responsible for paying the costs associated with accessing the data in the bucket. When you enable requester pays, anyone who wants to access the objects in your bucket will need to include a specific parameter in their request to indicate that they are willing to pay for the access. This can be useful in scenarios where you want to share data with others but do not want to incur the costs of data transfer and retrieval yourself.

`Static website hosting`:
This feature allows you to host a static website directly from your S3 bucket. When you enable static website hosting, S3 will serve the content of your bucket as a website, allowing you to specify an index document (e.g., index.html) and an error document (e.g., error.html). This is a cost-effective way to host simple websites or web applications that do not require server-side processing, as S3 can handle the storage and delivery of your website content without the need for additional infrastructure.

2. **Permissions** :



+ Block public access (bucket settings) : These restricts public access to all the content in your s3 bucket . You can enable if you want public access into the content of the bucket 

+ `s3 Bucket policy` : This is a resource-based policy that you can attach to your S3 bucket to define permissions for the bucket and its contents. A bucket policy is written in JSON format and specifies who can access the bucket, what actions they can perform, and under what conditions. Bucket policies allow you to grant or deny permissions to specific AWS accounts, IAM users, or groups, and you can also specify conditions such as IP address restrictions or time-based access. This provides a powerful way to manage access control for your S3 bucket and ensure that only authorized users can interact with your data.

`Difference S3 Bucket Policy and IAM policy for s3 bucket `:
+ S3 bucket policy is a resource-based policy that is attached directly to the S3 bucket, while an IAM policy is an identity-based policy that is attached to an IAM user, group, or role.


+ `Object Ownership`:
This feature allows you to control ownership of objects in your S3 bucket. By default, the AWS account that uploads an object to the bucket owns that object. However, with Object Ownership, you can configure the bucket to automatically take ownership of all objects uploaded to it, regardless of which AWS account uploads them. This can simplify access management and permissions for objects in the bucket, as all objects will be owned by the same account.


+ `Access control list (ACL)`:
This is a legacy access control mechanism that allows you to manage permissions for individual objects and buckets. ACLs are less flexible than bucket policies and IAM policies, and AWS recommends using bucket policies or IAM policies for most access control scenarios. However, ACLs can still be used in certain cases, such as when you need to grant access to specific AWS accounts or when working with cross-account access.


+ `Cross-origin resource sharing (CORS)`: 

This feature allows you to configure your S3 bucket to allow cross-origin requests from web browsers. CORS is a security feature implemented by web browsers that restricts web pages from making requests to a different domain than the one that served the web page. By configuring CORS for your S3 bucket, you can specify which origins (domains) are allowed to access the resources in your bucket, as well as which HTTP methods (GET, POST, etc.) are permitted. This is useful when you want to allow web applications hosted on different domains to access resources stored in your S3 bucket.


team4techsolutions.com --permission--> apple.com



3: **Metrics **

+  `Bucket Metrics`:
This feature allows you to monitor the performance and usage of your S3 bucket by providing various metrics such as the number of requests, data transfer, and storage usage. You can use these metrics to gain insights into how your bucket is being used and to identify any potential issues or areas for optimization.

+ `Storage class analysis`:

This feature allows you to analyze the storage class usage of your objects in S3. It provides insights or information into how your objects are distributed across different storage classes (e.g., Standard, Intelligent-Tiering, Glacier) and can help you optimize your storage costs by identifying objects that may be better suited for a different storage class based on their access patterns.

+ `Replication Metrics`:
This feature allows you to monitor the replication status of your objects in S3. If you have set up cross-region replication or same-region replication for your bucket, you can use these metrics to track the progress of replication and ensure that your data is being replicated as expected.


4. **Management**

+ `Lifecycle rules`:
This feature allows you to define rules for automatically transitioning objects between different storage classes or for expiring objects after a certain period of time. For example, you can set up a lifecycle rule to transition objects that have not been accessed for 30 days to the Infrequent Access storage class, or to expire objects that are older than 365 days. This helps you manage your storage costs by automatically optimizing the storage class of your objects based on their access patterns and retention requirements.

+ `Replication rules`:
This feature allows you to set up rules for replicating objects from one S3 bucket to another. You can configure replication rules to specify which objects should be replicated, where they should be replicated to (e.g., another region), and how the replication should be performed (e.g., asynchronously). This is useful for disaster recovery, data backup, or for maintaining copies of your data in different locations for compliance or performance reasons, migrating data from one region to another or aws account to another .

+ `Inventory Configurations:`
This feature allows you to generate reports about the objects in your S3 bucket. You can configure inventory reports to include details such as object size, storage class, encryption
status, and more. These reports can be generated on a daily or weekly basis and can help you gain insights into the contents of your bucket, identify trends in object storage, and assist with auditing and compliance requirements.



5. **File systems** : ( Take this away )

+ Versioning needs to be enabled to create a file system 

6. `Access Points in s3 bucket`:

This feature allows you to create custom access points for your S3 bucket. An access point is a named network endpoint that you can create to provide specific permissions and access controls for a subset of objects in your bucket. Access points allow you to manage access to your S3 bucket at a more granular or fined grained or narrow  level, enabling you to define different permissions for different groups of users or applications. You can create multiple access points for the same bucket, each with its own set of permissions and policies, which can help simplify access management and improve security for your S3 resources.

**USE CASE OF AN S3 BUCKET** 

+ `Backup and Recovery`:  It can be used to backup data which the data can be used for disaster recovery
+ `Data Storage` : You can store any amount of data 
+ `Static Website hosting` : S3 buckets can host HTML , CSS , Javascript and other web files .You can intergrate with other aws services like Route53 , Cloudfront Distribution (CDN)
+ `Software Delivery or File Distribution` : 
+ `Data Migration` : it can help you to migrate data from on premise to the cloud & verse versa 
+ `Archiving Data` : S3 has a Glacier which provides cost effective archiving solutions for data that needs to be retained for compliance , and long term access 
+ `Versioning`: to keep duplicate of your data and also protect your data for unintended delete etc 
+ `Big data analytics` : S3 can be used to store and analyze big data sets using services like Amazon Athena, Amazon EMR, and AWS Glue.



Amazon S3 SECURITY 

1.  `Enforced User Base Security on AMAZON S3` : This is how we conrol users to access s3 resources within our aws account 

+ Use AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) to manage access to your S3 buckets and objects. Create IAM policies that grant the least privilege necessary for users and applications to perform their tasks. 

+ We grant access both at the Bucket Level permission and Object level permission .When the user has bucket level permission they can configure the settings on the bucket . 
+ You can enable MFA (Multi Factor Authentication) .You can set your aws s3 to require MFA for users trying to access your S3 buckets through aws console 
+ You can enable MFA delete to prevent users from trying to delete objects or turn off versioning on your s3 bucket ( Data protection)

2. `Resource Base Security S3`
This is how we control access to our s3 resources from other aws accounts or from the public . This is done using bucket policies and access control lists (ACLs) .

+ Bucket Policies:  Use bucket policies to define permissions for your S3 buckets and objects. Bucket policies are written in JSON format and allow you to specify who can access your bucket, what actions they can perform, and under what conditions. You can use bucket policies to grant access to specific AWS accounts, IAM users, or groups, and you can also specify conditions such as IP address restrictions or time-based access 

example of a Bucket Policy 



`Example of a Bucket Policy`

{
    "Version": "2012-10-17", 
    "Statement": [
        {
            "Sid": "PublicReadGetObject",
            "Effect": "Allow",
            "Principal": "*",
            "Action": "s3:GetObject",
            "Resource": "arn:aws:s3:::mydemobucket067/202408112343_cover.jpeg"
        }
    ]
}


`Version` : Specifies the version of the s3 policy language 
`statement`: The statement specifies specific permissions and conditions 
`Sid` : This helps to identify statements within a policy giving more information about what the statement (permissiobn) will do . 
`Effect` : This simply specifies weather the permission in the statement is allowed or denied 
`Principal` : It specifies who the policy applies to or who can use the policy 
`Action`: This specifies what the user (principal) can do with the permission 
`resource` : This specifies which resources the policy applies to . it can be a single resource or multiple resources . 




+ `ACL ( Access control list)`:

This is a legacy access control mechanism that allows you to manage permissions for individual objects and buckets. ACLs are less flexible than bucket policies and IAM policies, and AWS recommends using bucket policies or IAM policies for most access control scenarios. However, ACLs can still be used in certain cases, such as when you need to grant access to specific AWS accounts or when working with cross-account access.

`Object ACL` : This will manage permission for individual objects in the bucket

`Bucket ACL` : This will manage permissions for the entire bucket and all objects in the bucket 

3. Enforcing Data Security in S3 :

+ Use encryption to protect your data at rest and in transit. S3 provides several options for encryption, including server-side encryption (SSE) with AWS-managed keys (SSE-S3), server-side encryption with AWS Key Management Service (KMS) keys (SSE-KMS), and client-side encryption. Choose the appropriate encryption method based on your security requirements and compliance needs.

+ Setup Data encryption in transit by setting a bucket policy to allow https request to and from your bucket 

+ Enable versioning on your S3 bucket to protect against accidental deletion or overwriting of objects. With versioning enabled, you can restore previous versions of objects if they are deleted or modified.

+ Use Object Lock to prevent objects from being deleted or overwritten for a specified period of time. This can help meet regulatory compliance requirements and protect critical data.

+ Implement access logging to monitor and audit access to your S3 bucket. This can help you identify unauthorized access attempts and ensure compliance with security policies.

+ Regularly review and update your bucket policies and IAM policies to ensure they follow the principle of least privilege and do not grant unnecessary permissions.

+ Use AWS CloudTrail to log API calls made to your S3 bucket for auditing and compliance purposes. This provides visibility into who is accessing your S3 resources and what actions they are performing.

+ Consider using AWS Config to monitor and evaluate the configuration of your S3 buckets against best practices and compliance standards. AWS Config can help you identify any misconfigurations or security vulnerabilities in your S3 setup.




**HOW TO CREATE A STATIC WEBSITE IN AN S3 BUCKET**

+ First enable Public access to your s3 bucket 
+ Add a bucket policy to allow access to the objects in the bucket (index.html)
+ Click on the s3 bucket go to properties , scroll down and look for `Static website hosting` and click on edit 
+ Configuration change from disable to enable , hosting type `host static website` : next type the index document e.g index.html and save changes > it will give you an s3 bucket endpoint 





**VERSIONING IN S3 BUCKET**

`What is versioning ?`

Versioning is a feature in Amazon S3 that allows you to keep multiple versions of an object in the same bucket. When you enable versioning on a bucket, every time you upload a new version of an object with the same key (name), S3 automatically creates a new version of that object instead of overwriting the existing one. This means that you can have multiple versions of the same object stored in your bucket, and you can easily retrieve or restore previous versions if needed. 

This feature is useful for data protection and recovery because it preserves every version of every object stored in the bucket, even if a user accidentally deletes or modifies an object. 

+ You can version all the objects in your s3 bucket 
+ Versioning is enabled at the bucket level 
+ When versioning is enabled ,existing objects do not have any versions only new objects uploaded will have version ID 


*Managing Versions* 


+ Each version of an object is assigned a unique version ID.(example of a version i.d `BCAbow4u7_Di.xPwKXZCBrEd2V5r1_Pe`)
+ You can list, retrieve, and restore previous versions of an object using the `version ID`.
+ Deleting an object with versioning enabled does not actually remove the object; it adds a `delete marker` to indicate that the object is logically deleted.

+ `What is a delete marker`: When you delete an object that has versioning enabled, S3 does not permanently remove the object. Instead, it adds a delete marker, which is a placeholder that indicates the object has been deleted.You can restore the object by deleting the delete marker 

**USE CASES OF VERSIONING** or `Advantages of Versioning in S3 Bucket` 

+ Protect against accidental deletion or overwriting of objects. If a user accidentally deletes an object or uploads a new version that overwrites an existing one, you can easily restore the previous version using versioning.

+ Maintain a history of changes to your objects. Versioning allows you to keep track of all changes made to an object over time, which can be useful for auditing and compliance purposes.

+ Enable data recovery. If an object is accidentally deleted or modified, you can quickly recover the previous version without needing to restore from a backup.

+ Facilitate application development and testing. Versioning allows developers to test changes to objects without affecting the production version, making it easier to iterate ( move through ) and improve applications.

+ Support for data retention policies. With versioning, you can implement data retention policies that require keeping multiple versions of objects for a certain period of time to meet regulatory compliance requirements.


**Disadvantages of Versioning in S3** 

+ Increased storage costs: Since versioning keeps multiple versions of objects, it can lead to increased storage costs if you have a large number of versions or if you frequently update objects.

+ Complexity in managing versions: Managing multiple versions of objects can add complexity to your application logic, especially when it comes to retrieving and restoring specific versions.

+ Potential for confusion: If not properly managed, having multiple versions of objects can lead to confusion about which version is the most current or which version should be used in a given context.

+ Performance impact: In some cases, retrieving specific versions of objects may take longer than retrieving the latest version, especially if you have a large number of versions to sift through.

**Best Practices for Versioning in S3**

+ Enable versioning for critical buckets to prevent accidental data loss.
+ Implement MFA (Multi-Factor Authentication) Delete for added security to prevent versioning from being disabled and objects deleted . 
+ Regularly review and clean up old versions of objects that are no longer needed to manage storage costs effectively 
+ Use versioning in combination with other S3 features such as lifecycle policies and Object Lock to optimize data management and protection.
+ Implement proper naming conventions and documentation for your versions to avoid confusion and ensure that you can easily identify and restore the correct version when needed.
+ Monitor your versioning usage and costs using S3 metrics and AWS Cost Explorer to ensure that your managing cost effieciently

**S3 Replication** 

`What is S3 Replication ?`

S3 Replication is a feature in Amazon S3 that allows you to automatically replicate objects from one S3 bucket (the source bucket) to another S3 bucket (the destination bucket) in the same or different AWS region. This feature is useful for disaster recovery, data backup, and maintaining copies of your data in different locations for compliance or performance reasons . It can also be used for data migration . With S3 Replication, you can ensure that your data is available and protected across multiple locations, providing increased durability and availability for your S3 objects.


You can configure s3 replication under `Management` by creating a replication rule 

Amazon S3 offers two types of replication to help you manage your data across different regions and accounts: `Cross-Region Replication (CRR)` and `Same-Region Replication (SRR)`. Here’s a breakdown of both:

1. `Cross-Region Replication (CRR)`:
+ CRR allows you to replicate objects from a source bucket in one AWS region to a destination
bucket in a different AWS region. This is useful for disaster recovery, compliance,data migration and latency reduction by keeping copies of your data closer to your users.

+ *Automatic replication* : Objects uploaded to the source bucket are automatically replicated to the destination bucket in another region.
+ Versioning has to be turned on , on both buckets ( source and destination)
+ Replication can take some time depending on the size and the number of objects
+ You can replicate specific objects based on tags and prefixes
+ when it comes to object ownership , The destination bucket can have a different owner than the source bucket. To replicate existing objects you will need to use a `Batch Operation Job`
+ You can replicate existing objects in the s3 bucket prior to setting replication and you can choose not to replicate existing objects but after replication is setup any new objects will be replicated from the source bucket to the destination bucket 

Source bucket ----> This is the bucket that has the data you need to replicate 
Destination bucket ---> This is the bucket where the data needs to be send to . 



*Use case* 

+ Disaster recovery backing up data in another region just in case something in the current region you can restore back the data 
+ Lower latency for users accessing the data ( sending data closer to the user)
+ Migrating data from one region to another during migration process
+ Compliance with data residency requirements 

e.g replicating data from us-east-1 to ca-central-1




2. `Same Region Replication (SRR)`

SRR allows you to replicate objects within the same AWS region, essentially creating duplicates in the same region's bucket. This is useful for data protection and backup within the same region.

+ Automatic Replication same as CRR
+ Versioning: Like CRR, SRR requires versioning to be enabled on both buckets.
+ SRR can help protect against accidental deletions or overwrites by keeping multiple copies of the data.
+ You can also specify which objects to replicate using prefixes and tags.
+ Configure Bucket policies and IAM permissions
+ when setting up replication we can select if we want the objects from our source bucket to move into a different storage class in our destination. 
+ Add a bucket policy to the destination bucket 

`Example`:

{
  "Version": "2012-10-17",
  "Statement": [
    {
      "Effect": "Allow",
      "Principal": {
        "Service": "s3.amazonaws.com"
      },
      "Action": [
        "s3:ReplicateObject",
        "s3:ReplicateDelete",
        "s3:ReplicateTags"
      ],
      "Resource": "arn:aws:s3:::backupreplicatedbucket01/*"
    }
  ]
}



3. `Cross Account Replication` 

Allows you to replicate objects from one aws account to another within an Organisational Unit.

+ Automatic Replication same as CRR
+  Versioning: Like CRR, SRR, cross account replication requires versioning to be enabled on both buckets.
+ Configure Bucket policies and IAM permissions


`example of cross account bucket policy`

{
  "Version": "2012-10-17",
  "Statement": [
    {
      "Effect": "Allow",
      "Principal": {
        "AWS": "arn:aws:iam::<SOURCE-ACCOUNT-ID>:root"
      },
      "Action": [
        "s3:ReplicateObject",
        "s3:ReplicateDelete",
        "s3:ReplicateTags"
      ],
      "Resource": "arn:aws:s3:::<DESTINATION-BUCKET-ARN>/*"
    }
  ]
}


`additional replication options `


`replication time controller  (additional fees)` : S3 Replication Time Control (RTC) is an add-on feature for S3 Replication (CRR/SRR) that guarantees your objects will be replicated to the destination bucket within 15 minutes, 99.99% of the time.

`replication metrics   (additional fees)`:
S3 Replication Metrics is a feature that provides detailed metrics and insights into the replication status of your objects in S3. With S3 Replication Metrics, you can monitor the progress of replication, track the number of objects replicated, and identify any replication failures or issues. This feature helps you ensure that your data is being replicated as expected and allows you to troubleshoot any replication problems effectively.
`delete marker replication` :
Delete Marker Replication is a feature in S3 Replication that allows delete markers (which indicate that an object has been deleted) to be replicated from the source bucket to the destination bucket. This ensures that when an object is deleted in the source bucket, the corresponding delete marker is also replicated to the destination bucket, helping to maintain consistency between the two buckets.
`replica modification sync` : Replica Modification Sync is a feature in S3 Replication that allows changes made in the destination bucket to be synced back to the source bucket.



**S3 storage classes** : 

`What is an S3 Storage class ?`:
An S3 storage class is a classification that defines the durability, availability, and cost of storing objects in Amazon S3. Each storage class is designed for different use cases and access patterns, allowing you to optimize your storage costs based on how frequently you access your data and how long you need to retain (keep) it. 

Different Types of Storage Classes in S3 

- Amazon S3 standard : General Purpose 
- Amazon s3 Standard-infrequent Access  ( IA)
- Amazon S3 One Zone-Infrequent Access 
- Amazon S3 Glacier Instant retrieval 
- Amazon S3 Glacier Flexible Retrieval  (formerly Glacier)
- Amazon S3 Glacier Deep Archive 
- Amazon S3 Intelligient Tiering 
- Reduced redundancy 





The main S3 storage classes include:
1. `S3 Standard - General Purpose`: This is the default storage class for S3 and is designed for frequently accessed data. It provides high durability, availability, and performance, making it suitable for a wide range of use cases.

+ 99.9999 availability 
+ Provides low latency 
+ It can sustain two concurren facility failures 

USE CASE ( frequently access data , Mobile and gaming application ,Content distribution )

2. `S3 Standard-IA (Infrequent Access)`: This storage class is
designed for data that is accessed less frequently but requires rapid access when needed. It offers lower storage costs compared to S3 Standard but has a retrieval fee for accessing objects.

+ Less expensive when compared to the  standard storage class
+ it offers durability as s3 standard storage class but at a lower cost and with a retrieval fee will be applied when retreiving data 
+ Its good for backups , disaster recovery data . 
+ Minimum Storage duration is 30 days 


3. `S3 One Zone-IA (Infrequent Access )`: This storage class is similar to S3 Standard
IA but stores data in a single availability zone, making it more cost-effective for infrequently accessed data that can be recreated if the availability zone is lost.
- Its provides lower storage cost when compared to s3 standard cost
+ Minimum Storage duration is 30 days 

`USE CASE` ( Cost optimization , compliance , secondary backup copies of on premise data , )


4. `S3 Intelligent-Tiering`: This storage class automatically moves objects between two access tiers (frequent access and infrequent access) based on changing access patterns, helping to optimize costs without performance impact.

5. `Glacier Instant Retrieval`:
This storage class is designed for long-term archival of data that requires immediate access when needed. It provides low-cost storage with retrieval times in milliseconds, making it suitable for data that is rarely accessed but needs to be retrieved quickly when required.

+ Long-lived archive data accessed once a quarter with instant retrieval in milliseconds
+ Minimum storage duration is 90 days 
+ Use case : Backup Data , Compliance 

6. `Glacier Flexible Retrieval (formerly Glacier)`:
This storage class is designed for long-term archival of data that is rarely accessed and can tolerate retrieval times of several hours. It offers lower storage costs compared to Glacier Instant Retrieval but has a longer retrieval time.

+Long-lived archive data accessed once a year with retrieval of minutes to hours

+ Minimum storage duration is 90 days 
+ Use case : Backup Data , Compliance 

7. `S3 Glacier Deep Archive`: This is the lowest-cost storage class for long-term
archival of data that is rarely accessed and can tolerate retrieval times of up to 12 hours.
+ Long-lived archive data accessed less than once a year with retrieval of hours
+ Minimum storage duration is 180 days 
+ Use case : Backup Data , Compliance 


8.`Reduced Redundancy` : Noncritical, frequently accessed data with milliseconds access (not recommended as S3 Standard is more cost effective)


`S3 Life Cycle Rule`:

Lifecycle rules allow you to automatically transition objects between different storage classes or expire objects after a certain period of time. For example, you can set up a lifecycle rule to transition objects that have not been accessed for 30 days to the Infrequent Access storage class, or to expire objects that are older than 365 days. This helps you manage your storage costs by automatically optimizing the storage class of your objects based on their access patterns and retention requirements.

**How to Setup An S3 Life Cycle Rule**

+ Go to the s3 bucket and click on management
+ Click on `Create lifecycle rule`
+ Give the rule a name and description
+ Choose the scope of the rule ( apply to all objects or specific objects based on prefix or
tags )
+ Define the lifecycle actions ( transition to another storage class or expire objects)
+ Set the timing for the lifecycle actions (e.g., after a certain number of days since
object creation or last access)
+ Review and create the lifecycle rule

This is a feature in s3 that helps to send notifications when certain actions takes places in your s3 bucket e.g `put request (Uploads an object into your bucket) , get request ( download an object from your bucket)`. This is good because you can monitor changes in your s3 objects

***AMAZON S3 DATA OR OBJECT SECURITY***

 `S3 ENCRYPTION `

This is when you secure your s3  objects from unauthorized access.It converts plain text or data into an encoded version (ciphertext).The process of  protecting data is called encryption and to read /unlock the data you need to decrypt THE data . (lock data = encryption  & unlock = decrypt data )

`Types of S3 Encryption`

1.  `Server-side encryption (SSE)` : This is when S3 handles the encryption and decryption of your data automatically.So this is a method of encrypting data at rest in any cloud storage which an example here is s3 . There are three options for server-side encryption:

**Three Options of Server side enryption in S3:**

a. `SSE-S3 (Server-Side Encryption with Amazon S3-Managed Keys)`:

+  This option uses keys managed by Amazon S3 to encrypt your data. It provides a simple and cost-effective way to encrypt your objects without the need for you to manage encryption keys.
+ This is the default key type that you can use . AWS manages the key for you .This means that aws will encrypt and decrypt automatically using AES-256 which is a strong block cipher 

b. `SSE-KMS (Server-Side Encryption with AWS Key Management Service)`:
+ This option allows you to use keys managed by AWS Key Management Service (KMS) for encryption. It provides additional features such as key rotation, access control, and audit logging for your encryption keys.
+ With SSE-KMS, you have more control over your encryption keys and can manage them using
AWS KMS. You can create and manage your own customer-managed keys (CMKs) or use AWS-managed keys for encryption.
+ You can create `customer managed keys` or `aws managed keys` to enhance security , This gives you more control and ability to audit key usage 
+ Keys are stored and managed in AWS Key Management Service (KMS).


**Benefits*

+ Its provides you key rotation and you as the customer is responsible for rotating the key 
+ It provides Access control using IAM policies
+ It provides key usage audit through cloudtrail


*Limitations of KMS* 

+ You may be impacted by the KMS (Key management service ) limits (Number of keys you can create) but to resolve this you will need to request an increase for the service quotas
+ To many api calls can be an issue for your KMS ( it generate data key API) and also when you download , it calls the decrypt kms api 
+ Slightly more expensive than SSE-S3 due to KMS API cost.

`Use Case` : When you need auditing, fine-grained access control, compliance, and security governance for sensitive workloads (e.g., PHI, PCI, PII).


c. ` Dual-Layer server side Encryption with AWS key Management Service Keys (DSSE-KMS)`

DSSE-KMS is an enhanced encryption option for Amazon S3 that applies two independent layers of encryption at rest, both using AWS KMS–managed keys. It is designed for organizations that require higher assurance, defense-in-depth, and compliance with strict regulatory frameworks.

Instead of relying on a single encryption key, S3 encrypts every object twice using this key — once with a data key, and again with a separate KMS key, and both layers must be decrypted for the data to be accessed. This reduces the chance of data exposure, even if a key is compromised.

DSSE-KMS is typically required by customers with very sensitive workloads, such as:

+ Healthcare (HIPAA/PHI)
+ Government and Public Sector (FedRAMP, CJIS)
+ Financial Services (PCI-DSS, SOX)
+ Critical Infrastructure or National Security


2. `Client Side Encryption (CSE)`

This is when you encrypt data on the client side before uploading it to S3. With client-side encryption, you are responsible for managing your own encryption keys and performing the encryption and decryption operations on the client side. This gives you full control over the encryption process and allows you to use your own encryption algorithms if desired.

+ You can use the AWS Encryption SDK or other encryption libraries to perform client-side encryption.
+ You are responsible for securely managing your encryption keys and ensuring that they are not lost or compromised
+ Client-side encryption can be more complex to implement and manage compared to server-side encryption, but it provides an additional layer of security by keeping the encryption process entirely under your control.
+ AWS stores only the encrypted object, and never has access to the plaintext data or your keys.
+ When you manage your own keys, you are fully responsible for : Key storage , Key rotation , Access Control , Encryption and Decryption logic . 


**Use Case**: When you want full control over encryption and key management, or when you need to use custom encryption algorithms that are not supported by AWS.

3. `Encryption in Transit or in Flight`: 

This is when you encrypt data while it is being transmitted between your client and S3, or between S3 and other AWS services. You can use HTTPS to encrypt data in transit, ensuring that it is protected from interception and tampering during transmission. Additionally, you can use AWS Key Management Service (KMS) to encrypt data in transit between S3 and other AWS services, providing an extra layer of security for your data as it moves within the AWS ecosystem.

+ Encryption in Transit : AWS S3 can encrypt Data in transit using HTTPS which uses ( SSL/TLS protocols ). Encryption in Transit ensures that data is protected while it is being transferred between a client and Amazon S3.To secure data in motion, S3 supports HTTPS, which uses SSL/TLS protocols to prevent eavesdropping, tampering, or man-in-the-middle attacks.

+ Amazon S3 Exposes or provides two endpoints ( HTTP and HTTPS ) ( http is unsecured while HTTPS ( Secured )
+ By default, most AWS SDKs, the AWS CLI, and modern browsers use the HTTPS endpoint automatically.
+ You can enforce HTTPS-only access at the bucket level using an S3 bucket policy to prevent insecure HTTP requests.

`example of an https bucket policy to enforce ssl/tls (https)`

{
    "Version": "2012-10-17",
    "Id": "EnforceHTTPS",
    "Statement": [
        {
            "Sid": "DenyHTTP",
            "Effect": "Deny",
            "Principal": "*",
            "Action": "s3:*",
            "Resource": [
                "arn:aws:s3:::your-bucket-name",
                "arn:aws:s3:::your-bucket-name/*"
            ],
            "Condition": {
                "Bool": {
                    "aws:SecureTransport": "false"
                }
            }
        }
    ]
}




**S3 ACCESS LOGS**

`What is an S3 Access Logs ? `

This is a feature in S3 that allows you to log requests made to your S3 bucket. When access logging is enabled, Amazon S3 creates log files that contain details about each request, such as the requester’s IP address, the time of the request, the action performed, and the response status. These logs can be useful for `monitoring and auditing access to your S3 resources, troubleshooting issues, and analyzing usage patterns `. You can configure access logging to store the log files in a designated S3 bucket for easy access and analysis.

+ Helps you analyze access patterns (e.g., frequency, source IPs, top objects accessed).
+ Supports compliance frameworks like SOC, HIPAA, and PCI, where access visibility is required.
+ Access logs are stored in another S3 bucket that you specify (known as the target bucket).
+ The target logging bucket must be in the same AWS Region as the source bucket.


**Access Log Format** ;
The access log entries

captures vital information such as 

+ Who accessed the bucket
+ When they accessed it
+ What operation they performed
+ From which (IP, browser, etc.)
+ Response status and protocol details


The access log entries are recorded in a specific format, which includes fields such as:

+ *Bucket Owner*: The AWS account ID of the bucket owner.
+ *Bucket*: The name of the S3 bucket.
+ *Time*: The time the request was made (in UTC).
+ *Remote IP*: The IP address of the requester.
+ *Requester*: The AWS account ID of the requester (if applicable).
+ *Request ID*: The unique ID for the request.
+ *Operation*: The type of operation (e.g., GET, PUT, DELETE).
+ *Key*: The object key being accessed.
+ *HTTP Status*: The HTTP response status code.
+ *Bytes Sent*: The number of bytes sent in the response.

Logs : its a series of events captured on your system and stored in a location 


+ Encryption (server side and client side encryption)
+ s3 Event notification 

`NOTE`  : 
+ Do not use the same bucket as your logging bucket . Its going to cause a logging loop and this will cost you money for storage 
+ When you store logs and you want to analyze them you can use a tool called Amazon "Athena"


Review s3 access logs bucket to ensure logs are coming in next class 




3. **AMAZON S3 MFA DELETE**

`What is S3 MFA Delete`:

MFA Delete is a security feature in Amazon S3 that adds an extra layer of protection to prevent accidental or unauthorized deletion of objects in your S3 bucket. When MFA Delete is enabled, you must provide a valid multi-factor authentication (MFA) token in addition to your AWS credentials to delete objects from the bucket. This helps to ensure that only authorized users with access to the MFA device can delete objects, providing an additional safeguard against data loss due to accidental deletions or malicious actions. 

MFA = Multi Factor Authentication 

MFA Delete protects your objects from 

+ Accidental deletion by authorized users
+ Malicious deletion by unauthorized users who may have compromised AWS credentials
+ MFA Delete can only be enabled or disabled by the bucket owner using the AWS CLI only through the root account 
+ It protects your objects in the bucket from permament delete 
+ It also protects your bucket from users from turning off versioning 


`Key points to note when it comes to MFA delete` 

+ MFA Delete can be enabled only by the Root User
+ Only works on Versioned S3 buckets
+ Cannot be managed via the AWS Console — only via AWS CLI or SDK
+ MFA Delete does not stop someone from deleting objects, but it protects permanent deletion of versions


`What must you do for MFA to work** ( Prerequisites)`

+ Versioning must be enabled on the s3 bucket in order to setup MFA delete 
+ You need to have an MFA device configured (this can be virtual MFA device (mobile app ) or you can have a hardware MFA)
+ Only the bucket owner (root account) can enable / disable MFA delete 

**How to enable MFA Delete using AWS CLI**

+ Enable versioning on the bucket if not already enabled. You can enable it on the aws console or through CLI By using the command below 
`aws s3api put-bucket-versioning --bucket your-bucket-name --versioning-configuration Status=Enabled`
+ You need access to the root Account ( sign in as the root user)
+ If MFA is not already setup at the root account you will need to setup one . 

In order to enabled and run the MFA delete command we will need the following 

+ The bucket name
+ You need the device arn 
+ AWS Profile 

below is the command to enable MFA Delete

  4. **AMAZON S3 PRE-SIGNED URL**  

+ A pre-signed URL in Amazon S3 allows you to `grant temporary access` to a specific object in your S3 bucket to anyone who has the URL. This can be particularly useful for allowing users to download or upload files without needing to make them public or share your AWS credentials.
+ you can create a pre-signed url on the aws console and on the commandline 

`What is the use case of using Pre-signed URL `
+ Sharing private content with users without making it public
+ Allowing temporary access to objects for specific operations (e.g., download, upload)
+ Enabling secure file uploads from client applications without exposing AWS credentials
+ Providing time-limited access to objects for third-party applications or services or individuals


5. **S3 GLACIIER VAULT LOCK** 

+ Amazon S3 Glacier Vault Lock is a feature that allows you to enforce compliance controls on your S3 Glacier vaults. With Vault Lock, you can create a vault lock policy that specifies the retention period for objects stored in the vault, and once the policy is locked, it cannot be changed or deleted. This ensures that your data remains protected and compliant with regulatory requirements, as it prevents any modifications to the retention settings of the vault. Vault Lock is particularly useful for organizations that need to meet strict compliance standards for data retention and immutability.


**Compliance control :** 

+ Designed to meet regulatory compliance (such as financial or healthcare regulations).
+ It Enforces write-once-read-many (WORM) storage models where data cannot be deleted or modified after it's written.(model)

**Vault Lock Policy** 

+ This policy defines who can delete archives and what actions are allowed on the vault and its content
+ Once the policy is locked, it cannot be overwritten, ensuring data integrity for the duration specified.

**Two-Step Process**

+ Initiate the Vault Lock: You can specify a Vault Lock policy, and it enters an in-progress state, allowing you to test the policy for a short period.
+ Lock the Vault: Once you are satisfied with the policy, you can lock it permanently. After this, it cannot be changed.

**Use Case**
+ Long-term data archiving with strict compliance requirements.
+ Ensuring data immutability for regulatory audits.
+ Protecting critical data from accidental or malicious deletion.
+ Financial Services: Ensuring data retention for a specified number of years to comply with regulations like SEC Rule 17a-4.
+ Healthcare: Storing patient records for a minimum retention period.
+ Legal: Preserving legal documents for audits or litigation purposes.

you can set a Vault Policy using AWS CONSOLE OR CLI OR SDK 

`Example of a vault lock policy`

{
  "Version": "2012-10-17",
  "Statement": [
    {
      "Sid": "DenyDelete",
      "Effect": "Deny",
      "Principal": "*",
      "Action": [
        "glacier:DeleteArchive",
        "glacier:DeleteVault",
        "glacier:DeleteVaultAccessPolicy",
        "glacier:DeleteVaultNotifications"
      ],
      "Resource": "arn:aws:glacier:us-west-2:123456789012:vaults/my-vault"
    }
  ]
}

`Steps on how to set a fault policy on aws console `

+ Go to the S3 Glacier service in the AWS Management Console.
+ Click on "Vaults" in the left navigation pane.
+ Select the vault for which you want to set the Vault Lock policy.
+ Click on the "Vault Lock" tab.
+ Click on "Initiate Vault Lock" to start the process.
+ Define your Vault Lock policy by specifying the actions you want to allow or deny, and the
resources it applies to.
+ Review the policy and click "Initiate Lock" to put the policy in place.
+ After the lock is initiated, you can test the policy for a specified period (e.g
7 days ) to ensure it works as expected. During this time, you can make changes to the policy if needed.
+ Once you are satisfied with the policy, click "Complete Lock" to permanently lock the policy

Note : Glacier standalone features no longer accepts new customers 


 **S3 OBJECT LOCK** 

+ Amazon S3 Object Lock is a feature that allows you to store objects using a write-once-read-many (WORM) model, which prevents objects from being deleted or overwritten for a specified retention period. With Object Lock, you can set retention periods on individual objects or entire buckets, ensuring that data remains immutable and compliant with regulatory requirements. This is particularly useful for organizations that need to meet strict compliance standards for data retention and immutability, such as in financial services, healthcare, and legal industries. Object Lock helps protect critical data from accidental or malicious deletion while ensuring that it remains accessible for the duration of the retention period.

+ Versioning must be enabled 
+ Object Lock in place it can block an object version deletion for a specific amount of time 


**Key Features of S3 Object Lock**

+ Write-Once-Read-Many (WORM) Model: Objects stored with Object Lock cannot be modified or deleted until the specified retention period expires.
+ Retention Modes: S3 Object Lock supports two retention modes: Governance Mode and Compliance Mode. In Governance Mode, users with specific permissions can override the retention settings, while in Compliance Mode, the retention settings cannot be overridden by any user, including the root user.
+ Legal Hold: You can apply a legal hold to an object, which prevents it from being
deleted or overwritten until the legal hold is removed, regardless of the retention period.
+ Versioning Requirement: Object Lock requires versioning to be enabled on the S3 bucket,
as it relies on versioning to manage the locked objects and their versions effectively.


+ It has two Lock Modes ( Governance Modes & Compliance Modes)

`Governance Mode`: Allows specific users (with special permissions) to overwrite or delete objects during the retention period.
example : you store an object and setup object lock for 3 months . If you have used the governanced mode , users with special permissions can still over write or delete the object before the 3 months elapses . 

`Compliance Mode` : Strictest form of protection; no one, including the root user, can delete or modify objects until the retention period expires. 

example : If you have used the Compliance mode , users no matter the type of permissions you have wont be able to delete or modify objects until after the retention period 

+`Retention Period` : 

You can set a retention period during which the object is protected.
The retention period can be based on regulatory or business requirements (e.g., retain for 7 years).
After the retention period, objects can be deleted or modified unless explicitly locked again.

+ `Legal Hold` :

S3 Object lock also has a feature called `legal hold` 
Legal Holds prevent object deletion, similar to a lock, but without an expiry date.
A legal hold can be applied and removed by authorized users at any time, without defining a retention period.

`To enable object lock on your s3 bucket` , 

+ click on the specific bucket
+ click on properties and scroll down to object lock .Click on edit and enable object lock 




7. **S3 ACCESS POINTS**

+ Amazon S3 Access Points is a feature that allows you to create unique access points with specific permissions and network controls for your S3 buckets. Each access point has its own hostname and can be configured with different access policies, making it easier to manage access to shared datasets in S3. Access Points provide a way to simplify data access for applications and users while maintaining security and control over who can access the data and how it can be accessed. This is particularly useful for organizations that need to provide controlled access to their S3 data across multiple teams, applications, or external partners without having to manage complex bucket policies or IAM permissions for each individual user or application.

+ It simplifies Access Management ( Each access point can have its own policy ,making it easy to control and manage permissions for specific use cases)
+ Custom network settings : we can setup access points to only accept request to our s3 buckets only within a VPC (Virtual Private Cloud) , it improves security by ensuring data access is restricted to certain environments 
+ We can have dedicated access points url : Each access point has its own DNS-compliant endpoint, allowing you to interact with a bucket via a unique and specific URL.This allows applications to use a dedicated access point instead of the bucket’s default endpoint, providing more granular control over traffic and requests.
+ It supports s3 Permissions and Policies : You can configure permissions using IAM policies, bucket policies, and access point policies, ensuring that only authorized users and roles can access the data through the access point.

Note;

+ Each access point has its own policy 
+ Each access point has a unique endpoint for accessing objects 
+ It can be public ,private or vpc restricted

8. **S3 CORS** Cross-Origin Resource Sharing (CORS ): 

+ Amazon S3 CORS is a feature that allows you to configure cross-origin resource sharing (CORS) rules for your S3 buckets. CORS is a security mechanism that enables web applications running in one domain to access resources in another domain. By configuring CORS rules for your S3 bucket, you can specify which origins are allowed to access the bucket's resources, as well as the HTTP methods and headers that are permitted. This is particularly useful for enabling web applications hosted on different domains to interact with your S3 data securely and efficiently, without exposing your bucket to unauthorized access.

Its a browser securrity rule controlling which websites can access resources in our s3 storage

+ `Origin` : 

The domain (protocol, host , and port ) from which the request is coming from (originates) 
e.g if a web page is hosted on https:team4techsolutions.com and it tries to access an s3 bucket the origin would be  https://team4techsolutions.com


+ `Cross Origin Requests`: 

Requests made from one domain (origin) to a different domain (S3 in this case). These requests are typically blocked by browsers for security reasons unless allowed through CORS configuration.


+ `CORS Rules` 

These rules define which origins can access your S3 resources, what HTTP methods are allowed, and what headers can be used in requests.

**HOW CAN WE ENHANCE THE PERFOMANCE OF OUR S3 BUCKET** 

Optimizing performance in Amazon S3 focuses on improving upload speed, download speed, and request efficiency—especially for large datasets, high-traffic applications, and global users.

1. **Multipart Upload**: For large files, use multipart upload to upload parts of the file in parallel, which can significantly reduce upload time and improve reliability.

wwhat it does 
+  Splits large files into smaller parts
+ Retries only failed parts
+ Allows parallel uploads for faster performance

`Steps to Upload a File Using Multi-Part Upload `:

+ Initiate the multipart upload to get an upload ID.
+ Upload each part of the file using the upload ID.
+ After all parts are uploaded, complete the multipart upload to assemble the parts into a single object

`CLI commands`
1. Initiate multipart upload:

`aws s3api create-multipart-upload --bucket your-bucket-name --key your-object`

2. Upload parts: 

`aws s3api upload-part --bucket your-bucket-name --key your-object --part-number 1 --upload-id your-upload-id --body part1`

`aws s3api upload-part --bucket your-bucket-name --key your-object --part-number 2 --upload-id your-upload-id --body part2`

3. Complete multipart upload:

`aws s3api complete-multipart-upload --bucket your-bucket-name --key your-object --upload-id your-upload-id --multipart-upload file://parts.json`


2. **S3 Transfer Acceleration**: This feature uses Amazon CloudFront's globally distributed edge locations to accelerate uploads and downloads to and from S3 buckets, improving performance for users located far from the bucket's region.

If users upload from different continents, enable S3 Transfer Acceleration to route traffic through CloudFront Edge locations for faster uploads.

+ Best for global teams, large media files, and SaaS apps
+ It cost more than normal transfers 


`Steps to Use or setup Transfer Accelerator on aws`

+ Go to the S3 bucket in the AWS Management Console.
+ Click on the "Properties" tab. 
+ Scroll down to the "Transfer acceleration" section and click "Edit".
+ Select "Enabled" to turn on transfer acceleration for the bucket.
+ Click "Save changes" to apply the settings.

or CLI 


`aws s3api put-bucket-accelerate-configuration --bucket your-bucket-name --accelerate-configuration Status=Enabled`





3. **S3 Object Caching**: Use Amazon CloudFront to cache frequently accessed S3 objects at edge locations, reducing latency and improving download performance for end-users.
+ Cache frequently accessed objects at edge locations
+ Reduces latency for global users
+ Ideal for static website hosting, media delivery, and APIs




**Storage Lens**

Amazon S3 Storage Lens is an analytics and visibility tool for S3 that provides organization-wide insights into your `storage usage`, `object counts`, and `activity trends` across all S3 buckets — not just one. It helps you `optimize costs`, `improve data lifecycle decisions`, and `detect anomalies (abnormal) ` such as unusual access or request spikes.

Storage Lens gives two categories of metrics:

+ Total storage usage
+ Storage by bucket
+ Storage by prefix
+ Object count
+ Growth over time
+ Storage class breakdown (Standard, IA, Glacier, etc.)
