# CLOUDFRONT 

`What is Cloudfront` 
Amazon CloudFront is a `content delivery network (CDN)` service provided by AWS that delivers data, videos, applications, and APIs to `users globally with low latency and high transfer speeds`. It uses a network of edge locations around the world to cache and deliver content closer to end-users, improving performance and reducing load on the origin servers. CloudFront supports various use cases, including website acceleration, media streaming, API acceleration, and secure content delivery.

+ can integrate cloudfront with other AWS services to improve application performance and security . 



Cloudfront will help to ensure that our users access our application , data , videos with 

+ Low latency 
+ Improve perfomance of your application , content is cached at the egde (locations)
+ Improve user experience 
+ Serve content globally 

`Core concepts or Terminologies used in Cloudfront`

1. `Edge Locations`:

These are data centers ( Mini data centres) located around the world where CloudFront caches copies of your content. When a user requests content, CloudFront delivers it from the nearest edge location, reducing latency and improving performance. 

These are part of AWS's global infrastructure for services like `Amazon CloudFront` (the content delivery network, or CDN) and `AWS global accelerators` . 


2. `Points of Presence` :

This is a general term for locations where AWS has infrastructure to serve content. It includes both edge locations and regional edge caches. Points of Presence are strategically located around the world to optimize content delivery and reduce latency for users accessing AWS services.


`Note `: aws has over 400 Edge Locations and more than 750 total Points of Presence (PoPs) globally.

**Cloudfront Features**

1. `Global Content Delivery` : CloudFront is a global CDN that uses AWS’s network of edge locations to distribute content closer to users around the world, ensuring fast delivery of web pages, videos, APIs, and applications.

2. `Build in security` 

+ You can integrate CloudFront with AWS WAF (Web Application Firewall) and AWS Shield to protect your application from DDoS attacks, Cross-Site Scripting (XSS), SQL Injection, and other common web exploits.
+ All content is delivered securely over HTTPS, ensuring data encryption in transit.

3. `Dynamic and Static Content` : CloudFront can deliver both static content (e.g., images, CSS, JavaScript) and dynamic content (e.g., API responses, personalized web pages), making it suitable for a wide range of applications.

4. `Cost Efficiency `

+ CloudFront offers a pay-as-you-go pricing model, where you only pay for the data transferred and requests made. This can be more cost-effective than maintaining your own global infrastructure for content delivery.
+ Since content is cached at edge locations, the number of requests reaching your origin (e.g., S3 or EC2) decreases, reducing data-transfer and compute costs.

5. Low Latency & High Performance
By caching your content at nearby edge locations, CloudFront ensures low latency, faster load times, and an improved user experience — no matter where your audience is located globally.

6. `Customizable Content Delivery` : You can configure CloudFront to deliver content based on specific behaviors, such as URL patterns, HTTP methods, and query string parameters. This allows you to optimize content delivery for different types of requests and user scenarios.

7. `Integration with AWS Services` : CloudFront seamlessly integrates with other AWS services like S3, EC2, Lambda@Edge, and API Gateway, allowing you to build powerful and scalable applications with enhanced performance and security.


`USE CASE OR WHEN IS IT NECESSARY TO IMPLEMENT CLOUDFRONT` 

+ When you have a global audience and want to ensure low latency and fast content delivery.
+ When you want to improve the performance of your website or application by caching content at edge locations.
+ When you need to protect your application from DDoS attacks and web exploits using AWS WAF and AWS Shield.
+ When you want to deliver both static and dynamic content efficiently.
+ When you want to reduce costs by minimizing data transfer from your origin servers and leveraging CloudFront
's pay-as-you-go pricing model.
+ When you want to integrate with other AWS services for a seamless and powerful application architecture.
+ When you want to serve content securely over HTTPS, ensuring data encryption in transit.
+ When you want to customize content delivery based on specific behaviors and user scenarios. 
+ When you want to distribute Software globally.CloudFront can distribute large files like software installers, game updates, and security patches efficiently.It delivers media content smoothly to users around the world, reducing buffering and maintaining high quality.Its good for platforms or applications like YouTube-style video sites, training portals, or corporate live events.


# AWS CLOUDFRONT ORIGIN :

An `Origin` is the location where your original content is stored — this is where CloudFront fetches data before distributing it to users through its global edge locations.
When a user requests content that isn’t already cached at the edge, CloudFront retrieves it from the origin, caches it, and then serves it to future users nearby.


### Different or Types of  Cloudfront Origins

1. `Amazon S3 Bucket` : You can use an S3 bucket as the origin for your CloudFront distribution. This is a common choice for serving static content such as images, videos, and web assets.
+ CloudFront pulls data directly from S3 and caches it at edge locations.
+ Combine with `Origin Access Identity (OAI)` to restrict S3 access only to CloudFront — this secures your bucket from public access.

2. `Amazon EC2 Instance` : You can use an EC2 instance as the origin for your CloudFront distribution. This is suitable for serving dynamic content or applications that require server-side processing.
+ CloudFront acts as a front layer to improve performance and global reach.

3. `Elastic Load Balancer (ELB)` : You can use an ELB as the origin for your CloudFront distribution. This is ideal for distributing traffic across multiple EC2 instances, providing high availability and scalability for your applications.
+ CloudFront can route requests to the ELB, which then distributes them to the backend instances

4. `AWS Lambda Functions` : You can use AWS Lambda functions as the origin for your CloudFront distribution. This allows you to execute serverless code in response to requests, enabling dynamic content generation and custom logic at the edge.
+ CloudFront can trigger Lambda@Edge functions to modify requests and responses, providing a powerful way to customize content delivery.

5. `Custom Origins` : You can use any HTTP/HTTPS server as a custom origin for your CloudFront distribution. This allows you to integrate with third-party services, on-premises servers, or any other content source that can serve HTTP responses.
+ CloudFront can fetch content from your custom origin and cache it at edge locations for improved performance.
`Example of setting up a CloudFront distribution with an S3 bucket as the origin`

6. `Api Gateways` ;
You can use Amazon API Gateway as the origin for your CloudFront distribution. This is ideal for accelerating API responses and improving performance for API-driven applications.
+ CloudFront can cache API responses at edge locations, reducing latency for users accessing your APIs globally

7. `Elemental MediaPackage `
You can use AWS Elemental MediaPackage as the origin for your CloudFront distribution. This is suitable for delivering live and on-demand video content with adaptive bitrate streaming.
+ CloudFront can cache video segments at edge locations, ensuring smooth playback for viewers around the world

8. `Vpc Origin `
You can use an Amazon Virtual Private Cloud (VPC) as the origin for your CloudFront distribution. This allows you to serve content from resources that are not publicly accessible, such as EC2 instances or load balancers within a VPC.
+ CloudFront can access your VPC resources securely using VPC endpoints, ensuring that your content
is delivered efficiently while maintaining the security of your private resources.


# Concepts Related to CloudFront Origins

`🌍 Origin Server`

+ This is the main source where your original content is stored.
+ It can be an S3 bucket, EC2 instance, Elastic Load Balancer, or any HTTP(S) backend.
When CloudFront doesn’t find a requested object in its edge cache, it fetches it directly from this origin server.

`Origin group` : 

An Origin Group helps improve availability and fault tolerance.
It allows you to configure multiple origins — typically a primary and a secondary.
If the primary origin becomes unavailable, CloudFront automatically fails over to the secondary origin, ensuring users continue to receive content without interruption.
+ This setup is common in disaster recovery or multi-region architectures.

`Origin Access Identity (OAI)` :
An OAI is a special CloudFront user that you can associate with your distribution to securely access your S3 bucket origin.
By creating an OAI and updating your S3 bucket policy to allow access only to that O
AI, you can prevent direct public access to your S3 bucket while still allowing CloudFront to fetch and serve content from it.
+ By doing this, your S3 bucket remains private and secure, and CloudFront serves content safely to users on your behalf.



`Features in Cloudfront` 

1. **Geo Restriction**

+ Geo restriction (also known as geoblocking) allows you to control access to your content based on the geographic location of your users. With this feature, you can specify which countries are allowed or denied access to your CloudFront distribution. This is particularly useful for complying with regional licensing agreements, content distribution regulations, or simply restricting access to certain markets. By enabling geo restriction, you can ensure that your content is only accessible to users in approved locations, enhancing security and compliance while optimizing content delivery for your target audience. 

+ Allow-list (`whitelist`) certain countries where users can access your content.
+ Block-list (`blacklist`) specific countries from accessing it.

If your application or website should not be accessible from China, Russia, or specific regions due to compliance or licensing, you can configure CloudFront to deny access for users from those countries.

2. **Price Classes** : 

CloudFront offers different price classes that allow you to control costs by specifying which edge locations can serve your content. By choosing a price class, you can limit the number of edge locations that CloudFront uses to deliver your content, which can help reduce costs while still providing good performance for your users. This is particularly useful for applications with a global audience, as it allows you to optimize content delivery based on your budget and performance requirements.

- Price class All : All regions - all edge locations own by aws --> Best perfomance , highest cost
- Price Class 200 : Most regions but it will exlude the most expensive edge locations - good balance of cost and reach 
- Price Class 100 : least expensive region --> Lowest cost but limited coverage 

3. **Cache Invalidations (TTL)**

+ Cache invalidation allows you to remove specific objects from CloudFront's cache before they expire. This is useful when you need to update content that has already been cached at edge locations, ensuring that users receive the most up-to-date version of your content. By creating an invalidation request, you can specify which objects to remove from the cache, and CloudFront will fetch the latest version from the origin server the next time those objects are requested. This helps maintain content freshness and consistency across your distribution.

`Steps to Create a Cache Invalidation in CloudFront`

You can do this through the aws console or CLI 

for aws console --->

1. Go to the CloudFront console in AWS Management Console.
2. Select the distribution for which you want to create an invalidation.
3. Click on the "Invalidations" tab.
4. Click on the "Create Invalidation" button.
5. In the "Create Invalidation" form, specify the paths of the objects you want
to invalidate. You can use wildcards (e.g., /images/*) to invalidate multiple objects at once.
6. Click on the "Invalidate" button to submit the invalidation request.


for CLI

`aws cloudfront create-invalidation --distribution-id your-distribution-id --paths "/*"`

This command will invalidate all objects in the distribution, forcing CloudFront to fetch the latest versions from the origin server. You can specify specific paths instead of "/*" if you only want to invalidate certain objects.



**GLOBAL ACCELERATOR**

What is Global Accelerator 

AWS Global Accelerator is a networking service that improves the availability and performance of your applications by directing user traffic through the AWS global network infrastructure. It provides static IP addresses that serve as a fixed entry point for your application, allowing you to route traffic to optimal endpoints based on health, geography, and routing policies. Global Accelerator helps reduce latency, improve application responsiveness, and enhance user experience by leveraging the extensive AWS global network to route traffic efficiently and reliably.

Instead of users connecting to your application over the public internet, Global Accelerator routes traffic over the `AWS global private network`, which is faster, more stable, and less congested.


+ It uses aws internal aws network to route traffic to your app
+ Global acelerator creates 2 anycast Ip for your application (static) anycast
+ The anycast ip sends traffic to the edge locations directly 

`Why Use Global Accelerator or advantages of Global accelerator?`

1. `Improved Performance` : By routing traffic through the AWS global network, Global Accelerator reduces latency and improves application responsiveness for users around the world.
2. `High Availability` : Global Accelerator automatically routes traffic to healthy endpoints, ensuring that your application remains available even if some endpoints become unhealthy or unavailable.
3. `Global Reach` : With AWS's extensive global infrastructure, Global Accelerator allows you to
serve users from edge locations around the world, providing a better experience for a global audience.
4. `Static IP Addresses` : Global Accelerator provides static IP addresses that serve as a fixed
entry point for your application, simplifying DNS management and improving reliability.
5. `Integration with AWS Services` : Global Accelerator seamlessly integrates with other AWS services like EC
2, Elastic Load Balancing, and CloudFront, allowing you to build powerful and scalable applications with enhanced performance and availability.
6. `DDoS Protection` : By routing traffic through the AWS global network, Global
Accelerator can help mitigate DDoS attacks and improve the security of your applications.
7. `Multi region support ; Global accelerator supports different aws regions which helps your app to become high availanle 
8. + Health Checks and automatic failovers


**What is Unicast and Anycast ip** 


**Anycast IP**: 

+ One IP address is shared by multiple servers or endpoints across the globe or network.
+ The same IP is announced in many AWS edge locations.
+ When a user connects, routing automatically sends them to the nearest and healthiest endpoint.

**Unicast IP**:
+ Each server or endpoint has its own unique IP address.
+ Users connect directly to that specific IP address, which may not be optimal for performance or availability
+  If the server is far away → higher latency
+ If that server goes down → no failover



`USE CASES` 

+ To serve traffic to your users globally ( application)
+ Gaming and video streaming applications ( less latency )
+ Real time application (better perfomance -- streaming applications)
+ Web app or Mobile app 



`Global Accelerator Architecture Terms`

1. `Endpoint Group`: 

An Endpoint Group represents a specific AWS Region where your application is deployed and where you want Global Accelerator to send traffic.

+ It defines which region users will be routed to.
+ You can have one or multiple endpoint groups depending on whether your app is deployed in one region or multi-region.

endpoint : This is the endpoint origin (ec2 ,application / network loadbalancer , elastic ip address )

Examples:

Endpoint Group 1 → us-east-1 
Endpoint Group 2 → eu-west-1


2. `Endpoint` : 

An Endpoint is the actual target resource inside the region that will receive the traffic.

+ Application Load Balancer (ALB)
+ Network Load Balancer (NLB)
+ EC2 instance
+ Elastic IP address


**DIFFERENCES BETWEEN CLOUDFRONT DISTRIBUTION AND GLOBAL ACCELERATOR** 

| Feature                           | AWS Global Accelerator                                 | Amazon CloudFront                                    |
|-----------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------|
| **Purpose**                       | Improves availability and performance of applications | Content delivery network (CDN) for distributing content |
| **Traffic Routing**               | Routes traffic to optimal endpoints via AWS network   | Caches and serves static and dynamic content from edge locations |
| **Entry Point**                   | Provides static IP addresses as entry points(multicast)| Uses a unique CloudFront URL for each distribution   |
| **Health Checks**                 | Monitors endpoint health and performs automatic failover | Not primarily focused on health checks; relies on origin health |
| **Caching**                       | No caching capabilities                                | Caches content at edge locations for faster delivery  |
| **Use Cases**                     | Suitable for global applications needing low latency  | Ideal for serving static content, APIs, and media    |
| **Static Content Delivery**       | Not designed for static content delivery               | Optimized for static content with caching mechanisms   |
| **Pricing Model**                 | Based on accelerator hours and data transfer          | Based on data transfer out, requests, and caching options |
| **Global Reach**                  | Provides global reach with improved performance        | Global distribution with edge locations worldwide     |




# Key Differences of Cloudfront Vs Global Accelerator 

### Functionality:

- CloudFront is primarily a CDN designed to cache content at various global edge locations to enhance delivery speed while 

Global Accelerator uses the AWS global network to route traffic more efficiently to application endpoints, improving performance and availability without caching.

### IP Addressing:   

- CloudFront provides a DNS name that resolves to different IP addresses depending on the geographic location of the client/customer while 
Global Accelerator uses static Anycast IP addresses that route user traffic to the nearest or most optimal AWS endpoint.

### Content vs. Traffic Optimization:

- CloudFront is more focused on content delivery—optimizing for reducing load times via caching mechanisms while 
Global Accelerator is more about optimizing the path that user traffic takes through the internet, focusing on improving application responsiveness and availability.

### Usage Scenarios:

- Use CloudFront when the primary goal is to cache content close to users to reduce web and API response times while 
Use Global Accelerator when the goal is to improve application availability and reduce latency by leveraging AWS’s global network to route user requests more efficiently.

What is the `SNOW FAMILY `

The AWS Snow Family is a collection of physical devices and services designed to facilitate the secure transfer of large amounts of data into and out of AWS. It includes devices like `AWS Snowcone, Snowball Edge, and Snowmobile`, which are ruggedized and portable to handle data migration in various scenarios, including on-premises to cloud, cloud to on-premises, and even between AWS regions. The Snow Family provides a cost-effective and efficient solution for transferring data when network connectivity is limited or when dealing with petabyte-scale datasets.

This is the smallest member of the snow family ,portable , it is good for edge computing , it has 8TB of usage storage and it can run edge computing workloads using AWS iot greengrassor AND amazon ec2 instances

`What is edge computing ? `

Edge computing is a distributed computing paradigm that brings computation and data storage closer to the location where it is needed, typically at or near the source of data generation. This approach reduces latency, improves performance, and enables real-time processing of data without relying on a centralized cloud infrastructure. Edge computing is particularly beneficial for applications that require low latency, such as IoT devices, autonomous vehicles, and real-time analytics.

**Storage options in AWS**

+  `Storage  gateway`

AWS Storage Gateway is a hybrid cloud storage service that enables on-premises applications to seamlessly use AWS cloud storage. It provides a bridge between on-premises environments and AWS, allowing you to securely store data in the cloud while maintaining low-latency access to it. Storage Gateway supports various storage interfaces, including file, block, and tape, making it suitable for a wide range of use cases such as backup and restore, disaster recovery, and data archiving.

 `Question`: You have an application on premise that you need to move to the cloud , when trying to move the application realized that the application needs some kind of re-archetecture to support cloud storage , what will you do ? 


Hybrid environment : You have cloud resources and on-premise 
cloud : everything is on the cloud 
on premise : Everything in a physical location 



**DIFFERENT TYPES OF STORAGE GATEWAYS**

1. `File Gateway` : This type of storage gateway provides a file interface to AWS cloud storage, allowing you to store and access files in Amazon S3. It is ideal for applications that require file-based storage and can be used for backup and restore, content distribution, and data archiving.

This allows you to store and retrieve files in Amazon S3 using standard file protocols like NFS and SMB. It acts like a file server, making it easy to integrate cloud storage with existing file-based applications on premise . 

- It has caching mechanism which means most recent used files are cached at the file gateway 
- Supports most of the s3 storage classes 
- Transitioning to glacier will need to be done using `life cycle policy` 
- SMB (SERVER MESSAGE BLOCK) which can be integrated with Active Directory for user authentication . 



`USE CASE` :

+ Backup & Disaster recovery : Companies can connect their application data from on premise and store the data into an s3 bucket using this approach for disaster recovery 
+ Data migration 

`Steps to Create a File Gateway`
1. Sign in to the AWS Management Console and open the Storage Gateway console.
2. Click on "Create gateway" and select "File Gateway" as the gateway type.
3. Choose the activation method (e.g., download and deploy the gateway software on a host
or use an Amazon EC2 instance).
4. Follow the prompts to configure the gateway settings, including network settings and storage options.
5. Once the gateway is activated, you can create file shares that connect to Amazon S3 buckets.
6. Configure your on-premises applications to access the file shares using standard file protocols (NFS or SMB).
`aws storagegateway create-gateway --gateway-name MyFileGateway --gateway-region us-east-1 --gateway-type FILE_S3 --activation-key your-activation-key`


2. `Tape Gateway` 
This type of storage gateway provides a virtual tape library (VTL) interface to AWS cloud storage, allowing you to store and access virtual tapes in Amazon S3 and Amazon Glacier. It is ideal for applications that require tape-based backup and archiving, providing a cost-effective and scalable solution for long-term data retention.
This allows you to use virtual tapes to store data in Amazon S3 and Amazon Glacier. It acts like a tape library, making it easy to integrate cloud storage with existing tape-based backup applications on premise .


- some companies may use physical tapes for backup but with Tape Gateway companies will use aws to eliminate the physical tape
- VTL (Virtual Tape Library) backed up by AMAZON S3 and glacier 
- It is used to backup data 
- It works with other leading backup software vendors 

# USE CASE FOR STORAGE GATEWAY 

- Backup and Disaster Recovery 
- Data Archiving 
- Hybrid Cloud Storage 
- Content Distribution 
- Data Migration 

`Steps to Create a Tape Gateway`
1. Sign in to the AWS Management Console and open the Storage Gateway console.
2. Click on "Create gateway" and select "Tape Gateway" as the gateway type.
3. Choose the activation method (e.g., download and deploy the gateway software on a host
or use an Amazon EC2 instance).
4. Follow the prompts to configure the gateway settings, including network settings and storage options.
5. Once the gateway is activated, you can create virtual tape libraries (VTLs)
and virtual tapes that connect to Amazon S3 and Amazon Glacier.
6. Configure your on-premises backup applications to use the virtual tape library for backup and arch
iving operations.
`aws storagegateway create-gateway --gateway-name MyTapeGateway --gateway-region us-east-
1 --gateway-type VTL --activation-key your-activation-key`

######################
# SQS 
######################

`What is SQS `

Amazon Simple Queue Service (SQS) is a fully managed message queuing service that enables you to decouple and scale microservices, distributed systems, and serverless applications. SQS allows you to send, store, and receive messages between software components without losing messages or requiring other services to be available. It provides a reliable, highly scalable, and secure way to manage the communication between different parts of your application, ensuring that messages are delivered in a timely manner even if some components are temporarily unavailable.


When we deploy multiple applications into the cloud environment , it can be come inevitable (unavoidable)
for them to communicate with each other  and this is true with architectures like Micro-services . 

SQS is a regional service 


# USE CASE for SQS:

+ *Application Decoupling* : For applications that need to run tasks asynchronously, you can use SQS to queue tasks that are processed by workers independently.

+ *Microservices Communications* : When multiple microservices need to interact but shouldn’t directly depend on each other, SQS provides the necessary abstraction.

+ *Load leveling*: Helps in distributing workloads to avoid overloading a particular component by storing tasks until a worker is ready to process them.

+ *its good for Batching processing* :  If you need to process large volumes of data in smaller batches, SQS can distribute these tasks efficiently.


**SQS**  : (Simple Queue Service) and its a `fully managed messaging` queuing service provided by AWS which helps in the enabling of application decoupling .It allows you to send, store, and receive messages between distributed systems, without losing messages or requiring each component to be always available.


**key features** 

+ Decoupling : Allows components to communicate asynchronously without being tightly integrated, enabling each part to operate independently.
+ Scalability: Automatically scales to handle the number of messages being processed, no matter the volume
+ Its secured : Integrated with AWS IAM, allowing you to control access to the queue and encrypt messages in transit or at rest with KMS.
+ messages can be retained : Messages can be retained in the queue for up to 14 days (default is 4 days ) 



SQS will receive messages from producers and  those messages will be processed by consumers .

what is a consumer and what is a producer in SQS ?

`Producers` are the services or applications that sends messages to SQS
`Consumers` are the services or applications that will poll (read) the messages from SQS 


| Producer Type                     | Description                                                       | Example AWS Services / Scenarios                              |
|----------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------|
| Application Servers              | Backend apps send tasks to be processed asynchronously.           | EC2 Web Server → SQS                                           |
| Microservices                    | One service sends work to another without tight coupling.         | Order Service → Billing Service via SQS                        |
| Event-Driven Systems             | Events trigger messages to queues automatically.                  | EventBridge → SQS when file lands in S3                        |
| Data Pipelines                   | Pipelines generate jobs that need background processing.          | Glue / EMR job dispatch tasks to SQS                           |
| CI/CD / Automation Tools         | Pipelines queue build/test/deploy commands.                       | Jenkins / GitHub Actions → SQS workers                         |
| IoT Devices / Edge Systems       | Devices send telemetry or processing triggers to cloud queues.    | IoT Core → SQS                                                 |
| Mobile / Web Frontend via API    | Frontend apps push tasks using API → backend → SQS.               | Mobile App → API Gateway → Lambda → SQS                        |




`Different Types of Consumers` 

| Consumer Type                    | Description                                                       | Processing Example / Pattern                                   |
|----------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------|
| Worker Servers                   | Continuously poll queue to process tasks.                         | EC2 Auto Scaling workers draining SQS                          |
| Lambda Functions                 | Automatically triggered to process messages from SQS.             | Lambda runs image/transaction processing                       |
| Microservices                    | Services read messages and perform business logic.                | Billing Service consuming "Process Payment" messages           |
| Container Workers (ECS/EKS)      | Long-running container workers poll queue for workloads.          | ECS task processing video conversion jobs                      |
| AWS Batch / Scheduled Workers    | Batch jobs periodically drain or process queue items.             | Nightly ETL job pulls SQS data                                 |
| ETL / Data Processing Systems    | Consume messages to trigger data movement / transformations.      | Glue job starts workflow from SQS messages                     |
| Notification / Gateway Services  | Convert SQS messages into external notifications.                 | SQS → SNS → Email/SMS                                          |




**TYPES OF SQS Queues**

**Standard Queue** :Offers unlimited throughput, allows at-least-once delivery, and provides best-effort ordering. It's the default queue type. 
+ Messaging order is not guaranteed 
+ It has default message retention (4 days and max of 14 days )
+ Limitation of message size 256 KB of message sent 
+ It can have duplicate messages

**FIFO Queue** : (First-in-First-Out) : Guarantees that messages are processed exactly once, in the exact order they were sent, with limited throughput
+ This means it treats the messages in the order they are delivered 
+ It has limited throughput 300 msg /s without batching 
+ It has the capability of removing duplicate messages (exactly once send capability)
+ Messages are processed in order by the consumer 



**HOW DOES THE SQS QUEUEING SERVICE WORKS** 

+ Message Lifecyle -----> A producer (sender) sends a message to the queue .Consumers (receivers) poll the queue to retreive and process the message , once a message is succesfully processed it is deleted from the qeueue 

+ It has what is called Visibility Timeout : ---> This is the amount of time the message or messages is hidden from other consumers while its being processed by one , if the message was not processed within the visibility timeout , it becomes available again 

+ Dead-Letter Queue (DLQ) : ---> Messages that fail to be processed after a configurable number of attempts can be moved to a DLQ for further inspection and troubleshooting.


**SQS -SECURITY**

+ It supports encryption in flight (https) 
+ It supports encrption at rest (SSE-KMS or SSE-SQS) key  
+ it supports client-side encryption
+ It supports users to control access using IAM policies to regulate access to the SQS api 
+ it has SQS access policies (which is similar to s3 bucket policies)



+ **Delivery delay* : This is how long a message will be held in the queue before its  delivered to the consumers 
+ **Receive message wait time* : also known as long polling, is a feature in Amazon SQS (Simple Queue Service) that controls how long a request will take for a queue to wait for messages to become available before returning a response


# SQS - Poll for Messages 

- This is when a consumer request messages from the queue 
- This is called long polling 
- It decrease number of api calls made to sqs while increase efficiency and latency of our application 
- Long polling is preferable to short polling



FIFO (First in first out)

+ Limited throughPUT for 300 msg/s without batching 3000 msg/s with batching 
+ It has a one send capability 
+ Messages from the producer are consumes in the same order by the consumer they way they were sent 
+ When creating SQS fifo the queue name must end with a .fifo e.g `class10.fifo` 




# SNS 

`What is SNS `  : Amazon Simple Notification Service (SNS) is a fully managed messaging service that enables you to decouple and scale microservices, distributed systems, and serverless applications. SNS allows you to send messages to multiple subscribers through various protocols such as HTTP/S, email, SMS, and AWS Lambda. It provides a reliable and scalable way to manage the communication between different parts of your application, ensuring that messages are delivered in a timely manner to the intended recipients.

`Note` : This is a regional service 

**Key Concepts of SNS** 

1.  `Topic` : A topic is a logical access point that acts as a communication channel for sending messages to multiple subscribers. Producers publish messages to a topic, and subscribers receive those messages based on their subscription preferences. in simple words a topic will be hopw we want to configure how messages will be send (Those who will receive the messages from sns)

+ A topic is a communication channel that acts as a hub for sending messages. Publishers (senders) send messages to the topic, and subscribers (recipients) receive those messages.
+ You can have multiple subscribers (This is where the message will be send ) (email, SMS, SQS, Lambda, HTTP endpoints) to the same topic, and each will receive messages as they are published.

2. `Publisher` : A publisher is an application or service that sends messages to an SNS topic. Publishers are responsible for creating and sending messages to the topic, which then distributes those messages to all subscribed recipients.
+ Publishers are the sources of messages in the SNS ecosystem. They create and send messages to a topic, which then distributes those messages to all subscribed recipients.
+ Publishers can be any application or service that needs to send notifications or messages to multiple recipients,
such as a web application, a backend service, or an IoT device or any aws service 

3. `Subscriber` : A subscriber is an application or service that receives messages from an SNS topic. Subscribers can choose to receive messages through various protocols such as HTTP/S, email, SMS, or AWS Lambda, depending on their preferences and requirements.
+ Subscribers are the recipients of messages in the SNS ecosystem. They receive messages from a topic based on their subscription preferences.
+ Subscribers can choose to receive messages through various protocols such as HTTP/S, email, SMS,
or AWS Lambda, depending on their preferences and requirements. This flexibility allows subscribers to receive notifications in the way that best suits their needs.

4.`Message Delivery:` : 

+ Push Notifications: You can send push notifications to mobile devices (iOS, Android, etc.).
+ SMS Messages: Send SMS messages to phone numbers.
+ Email: Send email notifications to email addresses.
+ HTTP/HTTPS Endpoints: Push messages to web servers for further processing.
+ SQS: Route messages from SNS to an SQS queue for asynchronous processing.
+ AWS Lambda: Trigger Lambda functions when messages are published to the topic

5.  `Message Fanout:`
+ SNS supports message fanout, which means that when a message is published to a topic, it can be delivered to multiple subscribers simultaneously. This allows you to easily send notifications to a large number of recipients without having to manage individual subscriptions for each recipient.
+ This enables powerful event-driven architectures where multiple systems can react to the same event.

`USE CASES FOR SNS`

+ `use for event notifications`;Application and System Alerts: Use SNS to send notifications about application events, system health, or security alerts to administrators or monitoring systems.
+ `User Notifications`: Send notifications to users about account activity, order updates, or promotional offers via email, SMS, or push notifications.
+ `Workflow Automation`: Trigger workflows or processes in response to specific events, such as a new
user registration or a completed transaction, by sending messages to AWS Lambda or other services.
+ `Fanout Messaging`: Distribute messages to multiple subscribers simultaneously, such as sending updates to  
multiple microservices or notifying different teams about a critical event.
+ `Mobile Push Notifications` Send push notifications to mobile devices. SNS supports sending platform-specific push notifications to iOS, Android, and other mobile platforms.
+ . `Broadcast Notifications`: SNS is useful for broadcasting messages to a wide range of recipients. For instance, in the event of an emergency, you can use SNS to send SMS or email notifications to a large number of people.
+ . `Decoupling your systems` : SNS is frequently used to decouple services in a distributed system. For example, when an application needs to communicate with multiple microservices, SNS can send notifications to these services without them needing to be aware of each other
+  `Application or infrastructure Monitoring`: SNS can be integrated with AWS services like CloudWatch to send real-time alerts via email, slack or SMS when certain thresholds or conditions are met (e.g., CPU utilization is too high on an EC2 instance)


**Features of SNS**

+ `Scalability` : SNS can handle a large number of messages and subscribers, making it suitable for applications of all sizes.
+ `Reliability` : SNS ensures that messages are delivered to subscribers in a reliable manner, with retries and error handling mechanisms in place.
+ `Security` : SNS integrates with AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) to control access.it also has an access policy to help control access to the SNS api 
+ `Message Filtering` : SNS allows subscribers to filter the types of messages they receive based on specific attributes. This means that not every subscriber has to receive all messages, allowing more efficient and targeted communication.
+ `Message Fanout` : SNS supports message fanout, which means that when a message is published to a topic, it can be delivered to multiple subscribers simultaneously. This allows you to easily send notifications to a large number of recipients without having to manage individual subscriptions for each recipient.
+ `Multiple Protocols` : SNS supports multiple protocols for message delivery, including HTTP/S,
email, SMS, and AWS Lambda. This flexibility allows subscribers to receive notifications in the way that best suits their needs.
+ `Integration with AWS Services` : SNS can be easily integrated with other AWS services such as
AWS Lambda, Amazon SQS, and Amazon CloudWatch, enabling powerful event-driven architectures and seamless communication between different components of your application.

`Steps to Create an SNS Topic and Subscribe to It`

There are two types of SNS topics we can create 

1. `Standard SNS topic` ; Standard SNS topics can support up to 12.5 million subscriptions per topic.
2. `FIFO SNS topic` : FIFO (First-In, First-Out) topics have a lower limit, supporting up to 100 subscriptions per topic.


1. Sign in to the AWS Management Console and open the Amazon SNS console.
2. Click on "Create topic" and choose the type of topic you want to create (Standard or FIFO).
3. Provide a name and display name for your topic, and configure any additional settings as needed
4. Click "Create topic" to create the topic.
5. After the topic is created, click on the topic name to open its details page.
6. Click on the "Create subscription" button to add a subscriber to the topic.
7. Choose the protocol for the subscription (e.g., Email, SMS, HTTP/S,  
AWS Lambda, SQS) and provide the endpoint (e.g., email address, phone number, URL, Lambda function ARN, or SQS queue ARN).
8. Click "Create subscription" to finalize the subscription.

        {
            "Effect": "Allow",

            "Effect": "Allow",
            "Action": [

            }
        },

            "Effect": "Allow",
            "Action": "sns:Subscribe",
            "Resource": "arn:aws:sns:ca-central-1:891377304437:class10-topic"
        },

            "Effect": "Allow",

        }
    ]

**AMAZON KINESIS SERVICES** 

Amazon Kinesis is a fully managed service for real-time data streaming and analytics. It allows you to collect, process, and analyze streaming data at scale, enabling you to gain insights and react to new information in real time. Kinesis provides several components, including Kinesis Data Streams, Kinesis Data Firehose, and Managed Apache Flink  old name was (Kinesis Data Analytics) , each designed to handle different aspects of the streaming data lifecycle.

1. `Kinesis Data Streams`

Kinesis Data Streams is a scalable and durable real-time data streaming service that allows you to build custom applications that process or analyze streaming data for specialized needs. It provides low-latency data processing and is ideal for use cases such as real-time analytics, log and event data collection, and streaming data pipelines. its good with social media platforms, financial transactions 


+ it has a Consumer and a producer ( Producers sends data to your kinesis data stream e.g Amazon Kinesis Agent ,AWS SDK , Amazon Kinesis Producer Library (KPL))
+ Consumer can be ( Application , client (computer ,cell phone etc ), SDK , Kinesis agents ) 
+ It has a Shard where you can save data into specific shards 
+ Partition Key ( specify if you want a particular data to be process in a particular shard )
+ It supports two capacity modes ( On-demand and Provisioned) 


**Main Features of Kinesis Data Streams**

+ Real time data processing : You can continuously ingest and process streaming data at scale.
+ Shard Management : Data streams are divided into shards, enabling parallel processing.
+ Data retention : Data can be retained for up to 365 days 
+ Integration : you can intergrate it with other aws services e.g Lambda , firehose ,s3 

**Kinesis Data Stream security**

- IAM is going to control access using iam policies
- You can enrypt data in flight using https endpoints
- server side encryption ( Encryption at rest )
- VPC endpoint available for kinesis within the vpc 
- You can monitor API calls ( data request ) using cloudtrail 


3. `Amazon Data Firehose`: 

Amazon Kinesis Data Firehose is a fully managed service that provides an easy way to reliably load streaming data into data lakes, data stores, and analytics services. It allows you to capture, transform, and deliver streaming data to destinations such as Amazon S3, Amazon Redshift, Amazon Elasticsearch Service, and Splunk. Kinesis Data Firehose automatically scales to match the throughput of your data and provides near real-time delivery of streaming data to your chosen destinations.

+ it has producers and consumers (producers , Application , client ,sdk, kinesis agent , kinesis data stream , Amazon cloudwatch.)
+ Consumers can be ( Amazon s3 , Amazon Redshifrt , Opensearch, Splunk , Datadog ) near realtime 
+ It supports different data formats , conversions , transform , compress 
+ It supports custom data transformation using lambda .
+ It can send failed data or back up data in s3 bucket 

ETL ---> extract Transform Load 


**KEY FEATURES AMAZON DATA FIREHOSE** 

+ Fully AWS Managed service 
+ Data format conversion 
+ Intergration : Supports loading data into services like Amazon S3, Amazon Redshift, Amazon Elasticsearch Service, and Splunk.
+ it supports Buffering and Compression : Supports buffering data for a specified time and compressing data to save storage.


3. **Managed Apache Flink (kinesis data analytic ):** 

This service enables you to analyze streaming data in real-time using standard SQL.AWS has made it in such away that its very easy query or request or look streaming data (information) to gain insight or understand in real time without being an expert in any programming language .


**Key Use Cases**

+ **Real-Time Analytics*: Analyze and respond to data streams in real-time, such as 
monitoring website clicks or social media interactions*.
+ **Data Ingestion*: Ingest large volumes of data from IoT devices, applications, and web logs.
+ **Machine Learning*: Stream data for real-time predictions and model training.
+ **Log and Event Data Processing*: Collect and analyze log data for operational insights.


**Amazon MQ**

Amazon MQ is a Managed Message Broker service provided by AWS.
its a managed broker service for `Apache ActiveMQ` and `RabbitMQ` that makes it easy to setup and operate message brokers in the cloud . It enables applications to communicate and exchange messages using various messaging protocols such as `JMS, AMQP, MQTT, OpenWire, STOMP, and WebSocket`.

Amazon MQ this service helps to replace on premise or existing architectures in the cloud which are already using technologies like this. It uses the Traditional Message broker replacement 

New message brokers in aws --->SQS  SNS , 

+ ActiveMQ
+ RabitMQ


On prem Message brokers 
+ IBM MQ
+ Onpremise ZeroMQ 

**Key Features** 

+ Its a Managed service in AWS : Amazon MQ manages the setup, operation, and scaling of ActiveMQ or RabbitMQ message brokers, including software updates, failure detection, and recovery.
+ Its Highly Available : It can be configured in Multi AZ 
+ its Scalable : Amazon MQ supports a range of message broker architectures, allowing scaling based on the needs of your application but not as much as SNS or SQS 
+ Its secured you can control access via IAM , encryption in transit and serverside encryption 
+ You can monitor using cloudwatch , monitoring metrics like throughput 

Re-engineering the application to use SNS , SQS  if you are already using different protocols for message brokers like MQTT etc then you can simply use a service in aws like AMAZON MQ . 


`USE CASE` : 

+ Decoupling our application : Amazon MQ is suitable for microservices architectures where different components communicate asynchronously.
+ Migrating from Existing Message brokers : If you are using on-premise ActiveMQ or RabbitMQ, Amazon MQ simplifies the migration process by providing a managed solution with the same open-source standards.




# MONITORING ( Monitoring vs Observability )

**Monitoring** is the process of collecting, analyzing, and using data to track the performance and health of an application or system. It involves setting up tools and systems to gather metrics, logs, and other relevant information to ensure that the application OR systems is running smoothly and to identify any issues that may arise.

It focuses on tracking pre-defined metrics and logs that represent the health of the system, such as:

+ CPU usage
+ Memory usage
+ Disk I/O
+ Network traffic
+ Application-specific metrics (e.g., request latency, error rates)
+ Log data (e.g., application logs, system logs)
+ Response time 

When these values exceed the thresholds you configure, the monitoring system will trigger alerts so that teams can respond quickly and fix issues before they impact users.`

Monitoring in AWS involves tracking and observing the performance, security, and overall health of your AWS infrastructure and services. It ensures that your cloud resources, such as EC2 instances, databases, VPCs, and other services, are running smoothly and securely. AWS provides various tools and services to help you monitor your environment effectively

`Interview Question` : 

`What is the difference between Monitoring and Observability ? `:

**Monitoring** is the process of `collecting and analyzing predefined metrics and logs to track the health and performance of a system to ensure the system is operating correctly. It focuses on specific indicators that represent the state of the system, such as CPU usage, memory usage, and response times. Monitoring helps identify issues based on these predefined metrics and can trigger alerts when thresholds are exceeded.

**Observability**, Observability is a broader concept that focuses on understanding the internal state of a system based on its outputs, such as logs, metrics, and traces. It is about how well you can understand what's happening inside a system without necessarily knowing all possible failure modes in advance.


with Monitoring The primary purpose of monitoring is to identify and alert on known issues. It answers the question, "Is my system behaving as expected?"

while in Observability : The goal of observability is to help you explore and investigate the system's behavior and understand unknown unknowns. It answers the question, "Why is my system behaving this way?"

Approach: Monitoring typically involves setting up alerts and dashboards to observe known metrics and logs. It relies on predetermined thresholds to notify you when something goes wrong (e.g., CPU utilization exceeds 80%).

On the otherhand , Approach: Observability focuses on collecting logs, metrics, and traces in a more holistic manner and using correlation to analyze them together. It allows for deep troubleshooting and root-cause analysis through exploratory analysis, even when a specific problem has not been anticipated.



| **Aspect**          | **Monitoring**                                          | **Observability**                                              |
|---------------------|---------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------|
| **Focus**           | Tracking known metrics and logs                         | Understanding the internal state from external outputs (logs metrics)       |
| **Purpose**         | Detecting known issues and alerting                     | Investigating unknown issues and understanding the system's state |
| **Nature**          | Reactive (alerts on predefined conditions)              | Proactive (explores behavior and correlations)                |
| **Tools**           | Dashboards, alerting systems (e.g., CloudWatch)         | Tracing tools, log aggregation, and analysis platforms        |
| **Approach**        | Based on thresholds and rules                           | Involves correlation (bringing together), context, and deeper analysis            |
| **Questions**       | "Is the system working as expected?"                     | "Why is the system behaving this way?"                        |



# CLOUD WATCH ( Monitoring and Observability)

Amazon CloudWatch is a monitoring and observability service provided by AWS that offers a comprehensive set of tools and features to help you monitor, analyze, and gain insights into your AWS resources and applications. It allows you to collect and track metrics, collect and monitor log files, set alarms, and automatically react to changes in your AWS environment.

It collects monitoring Data or operational data in the form of `logs , metrics , events` which provides a unified views in aws 

+ A primary monitoring tool in AWS, CloudWatch collects and tracks metrics, logs, and events. You can set up custom metrics, create alarms, and visualize data on dashboards.
+ It helps in monitoring resource usage (like CPU, memory, and disk space), application performance, and even custom metrics from your applications.
+ you can set CloudWatch Alarms which can trigger notifications via SNS or automate actions when thresholds are breached.
+ Cloudwatch is a regional service , if you want to monitor resources in different regions then you will need to create cloudwatch alarms in those regions.

**What is a Metrics** (`Cloudwatch Metrics`)

Metrics are numerical measurements that provide information about the performance, behavior, or state of a system or application.
Cloudwatch is going to collect and track metrics .These variables you can use to measure the perfomance of your aws resources or applications. These metrics can be CPU utilization ,disk read/write , and also custom metrics 

**There different types of Metrics**

1. `System Metrics`:These are metrics that provides information about the health of your systems /infrastructure e.g EC2 , Databases, network devices some of examples of these metrics can be ( CPU utilization , Memory usage ,Disk I/O , Network traffic)

2. `Application` : 

These are metrics that monitors the perfomance of our applications / software e.g

Response time : Time taken for your application to response to the request 
Request rate : number of request process per second by your application 
Error rates : frequency of errors occuring in your application . 

3. `Business Metrics`: 
These will provide insights about your business processes and its outcome 
e.g number of transactions etc


**What are Logs** ( `Cloudwatch Logs`)

Logs are records of events that occur within a system, application, or service. They provide detailed information about the behavior and activities of the system, which can be crucial for troubleshooting, debugging, and understanding the system's performance. CloudWatch Logs allows you to collect, monitor, and analyze log data from your AWS resources and applications.


**Different Types of Logs** 

+ System Logs
+ Application logs (access logs , transaction logs )
+ Security logs 
+ Audit logs 

 **Events**

This is a specific occurence or change in the state of a system , application , infrastructure or services .Events can represent a wide range of actions, from user interactions and configuration changes to system alerts and failures. Events are typically time-stamped and serve as a record of something significant happening within the system, often triggering automated responses or further actions. It has time stamped on the events that happens which we then term them as logs .

2. **Alarms** :

This is a feature in cloudwatch that monitors metrics and take actions by sending notifications based on specific thresholds or conditions that had been bridged e.g it can send an alarm if CPU utilization is more than 90% . The metrics here is `CPU utilization` and the `threshold 90%` 

+ Metrics:  : Metrics are the data points that CloudWatch collects from various AWS services. These can include metrics like CPU utilization, disk I/O, network traffic, or custom application metrics you publish to CloudWatch.

+ **Period* : How often cloudwatch will check the threshold to see if it has been bridge 

+ **Threshold type* :  A threshold is the condition that triggers the alarm. For example, you might set an alarm to trigger when CPU utilization exceeds 80% for a specified period.

+ *actions* : When an alarm state changes (from OK to ALARM, or from ALARM to OK), you can define actions to take. This can include sending notifications, auto-scaling actions, or executing AWS Lambda functions.

+ *threshold value* : This is the condition when bridge cloudwatch will send an alarm 

+ *Alarm States*: ( `Ok` : The metric is within the defined threshold , `Insufficient data`, there is no data to determined the state of the alarm , `Alarm state` the metrics is outside or have crossed the defined threshold)


**Setting up Actions** 

These are actions that you will want to be triggered when the alarm changes any state . some of these actions can be 

+ **Notification (You can get 3 different types of alerts --ok , alarm or insufficient data)
+ **Lambda action
+ **Auto Scaling action
+ EC2 action
+ Systems Manager action  


**Composite Alarms** :

Composite Alarms in AWS CloudWatch allow you to combine multiple CloudWatch Alarms into a single alarm. This is useful for monitoring complex conditions that involve multiple metrics or thresholds, enabling you to simplify your monitoring strategy and take action based on aggregated conditions.




3, **Logs**

1. `Log groups`

A Log Group is a collection of Log Streams that share the same retention, monitoring, and access control settings

- A log group helps you organised your log data . You can create a log for specific application or services or environments .
- You can setup retention policies . This is how long you want to keep the logs in your log group or aws account . 
- from your logs you can create a custom metrics which you can then monitor 
- Logs groups can be created by `users or by aws` . Log groups that are created by aws the log group name starts with `/aws` e.g `/aws/lambda/class6-sns-slack-lambda` you can create your own log group but the name must not start with `aws/`  
- within a log group we have what is called a log stream 

`Log Streams`: Each Log Group can contain multiple Log Streams (stream means where the logs is coming from). A Log Stream is a sequence of log events from the same source (e.g., an EC2 instance or a Lambda function).

`Contributor Insights` : 

Contributor Insights is a feature in AWS CloudWatch Logs that helps you identify and analyze the top contributors to specific log events. It provides insights into which resources, users, or applications are generating the most log activity, allowing you to focus on the most significant contributors to your logs for troubleshooting and optimization purposes.


4. **Network Monitoring** . Under cloudwatch we have the network monitoring features which has the following
Flow Monitors 
Internet Monitors
Synthetic Monitors 

+ `Flow Monitors`:

This is a feature in AWS CloudWatch that allows you to monitor and analyze network traffic flow within your AWS environment. It provides insights into the traffic patterns, source and destination of network traffic, and helps you identify potential security threats or performance issues related to network connectivity.

+ `Internet Monitors` :
This is a feature in AWS CloudWatch that allows you to monitor the availability and performance of your applications and services from the perspective of end-users across the internet. It helps you understand how your applications are performing for users around the world and identify any issues that may be affecting their experience.

+ `synthetic monitors`:
This is a feature in AWS CloudWatch that allows you to create and run synthetic tests to monitor the availability and performance of your applications and services. Synthetic monitors simulate user interactions with your applications, such as making HTTP requests or performing transactions, to ensure that they are functioning correctly and meeting performance expectations.


**Amazon Eventbridge** :

Amazon EventBridge is a serverless event bus service that allows you to connect applications using events. It enables you to ingest , filter, and route events from various sources, including AWS services, your own applications, and third-party SaaS applications.

Serverless means you do not need to provision /create or manage servers to run the service. 

+ No server management (no underlying infrastructure )
+ Automatically scale
+ Cost efficient 
+ Its an event driven architecture 
+ It has integrated security (inbuild security features lika IAM )
+ Its easy to integrate with different services especially within the aws ecosystem 

Note ; This is a regional service 

**Key Concepts of Amazon EventBridge**

1. **Events** :  

An event is a JSON object that describes a change in the state of a resource or an occurrence in your application. It contains details like the source, time, and type of the event.

2. **Event Buses** : These is were all your events will be captured .An event bus is a container for events

+ **Default Event Bus* :
They are Automatically created by aws when you create an aws account which stores all your events from that account 

+ *Custom Event Buses:* 
You can create your own event buses to handle application-specific events.

+ *Partner Event Buses*
 These are used to receive events from SaaS providers and other third-party sources.

3. **Rules :** 
Rules determine how events are processed. You can create rules to filter events based on specific criteria (e.g., event patterns) and route them to one or more targets.

4. **Targets** :

Targets are aws services or resources that responds to events 
some of these targets can be 

+ AWS lambda functions 
+ SNS topic 
+ SQS
+ Kinesis 
+ Api gateway 

5. **Event Patterns** 

Event patterns allow you to filter events based on specific attributes. You can define which events should trigger the rule by specifying conditions on attributes in the event.
