`sample code for lambda function for cloudwatch alarm creation` 

import boto3

def lambda_handler(event, context):
    # Create CloudWatch client
    cloudwatch = boto3.client('cloudwatch')

    # Correctly extract instance ID from EventBridge event for EC2 Instance State-change Notification
    instance_id = event['detail']['instance-id']
    
    # Define the alarm name and description
    alarm_name = f"CPUUtilization_Alarm_{instance_id}"
    alarm_description = f"Alarm when CPU exceeds 70% for instance {instance_id}"

    # Specify actions for different states
    action_sns_topic_arn = 'arn:aws:sns:ca-central-1:891377304437:myclass9-topic'  # Ensure this ARN is correct
    ok_actions = [action_sns_topic_arn]
    alarm_actions = [action_sns_topic_arn]
    insufficient_data_actions = [action_sns_topic_arn]

    # Create or update a CloudWatch alarm for the CPU utilization
    cloudwatch.put_metric_alarm(
        AlarmName=alarm_name,
        AlarmDescription=alarm_description,
        ActionsEnabled=True,
        OKActions=ok_actions,
        AlarmActions=alarm_actions,
        InsufficientDataActions=insufficient_data_actions,
        MetricName='CPUUtilization',
        Namespace='AWS/EC2',
        Statistic='Average',
        Dimensions=[{'Name': 'InstanceId', 'Value': instance_id}],
        Period=300,  # 5 minutes
        EvaluationPeriods=1,
        Threshold=70.0,
        ComparisonOperator='GreaterThanThreshold'
    )

    return {
        'message': f'Successfully created or updated CloudWatch alarm {alarm_name}'
    }


Other Features of Event Bridge

+ **Event Replay**: This feature allows you to replay past events that were captured by EventBridge. It is useful for testing and debugging purposes, as well as for recovering from failures.

+ **Event Archiving**: This feature allows you to store events in an archive for later retrieval and analysis. You can specify how long to retain archived events and access them when needed.

+ **Pipes**: (pipes)
EventBridge Pipes simplify the process of connecting event sources with targets. Pipes provide a more streamlined and efficient way to route events from sources to targets, reducing the complexity of managing individual rules and improving performance.


+ **Integration** under integration we have (Partner event sources , api destinations , connections ):
- Partner event sources allow you to receive events from SaaS providers and other third-party sources.
- API destinations enable you to route events to HTTP endpoints, allowing for integration with external systems and
services that are not natively supported as targets in EventBridge.
- Connections provide a secure way to manage credentials and authentication for API destinations, ensuring that your events
are sent securely to the intended endpoints.


+ **Schema Registry** ( under schame registry we have schemas )
Schemas allow you to define and manage the structure of your events in one central location. This helps ensure consistency in event formats and makes it easier to develop applications that consume events from EventBridge. You can create schemas for your events, which can then be used to generate code bindings for various programming languages, simplifying the process of working with events in your applications.



# AWS CLOUD TRIAL 
`What is Cloud Trial ?`: 

AWS CloudTrail is a service that enables you to monitor and log (capture) all API calls made within your AWS account. It provides a history of AWS API calls for your account, including the identity of the caller, the time of the call, the source IP address, and other relevant details. CloudTrail helps you track changes to your AWS resources, troubleshoot operational issues, and maintain compliance with security and governance requirements. 


Its an AWS service that records and logs every actions or event that occurs within your AWS environment . It provides a detailed audit trail for your aws resources activities , such API calls , user actions , changes to services .


+ CloudTrail is a service that enables governance, compliance, and operational and risk auditing of your AWS account.
+ When you create your aws account by default cloud trial is enabled. 
+ Cloud trail get or store history of events made from within your aws account ( console , SDK, CLI , AWS services)
+ Cloud trail logs and data from cloudtrail -  can be send into cloudwatch logs or s3 buckets . 
+ Cloud trail by default is applied to all regions by default but again you can set it to be in a single region 
+ Cloud trail should be the first service to start investigation to find out who did what in your aws account 
+ Cloud trail is a regional service 

**Importance of Cloud Trial for security & Compliance** 

+ It provides an audit trail of all API calls and actions taken within your AWS account, which is crucial for security and compliance purposes.
+ It helps you identify and investigate security incidents by providing detailed information about who did what and when
+ It allows you to monitor and enforce compliance with internal policies and external regulations by tracking changes to
resources and user activities.
+ It enables you to detect unauthorized access or suspicious activities by analyzing the logs and identifying patterns of
behavior that may indicate a security breach.
+ It helps you maintain accountability and transparency in your AWS environment by providing a comprehensive record of all
actions taken within your account, which can be crucial for audits and investigations.


## Cloud Trial Events

CloudTrail captures a wide range of events that occur within your AWS account. These events can be categorized into several types, including:

1. `Management Events`: These are events (activities) related to management of AWS resources such as creating , deleting , or modifying these resources for example like creating an ec2 instance , or modifying an s3 bucket , deleting an ec2 security groups ( These are actions performned on your aws resources)

+ By default your aws account will capture and logged (save) trails for all management events.

+ You have `read and write events` (read events will show who had access to the resources but did not edit or modify the resource) while the write event simply is creation or deleting , modifying of resources .You can seperate read events from write events 

2. `Data Events`: These are events that capture the activity on or within a resource. For example, in an S3 bucket, data events would include actions like GetObject, PutObject, and DeleteObject. In Lambda, data events would include Invoke actions.

+ By default data events are not captured or logged (saved) .These are high volume operatons for example an s3 bucket object level activities like put , delete , get objects are some of the events data events can captured or logged 

+ You can as well seperate read & write events just like the management events 
+ You can track events or data for specific resources for e.g S3 bucket





**Features of Cloudtrail**

+ `Dashboards`:
CloudTrail provides a dashboard that gives you an overview of your account activity, including recent API calls, user activity, and resource changes. It allows you to quickly identify trends and potential security issues.

+ `Event history`:
CloudTrail maintains a history of API calls and events that have occurred in your AWS account. You can view and search this event history to investigate specific actions, identify patterns, and troubleshoot issues. 


+ `insights`:

CloudTrail Insights is a feature that uses machine learning to automatically detect unusual activity in your AWS account. It analyzes the normal patterns of API calls and identifies anomalies that may indicate potential security threats or operational issues. When an anomaly is detected, CloudTrail Insights generates an event that you can investigate further.

+ `Lake ( Dashboard , Query,Event datastores & intergrations )`:
CloudTrail Lake is a feature that allows you to centralize and analyze your CloudTrail logs in a data lake. It provides a dashboard for visualizing and querying your CloudTrail data, making it easier to gain insights and identify trends in your account activity. You can create event datastores to store and manage your CloudTrail logs, and it integrates with other AWS services for further analysis and processing of your log data.


+ `Trails`:

A trail is a configuration that enables CloudTrail to capture and log events for your AWS account. You can create a trail to specify which events you want to capture, where to store the logs (e.g., S3 bucket), and how long to retain the logs. Trails can be applied to all regions or specific regions, and they can be configured to capture management events, data events, or both.


**Network Activity events**

+ Network activity events provide information about resource operations performed 
on a resource within a virtual private cloud endpoint.



**DIFFERENCE BETWEEN CLOUDWATCH AND CLOUD TRAIL** 

| Feature                     | AWS CloudTrail                                   | Amazon CloudWatch                                 |
|-----------------------------|--------------------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------|
| Purpose                     | Tracks API calls and activity in AWS accounts    | Monitors AWS resources and applications            |
| Data Type                   | Logs API activity (event history)                | Metrics, logs, and alarms                         |
| Monitoring                   | Provides insight into user activity and resource changes | Provides real-time monitoring and alerting       |
| Use Cases                   | Compliance auditing, security analysis, and troubleshooting | Performance monitoring, resource optimization, and alerting |
| Data Storage                | Stores logs in S3 buckets                        | Stores metrics in CloudWatch and logs in CloudWatch Logs |
| Event Types                 | Management events, Data events, and Insight events ,network events | Custom metrics, CloudWatch Logs, and system metrics |
| Integration                 | Integrates with AWS services for event tracking   | Integrates with AWS services for monitoring       |
| Cost Structure              | Pay for S3 storage and API calls                  | Pay for metrics, logs, and alarms                 |
| Log Retention               | Retained based on S3 bucket policy                | Configurable retention for logs                   |




# AWS CONFIG 

`What is AWS Config `:

AWS Config is a service that enables you to `assess, audit, and evaluate the configurations of your AWS resources`. It provides a detailed inventory of your AWS resources, tracks changes to those resources over time, and allows you to automate the evaluation of resource configurations against desired settings or compliance rules. AWS Config helps you maintain security and compliance by providing visibility into resource configurations and enabling you to identify and remediate non-compliant resources.it plays a very important in role monitoring , auditing , compliance and governance .

+ AWS Config is a security and compliance monitoring service that continuously tracks what resources you have in AWS and how they are configured.
+ Its a regional service 
+ You can aggregrate or accumulate the data across all regions and account 
+ There is posibility of storing the configuration data in an s3 bucket 

**Main Functionality**

1. `Resource Inventory`: AWS Config provides a detailed inventory of your AWS resources, including their configurations and relationships. It allows you to see how your resources are configured and how they relate to each other.
2. `Configuration History`: AWS Config tracks changes to your resources over time, allowing you to view the history of resource configurations and identify when changes were made.
3. `Compliance Assessment`: AWS Config allows you to define rules that specify desired configurations for your
resources. It evaluates your resources against these rules and identifies any non-compliant resources, helping you maintain security and compliance.
4. `Automated Remediation`: AWS Config can automatically remediate non-compliant resources by
triggering AWS Systems Manager Automation runbooks or AWS Lambda functions to bring resources back into compliance.
5. `Integration with AWS Services`: AWS Config integrates with other AWS services, such as AWS CloudTrail for tracking API calls, AWS CloudWatch for monitoring resource metrics, and AWS Security Hub for centralized security management.



**Importance Of AWS config for security and compliance** 

+ AWS Config provides visibility into the current and historical configurations of AWS resources, allowing you to understand the resource landscape and how it evolves (change) over time.
+ It helps in Change Tracking . With AWS Config, you can track changes to resource configurations, which helps in troubleshooting and auditing changes that might impact your environment.
+ **Its good for Compliance Management : AWS Config allows you to define rules based on best practices or compliance frameworks (e.g., CIS AWS Foundations). This ensures that your resources remain compliant with internal and external policies.
+ You can integrate with different services like SNS topics , lambda functions , Aws security hub to automate responses to compliance violations and enhance security 
+ It has snapshot & History : AWS Config provides a historical snapshot of resource configurations, enabling you to see how configurations have changed over time and revert to previous states if needed.



**HOW DOES AWS CONFIG WORKS**


+ `Resource recording` : continuously records the configuration of supported AWS resources in your account, storing these configurations in an S3 bucket.

+ `Configuration items` : Each recorded configuration is known as a configuration item (CI), which contains details about the resource, including its configuration and relationships with other resources.

+ `Configuration rules`: You can define rules that check whether your AWS resources comply with specified configurations. For example, you can create a rule to ensure that all S3 buckets have versioning enabled.
AWS Config evaluates these rules against your resource configurations and notifies you of any compliance violations.

+ `Compliance dashboard` : AWS Config provides a dashboard where you can view compliance statuses, evaluate the results of your rules, and track remediation efforts.

+ `Notification & Remediation` : AWS Config can send notifications via Amazon SNS when a resource violates a rule.
You can automate remediation actions using AWS Lambda functions to correct compliance issues automatically.

+ `Historic view of all changes` :AWS Config maintains a history of configuration changes, allowing you to view previous configurations and understand the timeline of changes made to your resources.



**Config Rules**

This is a feature in aws config that allows you to define your own desired configurations or compliance policies for your aws resouces
+ These rules helps you to `monitor , evaluate , maintain compliance in your aws environment (account)` by automatically checking on resource configurations against the predefined rules or standard 


**Types of Config Rules** 

1.`AWS Managed Rules` :These are predefines rules that comes with aws config which covers common compliance and governing scenarios 

2. `Custom Rules*` This is when you create your own custom config rules using Lambda , defining specific criteria and logic for compliance check .

`USE CASE` : This is good when you want to tailor your compliance requirements to match your organisational unique policy 


#### HOW DOES CONFIG RULES WORK 

+ Evaluation (continouly check your resources against the define rule)
+ Compliance reports  ( you can see the report to see compliant and non compliant resources )
+ Triggered action : It can trigger actions via SNS if a resource becomes non-compliant 

**NOTE** : AWS config rule does not prevent the action from happening . it has no deny option . Only evaluates and show us the resources that are non-compliant 


### Config Rules Remediations (fix)

( Steps taken to resolve non-compliant resources identified by aws config)

+ You can automate remediation of non compliant resources using SSM automation documents 
+ You can use AWS managed Automation Documents or create custom Automation Documents 
+ You can set Remediation Retries if the resources are still non compliant after auto remediation . 
+ you can setup your own custom remediation actions 


### use case for config Rules 

- security compliance 
- Operational compliance 
- Governance 

**Governance** :  ( how do you manage your organisation )

This is a framework of rules , practises , policies and processes by which organisations (team4tech solutions) is directed and controlled ( SOC Compliance ) . It establishes how decisions are made , who is responsible for making them , How authority is delegated , how organisation ensures there is accountaility .

**Compliance** :
Laws , regulations , standards and internal policies that governs an organisation operation 



Features of AWS CONFIG

+ Dashboard
+ `Conformance Packs` : A conformance pack is a collection of AWS Config rules and remediation actions that can be deployed and monitored as a single entity in your AWS account.
+ Rules - Aws Managed rules , Custom Lambda rule , Custom Rule using Guard




NETWORKING IN AWS 

**VPC - VIRTUAL PRIVATE CLOUD**

A VPC (Virtual Private Cloud) is a virtual network dedicated to your AWS account. It allows you to launch AWS resources into a virtual network that you've defined. A VPC provides you with complete control over your virtual networking environment, including selection of IP address range, creation of subnets, and configuration of route tables and network gateways.

This is a service that helps users , companies , organisation logically isolate network environments in the cloud 

1. `Isolation` : This is when you seperate your resources for security reasons using things like Subnets e.g private and public subnets 

2. `Subnets` : we have public and private subnets . a single VPC can have up to 200 subnets . There are two classifications of subnets 

+ Public Subnets : Public subnets have direct access to the internet through the `internet gateway `
+ Private subnets : Do not have direct access to the public internet , they get internet through `Nat gateway` 

3. `Routables` : Routables control how traffic goes into each subnet and each subnet must be associated to a routables . Any subnet that receives traffic through the public routable is a public subnet . The public routables gets its internet or traffic  through the internet gateway and any subnet that gets its internet through the private route table is a private subnet because all the traffic comes through the Natgateway 

4. `Internet Gateway (IGW)` : It allows internet into your VPC that is your resources can communicate to the internet once an internet gateway is attached


5. `Nat Gateway (Network Address Translation gateway`) : It allows resources in the private subnet to be able to send outbound traffic to the internet while preventing any inbound traffic that is not accepted. 


6. `Network Access Control List (ACL)` : This is a layer of security at the subnet level that acts as firewall that controls inbound and outbound traffic into your subnets 

7. `Security Groups` : This is a layer of security at the instance level that acts as a firewall that controls inbound and outbound traffic to your instances . Security groups are stateful while network ACLs are stateless


**UNDERSTANDING CIDR ( Classes inter-domain routing)**

`What is an IP address ?` : An IP address (Internet Protocol address) is a unique number assigned to a device connected to a network, such as the internet. It acts like a home address for your device, allowing data to be sent to and received from the correct destination.

`Different Types of IP Addresses `

IPV4 & IPV6 

Example

IPv4 address: 192.168.1.1
IPv6 address: 2001:0db8:85a3:0000:0000:8a2e:0370:7334

`IPV4 `
+ Most common format 
+ This was the first version of ip addresses 
+ Four numbers seperated by dots 

`IPV6 `

+ Newer version with a much larger address space.
+ Uses numbers and letters separated by colons.

Example: 2001:db8::1

In your house you have internet , this internet was gotten from an Internet Provider (MTN ,Orange , Bell , Rogers , Telus etc )
Modem () ----> Ip address space ---> connect devices to the internet , these devices use IP Address e.g of these devices can be ( TV , Smart Fridge , Smart Dish washer , Lap tops , Moble phones )



**What are some of the things you should keep in mine when creating a VPC**

+ Identify requirements: determine the number of subnets needed , host (resources that will use the network) , anticipate for future growth
+ Ensure when creating multiple vpc's each vpc CIDR should be Unique to avoid cidr overlapping 
+ Use VPC cidr block between /16 or 26 bits 
+ Subnet Design Matters (Per AZ, Per Function)

Design subnets based on:
AZ separation (HA)
Function: Public, Private-App, Private-DB
Future expansion
Example:
Public: 10.0.1.0/24
App private: 10.0.2.0/24
DB private: 10.0.3.0/24
+ High Availability 
+ Logging & Monitoring (Turn On Flow Logs)

**Definition of Key Terms**


1. we need to determined the host range (this is simply the number of ip addresses the host need to share  resources within the network)

`with IPV4 We have Bits` e.g 10.0.0.0


`what is a bits` (binary digit) : These are fundamental units of data or basic data in computing or networking . This is a contraction of "binary digits" they are the smallest pieace of information a computer can handle .Its simply  building blocks of data, represented as 0s and 1s.

+ Bits are used in binary code, which is the language that computers use to process data. Multiple bits are combined to represent more complex information.



`what is an IPV4?` : IPv4 (Internet Protocol version 4) is the fourth version of the Internet Protocol, which is used to identify devices on a network using an address.

+ An IPv4 address consists of 32 bits, divided into four groups (called octets) separated by dots. For example: 192.168.111.029 or 10.0.0.0

+ Each octet can have a value from 0 to 255, meaning an IPv4 address can represent approximately 4.3 billion unique addresses.  



`What is IPv6?` : 

 IPv6 (Internet Protocol version 6) it is the successor to IPv4, designed to address the limitation of IPv4 addresses.

 + An IPv6 address consists of 128 bits, written in eight groups of four hexadecimal digits, separated by colons. For example: `2001:0db8:85a3:0000:0000:8a2e:0370:7334`.
 + This means IPv6 can support almost limitless number of unique addresses (over 340 undecillion addresses).



 `What is a CIDR` :  CIDR (Classless Inter-Domain Routing) is a method for allocating IP addresses and IP routing. It uses a CIDR notation to specify IP address ranges.
+ A CIDR block is written as an IP address followed by a slash and a number (e.g., `192.168.1.0/24`). The number indicates how many bits are used for the network portion of the address



**How Can we calculate cidr range for our VPC and subnets**

VPC -- needs a cidr range and each subnet in the VPC will also need a Cidr range 


1. `Understanding the network structure `

An ip address is split into two parts : `network part & host part` 
`network part` identifies specific network while the host part identifies individual devices within the network e.g your home devices like ipads , laptops , tv,mobile

`Network Part`

This portion identifies the specific network you belong to.

`Example` : Your house has one Wi-Fi network, and every device connected to it belongs to that network.
The network part is what your router uses to identify:

“This device belongs to my home network.”

Examples of network identifiers:
Your home network: 192.168.0.0
Your company network: 10.0.0.0
Your AWS VPC: 10.0.0.0/16

`Host Part` 
This identifies individual devices on the network.
Examples of host devices in a home network:

+ Laptops
+ Phones
+ Smart TVs
+ iPads
+ Gaming consoles
+ Cameras

**Advantages of creating a Custom VPC (Virtual Private Cloud)** 

| Custom Vpc  (why)                                             | Default VPC                                   
|---------------------------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------
| Network Seperation or segmentaion (Public + Private subnets)   | By default aws places your ec2 instance in a public subnet                            
| Security control (custom security groups , ACL & Secure routes)| AWS by default configure your routing policies and acl for you                 
| control over IP ranges ,network size and ip overlaps           | No control over network size or ip ranges or to avoid ip overlapping
| you can have multiple subnets ( control of more subnets)       | No control over the number of subnets being created 
| You have full control over routables (customization )          | Default routes 
| You will be charge for a custom VPC                            | Free

vpc more - it creates a vpc and other resources e.g subnets , nat gateway , NACL , internet gateway etc automatically

VPC CIDR : 10.0.0.0/16 

what will be our subnet cidr



## Routables 

+ This usually refers to your network traffic .
+ This network traffic is managed within your vpc using what we called routable
+ In AWS your public subnets will get their internet from send through the Public Routables while
+ The private subnets will get their  internet (network traffic )  sent from the private routables you need to associate (link or join ) the subnets to these routables  
+ You need to associate the subnets to your routables e.g you will associate the public routable to your public subnets and private subnets to your private routables 
+ By default when you create a VPC it comes with One routable ( rename to pubblic routable)
+ create a private routables separately 
+ you have to edit routes , This helps to tell the routable where it gets its network from and where it sents the network to e.g for the public routables they will receive their network from the internet gateway and the private routables receives their internet from the Nat Gateway 



## Internet Gateway 

+ This gives internet to your VPC ( i.e) it allows your resources (EC2)
to connect to the internet .
+ Internet Gateways can scale horizontally and they are highly available ,reduntant 
+ If you are creating a custom VPC the internet gateway must be created seperately from the VPC and then attached to the VPC . 
+ Routables must be edited 


## Nat Gateway ( Network Address Translation Gateway)
+ This enables instances in the private subnet to receive inbound traffic and and send out ( Outbound traffic ) 
+ you need to allocate static ip address 

### Outbound Traffic : 
Instances in the private subnets usually dont have public ip address & can not directly communicate with the public internet .
The Natgateway translates the private ip addresses to a public ip address before forwarding the traffic to the internet .

### Inbound Traffic : 

In contrast to the internet gateway , the Nat gateway does not allow inbound traffic iniated from the internet to reach instances in the private subnet . It has extra layer of security where it hides your private ip address from your ec2 instances from direct exposure to the internet 

## Nat Instance :

+ This is simply ec2 instance configured to help in Routing traffic to your private subnets 
+ You are responsible is managing unlike the Nat Gateway 


## NACL : Network Access Control List  and Security Groups 

+ This enhance security at the subnet level : NACL acts as a firewall controlling traffic in and out of your subnets within your VPC .
+ One NACL per subnet , new subnets are assigned to the default NACL and the default NACL accepts everything inbound and outbound 
+ When you create a VPC it comes with a default NACL and you can create your own NACL .
+ You can define your rules .
+ Newly created NACL will deny everything.
+ Stateless : When there is an inbound traffic rules this applies seperately from outbound traffic rules .Both inbound and outbound rules applies independently

3 - Create an internet gateway and attached to your VPC 
4 - Create a Natgateway 
5 - Create a routable and name it private routable. since our vpc when created comes with a default rouatble you can rename or create and call it public routable

**Methods to Login into our instances in the private subnet**



1. `Bastion Host  (Agent forwarding or ssh key pairs)`

**Agent forwarding :** 

+ This approach is great if you actually created the ec2 instances and you have the keypairs saved on your local machine 
+ This will work if the keypair for both instances or all instances are the same . 

**Steps to use ssh agent**

+ Start the ssh agent on your local machine by running the following command  `eval "$(ssh-agent -s)"`
+ Add your ssh-keys to the ssh-agent  `ssh-add <keypairname>` (makesure you are at the location where your key is )  or `ssh-add /path/to/bastion-key` 
+ Ssh to the bastion host with agent forwarding command  `ssh -A  <username>@bastion-public-ip` e.g `ssh -A ubuntu@645.678.8968` this will log you into the bastion host 
+ From the bastion host you can run the following cammand to login into your instance in the private subnet `ssh <username@private-instance-ip>` e.g `ssh ubuntu@935.495.649`

**Keypairs**

+ Create an authentication between your local and the bastion host e.g generate ssh keys from your local , copy the public key and add inside the bastion authorized_key inside the .ssh directory in the bastion host . 
+ Add an iam role to give you access to login into the ec2 instance in the private subnet 
+ Create a connection from your bastion to the server in the private subnet by generate a public key e.g ssh-keygen on the bastion , copy the public key and paste inside the server in private authorized_keys in the .ssh directory . 
+ Login into the bastion host e.g ssh `<username@bastion-public-ip>` and from the bastion you can now ssh into the server in the private subnet `username@bastion-public-ip`


2. `VPN (Client VPN)`

3. `Session Manager` 






**NACL (Network Access Control List)**

These are virtual firewalls that helps to protect the inbound and outbound traffic at the subnet level 
A NACL is a stateless firewall that controls inbound and outbound traffic at the subnet level.



## NACL : Network Access Control List  and Security Groups 

+ This enhance security at the subnet level : NACL acts as a firewall controlling traffic in and out of your subnets within your VPC .
+ One NACL per subnet , new subnets are assigned to the default NACL and the default NACL accepts everything inbound and outbound 
+ When you create a VPC it comes with a default NACL and you can create your own NACL .
+ You can define your own rules .
+ Newly created NACL will deny everything.
+ NACL are Stateless in nature: When there is an inbound traffic rules this applies seperately from outbound traffic rules .Both inbound and outbound rules applies independently 



KEY POINTS TO NOTE 

+ Subnet-level firewall
+ Stateless → rules must be added for both inbound and outbound
+ Uses numbered rules (lowest number wins)- highest priority 
+ You can set NACL to Allow or Deny Traffic
+ Default NACL that comes with your vpc at the time of creation → Allow all inbound + outbound
+ Custom NACL → DENY all until you add rules


# DIFFERENCE BETWEEN A SECURITY GROUP AND NACL 

| NACL    (why)                                                            | Security Group                                  
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------
|Operates at the Subnet level ( Security firewalls at subnet level)        | The operate at EC2 level ( firewall at ec2 instance level)                           
|NACL are stateless ( they examine inbound & outbound rules independentl)  | security groups are stateful what gets in automatically gets out                 
|They support `allow and deny rules `                                       | supports allow rules only 
|rules are evaluated in order (lowest to highest) deciding to allow traffic| All rules are evaluated before deciding to allow traffic or not 





**VPC PEERING**

What is VPC Peering 

VPC (Virtual Private Cloud) Peering is a private networking connection between two VPCs, allowing them to communicate as if they were part of the same network.


`KEY POINTS TO NOTE`

+ Its a private connection --> Traffic does not go over the internet 
+ No VPN or Direct connect is needed for this setup 
+ Its a one to one relationship connection ( Not transitive )
+ Its supports Same region peering , Cross region peering and Cross Account Peering
+ When peering connection is setup routables must be updated on both VPC for it to work 
+ DNS resolution can be enabled with DNS hostnames + Peering DNS settings


**USE CASE**

+ Share services across different VPC'S E.g your application server in another vpc and your database in another vpc 
+ Multi Region High Availability 
+ Data Sharing
+ This is good for Hybrid Architecture 


**Note** : When creating a VPC make sure the cidr blocks are completely different to avoid instances where you might want to peer the vpcs and you are faced with CIDR overlapping e.g   `VPC A` has cidr : `10.0.0.0/16` and `VPC B` has cidr `10.0.0.0/16 `this will cause a cidr conflict in this case peering will not be able to work .



**HOW VPC PEERING ACTUALLY WORKS (FLOW )**

Assume we have two VPCS name VPC-A and VPC B

1. VPC A sends a peering request 
2. VPC B accepts the request 
3. Then you add routable entries in both VPC - by inter-changing them for example u take the vpc a cidr and as a route to vpc b and vice versa
4. Your instances or resources can now communicate with each other privately 



Project vpc cidr : 172.31.0.0/16
class vpc cidr : 10.0.0.0/16

edit routes for both vpc 


Requester : This is the VPC that will send a peer request to the other VPC e.g in our case we will use VPC A to send a request to VPC B . 
Accepter(peer vpc)  : This is the VPC that accept or rejects the request 


1. Same region Peering 
2. Cross Region Peering 
3. Cross Account Peering

# VPC ENDPOINTS 

A VPC Endpoint is an AWS networking feature that allows you to privately connect your VPC to supported AWS services such as:

+ S3
+ DynamoDB
+ SNS
+ SQS
+ Secrets Manager
+ Systems Manager (SSM)
+ And many others (over 100+ services)

Traffic stays inside AWS and does NOT go through the public internet.
That means no Internet Gateway (IGW), no NAT Gateway, and no public IPs are required to access these services.


**Types of VPC ENDPOINTS** 

In aws there are 2 type of VPC endpoints 

1. **Interface Endpoint ( Powered by AWS private link)**

*USE CASE* : The interface endpoint allows you to connect to AWS services like EC2 , S3 ,DynamoDB , and many other aws services

+ These are VPC interfaces that are created in your VPC with private ip addresses 
+ It creates an ENIO(Elastic Network Interface) insde your subnets 
+ The traffic between your VPC and the service stays within the amazon network 
+ This endpoint supports aws managed and customer managed services 
+ AWS charges per hour for endpoint and per GB of data processing 



2. **Gateway Endpoint**

USE CASE : Its used only to connect `S3 and Dynamodb` 

+ This is an entry point for traffic within a VPC that leads to these services 
+ Routables are modified automatically by aws to ensure traffic to theses services are directed to the endpoint 
+ It only supports or available for Amazon S3 and DynamoDB
+ Free to use 


**Benefits of using Endpoints (VPC ENDPOINTS)**

1. `To improves security` : 

This prevents your traffic to go through the public internet , thus reducing exposure to external threats or attacks .

2. `Reduce Latency: `

Since resources are already within a private aws network , it will reduce latency unlike the traffic going through the public before coming to our aws network .

3. `Its cost effective` :

Our endpoints have charges , but it can be more cost efficient compared to transfering data especially large data over the internet which will cost data transfer charges .







**VPC Flow Logs**

VPC flow logs capture information about the IP traffic going to and from network interfaces in your Virtual Private Cloud (VPC). Flow logs provide valuable or good insights into network traffic patterns, security analysis, troubleshooting, and compliance monitoring.

This captures inbound traffic (in ) and outbound traffic (out) in your VPC and subnets 

+ It captures Logs for your VPC
+ It can capture subnet flow logs 
+ ENI ( Elastic Network Interface) flow logs



## Key Features of VPC flow logs 

- Captures traffic data (inbound and outbound traffic)
- You can customize specific filters on what to capture e.g accepted traffic (this is traffic that was allowed into your vpc), rejected (this is request that was rejected to get into your vpc ), or all (both accpeted and rejected )
- You can intergrate VPC flow logs with other AWS services e.g you send logs to cloudwatch logs or s3 bucket . You can intergrate with Lambda function that can response to specific traffic patterns.

+ Log Group : This is how you will store your logs. The logs are stored in Amazon CloudWatch Logs or Amazon S3 (depending on your configuration)
+ Log Stream : Each VPC flow log consists of log streams that contain records for each network interface (ENI) being monitored.
+ Log record : Each log record contains information about a single network flow.



## Benefits of VPC flow Logs 

1. `Security Monitoring` 

+ Helps in detecting suspicious network activity, such as unauthorized access attempts or malicious traffic.
+ Its good for monitoring your network and trouble shoot network connectivity errors
+ Its good for compliance ( SOC Compliance )
+ Identify unauthorized access attempts
+ Investigate incidents

2. `Improved Network Troubleshooting`

- it helps in Diagnosing connectivity issues
- You can pinpoint which security groups or network ACLs may be blocking traffic by seeing this through a vpc flowlogs
- Performance monitoring like latency issues etc 
+ VPC flogs can answers questions like : Why cant my ec2 instance connect to my RDS , why is my lambda timing out , why is my loadbalancer showing a 504 error 


3. `It can help in Auditing `

+ You can maintain a record of who accessed your network and how, which helps in auditing and compliance purposes.


**Where can you send VPC flow logs for storage** 

*Destinations is where you can send your flow logs*

+ Send to `CloudWatch Logs`
+ Send to an `Amazon S3 bucket`
+ Send to `Amazon Data Firehose` in the same account
+ Send to `Amazon Data Firehose` in a different account




### DEMO SENDING VPC FLOWS LOGS TO Cloudwatch Logs

- You need to create a cloudwatch log group 
- Create a Iam role to grant permission for your vpc flowlogs to write inside your cloudwatch log group

                    "aws:SourceArn": [
                        "arn:aws:logs:ca-central-1:891377304437:*",
                        "arn:aws:logs:us-east-1:891377304437:*"
                    ]
                }
            }
        }
    ]
}


### Intergreting the default aws Logs Format inside  Log group 

 2 891377304437 eni-0b6f05bf71bc89078 162.216.150.90 10.0.1.144 53901 9558 6 1 44 1781229233 1781229248 ACCEPT OK

2 891377304437 | eni-0af016a896bf3a6c6 | 79.110.62.73 |  10.2.33.29 | 43632 | 25690 | 6 |  1 |  40 | 1716047782|  1716047794 | ACCEPT | OK

+ 2 = Entry ID 
+ 891377304437 = aws account I.D 
+ eni-0af016a896bf3a6c6 = The network interface ID (ENI) which is associated with the network traffic
+ 79.110.62.73 = Source ip address (where is the traffic coming from)
+ 10.2.33.29 = Destination IP ( where is the traffic going to )
+ 43632 = Source Port number ( the port of the ip address where the traffic is coming from)
+ 25690 = Destination port ( where the traffic is going to port )
+ 6 = The protocol Type (6 indicates TCP)
+ 1 = This is the number of Packets transfered or flagged 
+ 40 = The number of bytes transfered (Packet size or byte count) e.g 40 bytes
+ 1716047782 = The start of the captured window which is Timestamp or session ID ( Unix timesstamp for unix-like system) start time
+ 1716047794 = This is the end of the capture window (Unix timesstamp for unix-like system) end time 
+ ACCEPT = means the network traffic was accepted into our vpc 
+ OK = (results of the action) logging status. other logging status can be No data , Skipdata or Denied

{
  "Version": "2012-10-17",
  "Statement": [
    {
      "Sid": "AllowVPCFlowLogsPutObject",
      "Effect": "Allow",
      "Principal": {
        "Service": "vpc-flow-logs.amazonaws.com"

      "Condition": {
        "StringEquals": {
          "aws:SourceAccount": "YOUR-AWS-ACCOUNT-ID"
        },
        "StringLike": {
          "s3:prefix": "AWSLogs/YOUR-AWS-ACCOUNT-ID/elasticloadbalancing/"
        }
      }
    }
  ]
}




# AWS SITE to SITE VPN 


 AWS SITE to SITE VPN 

This is a fully managed service that establishes secure connections between your on-premises networks or branch offices and your AWS resources using IP Security (IPSec) tunnels. This service enables easy or stress free or seamless integration of your existing network infrastructure at your office with AWS, facilitating secure data transfer and communication


`KEY Features `?

+ `Its Highly Available` : Each VPN connection includes two tunnels across multiple Availability Zones, ensuring redundancy and continuous availability.

+ `Security `: Utilizes IPSec protocols to encrypt data in transit, maintaining confidentiality and integrity between your on-premises network and AWS

+ `Perfomance Optimization` : For globally distributed applications, the Accelerated Site-to-Site VPN option leverages AWS Global Accelerator to route traffic through the nearest AWS edge location, enhancing performance by reducing latency


`USE CASE `: 

+ Secure connection between your AWS resources and On premise location 
+ Application Migration 
+ Hybrid Environment ( AWS and on premise communication)
+ Data Migration ( for on premise data transfer to aws and vice versa)
+ Disaster Recovery (Secure connection to replicate data in aws and vice versa)
+ Remote Workforce ( This can grant secure access for employees working from home (remote empoyees)


**How to Setup Site to Site VPN** 

+ Virtual Private Gateway (VGW) or Transit Gateway
+ Customer Gateway (CGW) 


1. Create a Customer Gateway : Define your on-premises device or software application that will connect to AWS.
2. Create a Target Gateway : Choose between a Virtual Private Gateway or a Transit Gateway on the AWS side.
3. Configure Routing : Set up routing so that traffic between your on-premises network and AWS resources flows through the VPN connection.
4. Establish the VPN connection : Create the VPN connection using the AWS Management Console, AWS CLI, or API, and configure your customer gateway device accordingly.

How this works 


1. `Customer Gateway (CGW)` --> This is your side (on-prem, office, or Azure/GCP).

`Examples of a CGW:`

+ Your hardware firewall (Fortinet, Cisco, Palo Alto, etc.)
+ Your router
+ A virtual appliance
+ Azure VPN Gateway (if connecting AWS ↔ Azure)

CGW holds your:

+ Public IP
+ Routing options
+ BGP ASN (if dynamic routing)


2. `VGW : Virtual Private Gateway` ---This is AWS side of the VPN.

+ This is VPN connector on the aws side of the VPN connection 
+ VGW is created and attached on the vpc from which you want to create the site to site vpn on 
+ It Receives encrypted traffic
+ Decrypts it
+ Sends it inside the VPC



3. `VPN Connection`

This is the actual encrypted IPSec tunnel.
AWS automatically creates 2 tunnels for high availability:
Tunnel 1 is active and the primary tunnel 
Tunnel 2 is a standby backup or failover 

If tunnel 1 fails, tunnel 2 takes over.


**CUSTOMER GATEWAY** 

+ This is a physical device or software application this should be on the side of the Customer VPN ( on premise )
+ From the customer gateway device you can use the public ip to route traffic 



## AWS VPN Cloudhub 

+ it provides secure comminication between multiple sites if you have multiple vpn connections
+ it has a low cost hub and spoke model 
+ With this you can connect multiple VPN on a singe VGW (Virtual private gateway)


# AWS CLIENT VPN 

AWS Client VPN is a managed service that enables you to securely connect your users or devices to your Amazon Web Services (AWS) infrastructure and on-premises networks using a Virtual Private Network (VPN). It provides an encrypted connection over the internet, ensuring secure communication between clients and AWS resources or private networks.

HOW IT WORKS 

1. VPN endpoint : 
2. Authentication : You can configure actve directory , SSO , SAML 
3. Route Traffic 
4. Authorization Access 
5. Clent connections

+ This helps for employees working from home to securely connect on an ecrypted network to our resources in aws (private subnet)
+ Hybrid Cloud Connectivity : This can also lead to secure connection between on premise , cloud and remote users 
+ Data protection : This will ensure sensitive data is encrypted while in transit between users and your aws resources . 
+ Your engineers need SSH/RDP access to private subnets.


VPN ----Virtual Private Network ---> Encrypted 



# DIRECT CONNECT DX 


AWS Direct Connect (DX) is a `dedicated, private, physical network connection between your on-premises data center and AWS`.

+ A dedicated connection must be setup to route traffic using Direction Connect which uses an AWS Direct connection locations . 
+ You need to setup a Virtual Private Gateway (VGW) on your VPC 
+ Direct connects helps you to access public resources s3 and private resources like EC2 instances .
+ Direct connect supports IPV4 and IPV6

**Key Features** 

+ Consistent Network Perfomance 
+ Higher Bandwith Options 
+ Enhanced security when compared to site to site vpn 


## USE CASE 

+ Enhanced Security : It provides a private and dedicated network for you to connect from your on premise to the cloud.
+ Its is good for Hybrid environment ( connect your on premise to aws cloud )
+ Compliance reasons 
+ To increase bandwith throughput when using large data sets to lower cost 
+ Consistent network perfomance ( dedicated connection it reduces latency)
+ It improves reliability with aws (SLA) Service Level Agreement ensuring high availability and uptime 
+ Data transfer and Migration 

## Direct Connect , Connection Types 

+ Dedicated Connections 
+ Hosted Connections ( aws direct connect partners , capacity can be added and removed on demand )

## Direct Connect Enryption 

+ Data in transit is not encrypted but it is private 
+ You can use Direct Connect + VPN  to provide ipsec enryption  and its good for extra layer of security

**TRANSIT GATEWAY**  



It simplify and centralize connectivity across multiple Amazon VPCs (Virtual Private Clouds) and on-premises networks

This is a service in aws that is a network transit hub you can interconect your vpcs within ur aws account and also with your on premise network . It acts as a central hub which helps to simplify the management of network connections

+ Its a regional resource and can work with cross region and also account to account sharing 
+ You can use it across different aws accounts using RAM ( Resource Access Manager)
+ You can control the routes by determine which vpc can speak to which .
+ It can work with other services Direct Connect , VPN connections
+ It supports IP multicast 

Main Features of Transit Gateway 

+ Centralized Hub for connectivity
+ Scalable 
+ It simplifies routing 
+ Increase security with isolation 
+ Network seperation 
+ Inter-region 


AWS Transit Gateway is a central network hub that connects:

+ Multiple VPCs
+ On-prem networks (VPN / Direct Connect)
+ Other AWS accounts
+ Other AWS regions (via TGW Peering)


          +--------------------+
          |  Transit Gateway   |
          +---------+----------+
                    |
   +----------------+----------------+
   |                |                |
 VPC1             VPC2             VPC3


## DIFFERENCE BETWEEN VPC-PEERING AND TRANSIT GATEWAY 

| VPC PEERING                                                   | TRANSIT GATEWAY                              
|---------------------------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------
|its a way of connecting 2 or more vpc together                 | allows you to connect 2 or more vpc together & u can connect on premise                         
|It does not support transitive routing                         | Its supports transitive routing              
|less scalable because u need individual connections            | This is scalable 
|it supports simple routing with routables associated with each vpc| supports centralized routing with routables that can be associated with different vpc
|Good for small simple networking                               | more complex networking 


Why use TGW?

+ Avoid VPC peering limits
+ Improve scalability
+ Connect 100s of VPCs
+ Connect multi-account environments
+ Connect on-prem via VPN & Direct Connect
+ Centralize routing & security
+ Reduce complexity



**Hands on for Region to Region Transit Gateway Setup**

+ Cross region connecitvity

Documentation of setting a Transit Gateway for Cross Region 

We have two VPC one in Ca-central-1 and Eu-west-2 

VPC-A is ca-central   while VPC-B is eu-west-2 

`Step 1`

1. **In Region A (e.g ca-central-1 )**

+ Create a Transit Gateway 
+ Create a transit gateway attachment for VPC-A and the the attachment type select `VPC`
+ Create a another transit gateway attachment this time select Peering connection as the type
+ Edit routables ( take the cidr from vpc-B and add to the private on vpc selecting transit gateway as the target)
+ Edit Transit Gateway routables in this region by clicking on transit gateway routables ,select the routables and click add `static route` add the cidr from VPC B and select peering connection as transit gateway . 

`step 2`

2. **In Region B (e.g eu-west-2)**

+ Create a Transit Gateway 
+ Create a transit gateway attachment and the attachment type is VPC 
+ Accept the peering Attachment request that was sent or iniated by VPC-A 
+ Edit routables (private routables or public ) by adding your VPC-A cidr and selecting Transit Gateway as target 
+ Edit transit gateway routables in this region by adding a static route using VPC-A cidr and selecting Peering connection as the target .

***SAME REGION TRANSIT GATEWAY SETUP**

STEP 1 — Create Transit Gateway & Attach VPCs

1. Create a Transit Gateway

+ Go to VPC Console → Transit Gateways → Create Transit Gateway
+ Use defaults unless enterprise requirements specify otherwise
+ Leave DNS and auto-propagation enabled

2. Create a VPC Attachment for VPC-A

+ Go to Transit Gateway Attachments → Create attachment
+ Attachment type: VPC
+ Select VPC-A
+ Select at least one subnet per AZ

Create attachment

3. Create a VPC Attachment for VPC-B

+ Repeat the same steps
+ Attachment type: VPC
+ Select VPC-B
+ Select subnets

Create attachment

4. Attach both VPCs to the Transit Gateway

TGW ←→ VPC-A
TGW ←→ VPC-B

🟩 STEP 2 — Configure Routing (TGW + VPC Route Tables)
1. Add Routes in the Transit Gateway Route Table

Go to Transit Gateway → Route Tables and verify:

Destination	Attachment

VPC-A CIDR	Attachment-A
VPC-B CIDR	Attachment-B

If not present:

Add route for each CIDR manually

2. Update VPC Route Tables for VPC-A

+ Go to VPC-A Route Tables (private)

Add route:

+ Destination: VPC-B CIDR
+ Target: Transit Gateway

3. Update VPC Route Tables for VPC-B

+ Go to VPC-B Route Tables (private)

Add route:

+ Destination: VPC-A CIDR
+ Target: Transit Gateway

🟧 Final Result

### Egress Only internet Gateway 

egress = outbound traffic 
ingress = inbound trafic 

It allows only outbound communication over IPV6 from your instances within your VPC to the internet .It prevents the internet from initiating an inbound connection to your ec2 instances within your VPC . 

An Egress-Only Internet Gateway (EIGW) is an AWS service designed to enable IPv6-only instances in a Virtual Private Cloud (VPC) to connect to the internet for outbound traffic while ensuring that no inbound traffic is allowed from the internet. It is specifically tailored for IPv6 traffic, as IPv6 does not use NAT (Network Address Translation) like IPv4 does

`USE CASE `: 

+ You might have instances in the private subnets that needs access to the internet based on services e.g an instance needs updates , accessing external api , secrets , ) without exposing themselves to inbound internet traffic 
+ Compliance and Security Requirements .Some companies or organisations have strict security or complaince policies which may require outbound-only access for ipv6

ingress : this means inbound traffic 
egress : this means outbound traffic

# SERVERLESS TECHNOLOGY 
##############################

serverless technology or computing .This is service in aws that allows users (developers , devops ,cloud) to build and run applications without having to manage the `underlying infrastructure` . AWS will handle the task of provisioning , scaling and maintenance ,while enabling users to focus on solely writing the code for their application or service . This approach offers automatic scaling, high availability, and a pay-for-use billing model, enhancing agility and optimizing costs.


Serveless simply means --> You dont manage servers , all you need to is write the code and the cloud is going to run it for you 
You dont worry aboout 
+ servers 
+ Operating systems
+ scaling 
+ Patching 
+ Capacity planning 


`Interview Questions` : What is your experience with serverless Technologies 


**Key Services in AWS that uses serverless Architecture (technology or computing)**

1. AWS Lambda (Functions)
2. Amazon API gateway (Serverless API)
3. AWS Fargate 
4. Amazon DynamoDB (serverless NoSql Database)
5. AWS step functions 
6. Amazon Eventbridge 
7. AWS Appsync 
8. SNS & SQS 
10. Kinesis (Amazon data firehose , Kinesis Data stream , Kinesis analytics )
11. Elastic Cache
13. AWS cognito 

***Benefit of using Serverless instead of Server services like EC2 instance**

1. No server management : No operational overhead , No need to setup (patching servers , scale) allowing you to focus on the code 
2. Scalabiility there is automatic scaling : Serverless applications scale automatically in response to demand, ensuring optimal performance during traffic spikes or lulls.
3. Its cost efficient. It has the pay per use model.You only pay for what you use. Costs are incurred based on actual execution time and resource consumption, reducing expenses on idle infrastructure.
4. It is highly available.Serverless platforms are designed with high availability and fault tolerance, ensuring uptime and reliability.
5. Its event driven.Serverless platforms are ideal for event-driven workloads, responding instantly to triggers such as HTTP requests, database changes, or file uploads.



# AWS LAMBDA 

AWS Lambda is a serverless compute service that allows you to run code in response to events without provisioning or managing servers. It automatically scales applications by running code in response to each trigger or event and manages the compute resources required to run the code.


**Common Use Cases for Lambda functions** 

CI/CD pipeline application
Serverless  application 


1. *Task Automation* : Automate operational tasks like starting/stopping EC2 instances or cleaning up resources ami images .
2. *File processing* : Automatically process files uploaded to S3, such as resizing images or transcoding videos.
3. *Real time notification:* :  Trigger notifications or alerts using Amazon SNS or other communication services to slack 
4. *API Backend:* Build serverless APIs using Amazon API Gateway and AWS Lambda.
5. *Data Transformation : Perform real-time data transformation, such as streaming data processing from Amazon Kinesis, cloudwatch log groups etc 
6. Queue processing




***AWS Lambda supports the following Languages**
we call these runtime 

+ Python 
+ Nodejs 
+ Java 
+ C# (.Netcore)
+ Golang 
+ Ruby 
+ C# / Powershell 

## What are some of the services that you can intergrate your Lambda functions with 

+ API gateways
+ Kinesis
+ Dynamodb 
+ S3 
+ Cloudfront 
+ Cloudwatch + Eventbridge
+ Cloudwatch Logs
+ SNS + SQS
+ Cognito 




| EC2 INSTANCE                                                          | WHY LAMBDA                             
|-----------------------------------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------
|EC2 instances  ( Virtual Machines or servers in the cloud)             | Virtual functions- no servers to manage                         
|Limited RAM and CPU ( Provisioned )                                    | Limited by time - short executions            
|EC2 instance will continously running                                  | It runs on demand - Event driven 
|Means Intervention to add or remove servers ( manually or auto scaling)| Scaling is automatic 




**Limitations of Lambda Functions**  

1. **Execution Limits**

 + The maximum timeout for a Lambda function is 15 minutes. For tasks requiring longer processing, you'll need alternative solutions like `Step Functions` or `EC2`.
 + Memory can range between 128 MB and 10,240 MB. CPU power is proportional to the memory allocated, so heavy computational tasks may require alternative setups
 + When it comes to disk usage Lambda provides 512 MB to 10GB of ephemeral storage
 + You can setup Enironmental Variables up to (4 KB)

2. **Deployment Limitations**

+  Lambda has a deployment size ( compressed zip): 50 MB of code uploaded directly using aws management console , terraform , cloudformation 
+ Size of uncompressed deployment ( code + dependencies ): 250 MB when uploading through an S3 Bucket . 
+ layer size . Each Lambda layer has a limit of 50 MB, and a function can use up to 5 layers.

3. **Service Limits**

+ Executions can take place at the same time up to 1000 lambdas  (but this can be increased ) per region.
+ Burst concurrency meaning it has a initial burst limit of up to 500 invocations per minute in most aws regions , note in some regions like North Virginia there high burst limits which 3,000 invocations per minute 

4. **Debugging**

+ It can be difficult to debug a lambda function due to limited access to the underlying architecture 






`Interview Question ?`

if you have a Lambda function that exceeds the deployment package size limits (50 MB for a zipped package and 250 MB for an unzipped package)
how will you still go ahead to deploy the lambda ?



+ Note if you have a zip package and its more than 50 MB you can still deploy the lambda using an s3 bucket

1. **We can use Lambda Layers**

What are layers ? :  Lambda layers allow you to include additional libraries, dependencies, or configuration files separately from your main function code.

How does this help ?

+ This helps in the sense that , you can remove large dependencies  ,files or libraries , which will reduce the size of the main deployment package ,allowing you to deploy the code in layers (parts)

+ Each layer has a 50 MB zipped size limit, and a function can use up to 5 layers, giving you an additional 250 MB for dependencies.


2. **We can Use Amazon S3 for large files** 

Instead of packaging large assets or binaries within the Lambda deployment package, store them in an S3 bucket and retrieve them during function execution.
It Keeps the deployment package lightweight by offloading static files, binaries, or configurations to S3.

3. **Use AWS Elastic File System (EFS)**

+ Amazon EFS is a scalable, elastic, and managed network file system that can be mounted to Lambda functions.Attach EFS to your Lambda function to provide virtually unlimited storage for large libraries, files, or datasets.

4. **Use Container Images** 

+ AWS Lambda supports deploying functions as container images instead of traditional zipped packages.The container image can be up to 10 GB, allowing significantly larger deployment packages.

5. **Split Functionality**

+ Break down the Lambda function (Put code into modules, split the code into small and manageable pieces and each pieace is a seperate lambda function and you can use a step function execute multiple lambda functions)





*Core concepts of AWS LAMBDA**

1. *FAAS : Function as a service*

`What is FAAS` : Function as a Service (FaaS) is a cloud computing model that allows developers to execute code in response to events without provisioning or managing servers. AWS Lambda is a leading example of FaaS.


AWS Lambda functions are built with specific components:

2. *Anatomy of a Lambda Function* 

a) . Handler Function (Entry Point).

`What is a Handler` ? : 

+ The handler is the entry point of the Lambda function where execution begins.
+ It is a specific function within your code that AWS Lambda automatically calls when triggered.

Lambda has different handlers depending on the language OR runtime 

e.g 

+ Python: lambda_function.lambda_handler(event, context)
+ Node.js: exports.handler = async (event) => { ... }
+ Java: public String handleRequest(Map<String, String> event, Context context)

b) . Event Object

`what is an event object`: The event parameter provides the input data for the function. The structure of the event depends on the service or trigger (e.g., S3, API Gateway, DynamoDB).

c) .  Context Object

`what is a context object` : 

The context parameter contains runtime information about the Lambda function.


3. **Supported Runtimes** 
Lambda supports different programming languages and versions of those languages which we call them runtime 

+ Python  (e.g python 3.8 ,3.9)
+ Nodejs  (e.g Node.js 14.x 16.x)
+ Java   (e.g java 8 , java 11)
+ C# (.Netcore)  
+ Golang  (e.g Go 1.x )
+ Ruby 
+ C# / Powershell 

Note : AWS lambda allows custom runtimes using the AWS runtime API, enabling languages like PHP, Ruby, or others.


invocation --triggered a lambda function 


**How can you improve the perfomance of your Lambda function up to 10x Java** 


When you invoke (request it to run by an event ) a lambda function using Java Init ----> Invoke--->shutdown
when enable `snapstart` there is no initialized step : its already pre-initiazed : ------> Invoke ----> shutdown 


**Lambda additional resources** 

*code-signing-configurations*: 

code signing ensures the integrity and authenticity of your Lambda function code by verifying that the code has not been tampered with and that it comes from a trusted source. Code signing is important in security-conscious environments where it’s critical to ensure that only authorized code is deployed.

**Event source mappings*

event source mappings are used to link an AWS service or resource to a Lambda function, enabling the Lambda function to be triggered by events from that source. Event source mappings automatically handle the invocation of your Lambda function when events are generated, such as when new records are added to an Amazon SQS queue or new objects are uploaded to an S3 bucket


*Layers*

Lambda Layers are a distribution mechanism for libraries, custom runtime components, and other dependencies that you can include in your Lambda function. They allow you to package and share common code, libraries, and dependencies across multiple Lambda functions. Using Lambda layers can simplify the management of shared resources, `reduce function deployment size`, and improve code reusability.

`note` : When you create a lambda layer you must ensure the runtime is the same as your current lambda function runtime to avoid any compactability issues.




HANDS ON 

+ You can create a lambda function using AWS console , Commandline , Infrastructure as code ( terraform and Cloudformation )
+ You can upload your run time or code using Command line or aws console 



`Code to Start Instance` 
Pythin run time 


import boto3
import os

def lambda_handler(event, context):
    """
    AWS Lambda function to start all EC2 instances in a specified VPC.
    
    Args:
        event: Dictionary containing the VPC ID.
        context: Runtime information for the Lambda function.
    """
    # Initialize EC2 client
    ec2_client = boto3.client('ec2')
    
    # Get the VPC ID from the event or environment variable
    vpc_id = event.get("VpcId") or os.getenv("VPC_ID")
    
    if not vpc_id:
        return {
            "statusCode": 400,
            "body": "No VPC ID provided. Pass the VPC ID via the event or environment variables."
        }
    
    try:
        # Describe instances in the specified VPC
        response = ec2_client.describe_instances(
            Filters=[
                {"Name": "vpc-id", "Values": [vpc_id]},
                {"Name": "instance-state-name", "Values": ["stopped"]}
            ]
        )
        
        # Collect instance IDs
        instance_ids = [
            instance["InstanceId"]
            for reservation in response.get("Reservations", [])
            for instance in reservation.get("Instances", [])
        ]
        
        if not instance_ids:
            return {
                "statusCode": 200,
                "body": f"No stopped instances found in VPC {vpc_id}."
            }
        
        # Start the instances
        start_response = ec2_client.start_instances(InstanceIds=instance_ids)
        
        # Collect the current state of each instance
        starting_instances = start_response.get('StartingInstances', [])
        result = [
            {
                "InstanceId": instance['InstanceId'],
                "CurrentState": instance['CurrentState']['Name'],
                "PreviousState": instance['PreviousState']['Name']
            }
            for instance in starting_instances
        ]
        
        return {
            "statusCode": 200,
            "body": {
                "message": f"Successfully started instances in VPC {vpc_id}.",
                "details": result
            }
        }
    except Exception as e:
        # Handle any exceptions that occur
        return {
            "statusCode": 500,
            "body": {
                "message": f"An error occurred while starting instances in VPC {vpc_id}.",
                "error": str(e)
            }
        }


Since 7:00 AM EST = 12:00 PM (noon) UTC, use:

cron(0 12 ? * MON-FRI *)

Breakdown:

Minutes → 0
Hours → 12
Day of month → ?
Month → *

`send slack notification for cloudwatch monitoring`

import json
from urllib.parse import urlencode
from urllib.request import Request, urlopen
from datetime import datetime
import boto3

session = boto3.session.Session()


class CloudWatchAlarmParser:
    def __init__(self, msg):
        self.msg = msg
        self.timestamp_format = "%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S.%f%z"
        self.trigger = msg["Trigger"]

        if self.msg['NewStateValue'] == "ALARM":
            self.color = "danger"
        elif self.msg['NewStateValue'] == "OK":
            self.color = "good"

    def __url(self):
        return ("https://console.aws.amazon.com/cloudwatch/home?"
                + urlencode({'region': session.region_name})
                + "#alarmsV2:alarm/"
                + self.msg["AlarmName"]
                )

    def slack_data(self):
        _message = {
            'text': '<!here|here>',  # add @here to message
            'attachments': [
                {
                    'title': ":aws: AWS CloudWatch Notification :alarm:",
                    'ts': datetime.strptime(
                            self.msg['StateChangeTime'],
                            self.timestamp_format
                          ).timestamp(),
                    'color': self.color,
                    'fields': [
                        {
                            "title": "Alarm Name",
                            "value": self.msg["AlarmName"],
                            "short": True
                        },
                        {
                            "title": "Alarm Description",
                            "value": self.msg["AlarmDescription"],
                            "short": False
                        },
                        {
                            "title": "Trigger",
                            "value": " ".join([
                                self.trigger["Statistic"],
                                self.trigger["MetricName"],
                                self.trigger["ComparisonOperator"],
                                str(self.trigger["Threshold"]),
                                "for",
                                str(self.trigger["EvaluationPeriods"]),
                                "period(s) of",
                                str(self.trigger["Period"]),
                                "seconds."
                            ]),
                            "short": False
                        },
                        {
                            'title': 'Old State',
                            'value': self.msg["OldStateValue"],
                            "short": True
                        },
                        {
                            'title': 'Current State',
                            'value': self.msg["NewStateValue"],
                            'short': True
                        },
                        {
                            'title': 'Link to Alarm',
                            'value': self.__url(),
                            'short': False
                        }
                    ]
                }
            ]
        }
        return _message



def lambda_handler(event, context):
    sns_message = json.loads(event['Records'][0]['Sns']['Message'])
    print(sns_message)

    webhook_url = 'https://hooks.slack.com/services/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' # Set destination URL (slack webhook)

    slack_data = CloudWatchAlarmParser(sns_message).slack_data()
    slack_data["channel"] = 'devops-cloud-master-class9' # change to your own channel 


    request = Request(
        webhook_url, 
        data=json.dumps(slack_data).encode(),
        headers={'Content-Type': 'application/json'}
        )
    response = urlopen(request)
    return {
        'statusCode': response.getcode(),
        'body': response.read().decode()
    }


if __name__ == "__main__":
    print(lambda_handler(None, None))


by default lambda comes 3 seconds execution time 




**Lambda Configurations** 

+ General Configuration 
+ Triggers 
+ Permissions
+ Destinations 

+ `Function URLs`

A function URL is a dedicated HTTP(S) endpoint for your function. When your function URL is configured, you can use it to invoke your function through a browser, curl, Postman, or any HTTP client. When you configure a function URL from the main function page, Lambda assigns the function URL to the $LATEST unpublished version of your function. You cannot assign a function URL to any other function version, but you can assign a function URL to any function alias.

To secure your function URL, you can choose between IAM authorization (AWS_IAM) or no authorization (NONE). If you decide not to use IAM authorization, you should implement your own authorization logic in your function. Function URLs can support JWT/OIDC/OAuth2 authentication methods.

To control access to your function URL from other origins, you can also configure Cross-Origin Resource Sharing (CORS).

`Alaias in Lambda function `: 

In AWS Lambda, Aliases are named pointers to a specific Lambda function version.
They allow you to reference a function using a friendly name such as dev, qa, staging, or prod instead of referencing a version number directly.


Why Use Aliases?

1. `Environment Separation`

Instead of creating multiple Lambda functions:

process-orders-dev
process-orders-qa
process-orders-prod

You can use:

process-orders:dev
process-orders:qa
process-orders:prod

2. `Safe Deployments`

You can deploy a new version and simply move the alias:

Before:

prod -> Version 10

After testing:

prod -> Version 11

No application changes required








**Edge Function** 


An edge function in the context of aws refers to lambda function that runs at the AWS edge locations . These are part of AWS Lambda@Edge, a feature of AWS Lambda that allows you to run your code closer to the users (at edge locations), reducing latency and improving performance for globally distributed applications.

+ This is a code which you write and attached to your cloudfront distribution 
+ This code is executed closer to your users at minimal latency 
+ Cloudfront provides two options : `Cloudfront Function and Lambda@Edge`
+ You dont need to manage servers if you want to deploy globally .
+ Fully serverless 
+ You only pay for what you use


Use case for `Cloudfront function and Lambda@Edge` which are the two types of edge function 

+ Dynamic web application at edge 
+ Website security and privacy (block request for users or country)
+ URL rewriting and redirections ( redirect users based on url pattern e,g redirect http to https )
+ Header Manipulation ( add ,remove or modify HTTP headers in a request and response e.g like adding security headers content security policy)
+ A/B testing : route traffic to different versions of your application for testing purposes e.g 10% of traffic to a new feature 
+ security and authentication 
+ User tracking and analytics 


**Cloudfront Functions**

+ Lightweight functions written in Java
+ High scalability ,latency sensitive CDN customizations
+ Cloudfront functions can Used to change viewer request and responses 
(viewer request : after cloudfront receives a request from a viewer)
viewer response: before cloudfront forward the response to the viewer
+ You can manage the code within your cloudfront 

**Lambda@Edge** 

+ Written Node js or Python 
+ It can scale 1000s of request per second 
+ it can be Used to change viewer request and responses 
(viewer request : after cloudfront receives a request from a viewer)
(origin request : before cloudfront forwards the request to the origin)
(origin response: After cloudfront receives the request from the origin)
(viewer response: before cloudfront forward the response to the viewer)

**DIFFERENCE BETWEEN LAMBDA@EDGE  AND CLOUDFRONT FUNCTION**

| Feature                      | CloudFront Function                | Lambda@Edge                            |
|------------------------------|-------------------------------------|----------------------------------------|
| Runtime Support              | JavaScript                          | Node.js and Python                     |
| Number of Requests           | Millions of requests per second     | Thousands of requests per second       |
| CloudFront Triggers          | Viewer's request/response           | Viewer's request/response, origin request/origin response |
| Max Execution Time           | < 1 ms                              | 5 - 10 minutes                         |
| Max Memory                   | 2 MB                                | 128 MB up to 10 GB                     |
| Total Package Size           | 10 KB                               | 1 MB - 50 MB                           |
| Network & File System Access | No                                  | Yes                                    |
| Access to Request Body       | No                                  | Yes                                    |
| Pricing                      | Free Tier available/1/6 of edge price | No free tier, charged per request & duration |


Lambda@Edge vs Regular Lambda 

+ Regular lambdas runs in an aws data center (regions) to process events 
+ lambda@edge runs in the aws Edge locations, which are geographically distributed points of presence (PoPs) around world 

**Note*: By default Lambda functions are deployed outside your VPC unless you setup the lambda to be deployed in your VPC.

What is a Database ? 

A database its simply a storage that helps to organize, collect ,store information electronically. 
Databases are designed to store, retrieve, manage, and manipulate large amounts of information efficiently. They are used in a wide range of applications, from websites and mobile apps to enterprise systems and cloud-based services.

`What is a Dynamodb` 

Amazon DynamoDB is a fully managed `NoSQL database service` provided by AWS. It is designed to deliver fast and predictable performance with seamless scalability. DynamoDB is ideal for applications that require high availability, low latency, and scalability, such as web and mobile applications, IoT, and gaming applications.


`What is a NoSQL database service ?`

NoSQL (short for Not Only SQL) refers to a category of databases that are designed to store and manage `unstructured`, `semi-structured`, or `structured data without using traditional relational database models`. Unlike relational databases, which use tables with rows and columns to store data, NoSQL databases can use various models such as key-value stores, document stores, column-family stores, and graph databases


*SQL (Structured Query Language)*

This is a programming language that is used to manage and manipulate relational databases 
This language allows users to be able to carryout task in the database like querying data (requesting), updating, insert and deleting data(information)
you can use this programming language with databases like ( PostgreSQL , MySQL , Oracle , SQL server etc )  . 

*What is relational database* : The structure of a relational databases uses tables(relations) to store data .each table has rows (records) and columns (attributes )

*non-relational database* : The structure of a non relational database uses different models such key - value pairs , documents , graphs , wide column stores .
